, taken from the above formula. As in Construction
2.2.21
, the operad structure of
\textbf{B}^{\mathcal{P}}
is canonically defined via the structure of
\mathcal{P}
, so that
\textbf{B}^{\mathcal{P}}
is in fact a suboperad of
\textbf{B}^{\mathcal{P}/}
. (See also [
[
11
]
, §2.1.1]).

As in the proof of Proposition
2.2.22
, one can verify the following.

Proposition 2.2.24
.

There is a canonical isomorphism

\BMod(\mathcal{P})\cong\Alg_{\textbf{B}^{\mathcal{P}}}(\mathcal{S})

between the category of
\mathcal{P}
-bimodules and the category of algebras over
\textbf{B}^{\mathcal{P}}
.

Finally, we construct an operad encoding the category of left
\mathcal{P}
-modules.

Construction 2.2.25
.

We denote by
\textbf{L}^{\mathcal{P}}
the
\mathcal{S}
-enriched operad whose set of colors is
\Seq(C)
and whose spaces of operations are given as follows. For simplicity of equations, we just describe the spaces of 2-ary operations. These are concentrated in objects of the form:

\textbf{L}^{\mathcal{P}}\left(\,\left(c_{1},\cdots,c_{r_{1}};c\right),\left(c_{r_{1}+1},\cdots,c_{r_{1}+r_{2}};c^{\prime}\right);\left(c_{\sigma(1)},\cdots,c_{\sigma(r_{1}+r_{2})};d\right)\,\right):=\bigsqcup_{\alpha}\mathcal{P}(c,c^{\prime};d)

where
\sigma\in\Sigma_{r_{1}+r_{2}}
. The coproduct ranges over the subset of
\Sigma_{r_{1}+r_{2}}
consisting of those
\alpha
satisfying that for every
i\in\{1,\cdots,r_{1}+r_{2}\}
, the two colors
c_{\sigma(i)}
and
c_{\alpha(i)}
coincide. From the above formula, we denote by
\textbf{L}^{\mathcal{P}}_{\alpha}(-)
the component of
\textbf{L}^{\mathcal{P}}(-)
corresponding to
\alpha
. Observe that there is a canonical map

\mathcal{P}(c,c^{\prime};d)=\textbf{L}^{\mathcal{P}}_{\alpha}\left(\,\left(c_{1},\cdots,c_{r_{1}};c\right),\left(c_{r_{1}+1},\cdots,c_{r_{1}+r_{2}};c^{\prime}\right);\left(c_{\sigma(1)},\cdots,c_{\sigma(r_{1}+r_{2})};d\right)\,\right)\longrightarrow

\textbf{B}^{\mathcal{P}}_{\alpha}\left(\,\left(c_{1},\cdots,c_{r_{1}};c\right),\left(c_{r_{1}+1},\cdots,c_{r_{1}+r_{2}};c^{\prime}\right);\left(c_{\sigma(1)},\cdots,c_{\sigma(r_{1}+r_{2})};d\right)\,\right)=\mathcal{P}(c_{1};c_{1})\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathcal{P}(c_{r_{1}+r_{2}};c_{r_{1}+r_{2}})\otimes\mathcal{P}(c,c^{\prime};d)

given by inserting the unit operation
\id_{c_{i}}
into the factor
\mathcal{P}(c_{i};c_{i})
for
i=1,\cdots,r_{1}+r_{2}
. In this way, the operad structure of
\textbf{L}^{\mathcal{P}}
is defined via the structure of
\mathcal{P}
, so that
\textbf{L}^{\mathcal{P}}
forms a suboperad of
\textbf{B}^{\mathcal{P}}
.

Proposition 2.2.26
.

There is a canonical isomorphism

\LMod(\mathcal{P})\cong\Alg_{\textbf{L}^{\mathcal{P}}}(\mathcal{S})

between the category of left
\mathcal{P}
-modules and the category of algebras over
\textbf{L}^{\mathcal{P}}
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to the ones above.
∎

2.3
Operadic transferred model structures

In this subsection, we assume further that
\mathcal{S}
is a
symmetric monoidal model category
(cf. Hovey’s
[
29
]
). Let
\mathcal{P}
be a
C
-colored operad in
\mathcal{S}
and let
A
be a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra. We list here all the mentioned operadic categories, except the category of
\mathcal{S}
-enriched operads
\Op(\mathcal{S})
, including

\{\Coll_{C}(\mathcal{S}),\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S}),\LMod(\mathcal{P}),\RMod(\mathcal{P}),\BMod(\mathcal{P}),\IbMod(\mathcal{P}),\Alg_{\mathcal{P}}(\mathcal{S}),\Mod_{\mathcal{P}}^{A}\}=:\mathbb{A}.

(2.3.1)

Definition 2.3.1
.

Let
\mathcal{M}
be any of the categories in
\mathbb{A}
. The
transferred model structure
on
\mathcal{M}
is the one whose weak equivalences (resp. fibrations) are precisely the levelwise weak equivalences (resp. fibrations).

We wish to set up several suitable conditions on the base category
\mathcal{S}
assuring the existence of the transferred model structure on every element of
\mathbb{A}
. As we have seen previously, each category
\mathcal{M}\in\mathbb{A}
can be represented as the category of algebras over a certain operad (or category) (cf. Remarks
2.1.2
,
2.2.14
, Example
2.2.5
, Propositions
2.2.18
,
2.2.20
,
2.2.24
,
2.2.26
). Consequently, one just needs to consider the transferred model structure on the category
\Alg_{\mathcal{P}}(\mathcal{S})
. According to the literature, we know several criteria assuring the existence of that. Here are several settings.

Definition 2.3.2
.

A
symmetric monoidal fibrant replacement functor
on
\mathcal{S}
is a symmetric monoidal functor
\mathbb{R}:\mathcal{S}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}
together with a monoidal natural transformation
\varphi:\Id\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
such that for each object
X\in\mathcal{S}
, the map
\varphi_{X}:X\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}(X)
exhibits
\mathbb{R}(X)
as a fibrant replacement of
X
.

Definition 2.3.3
.

A
functorial path data
on
\mathcal{S}
is a symmetric monoidal functor
\mathbb{P}:\mathcal{S}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}
together with monoidal natural transformations
s:\Id\rightarrow\mathbb{P}
and
d_{0},d_{1}:\mathbb{P}\rightarrow\Id
such that the composed map

X\overset{s_{X}}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{P}(X)\overset{(d_{0},d_{1})}{\longrightarrow}X\times X

exhibits
\mathbb{P}(X)
as a path object for
X
.

The following statement is due to [
[
30
]
, Theorem 3.11].

Proposition 2.3.4
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{S}
is strongly cofibrantly generated (i.e., cofibrantly generated with domains of the generating cofibrations and trivial cofibrations being small). If
\mathcal{S}
admits both a symmetric monoidal fibrant replacement functor
\mathbb{R}
and a functorial path data
\mathbb{P}
then the transferred model structure on
\Alg_{\mathcal{P}}(\mathcal{S})
exists for every operad
\mathcal{P}
.

There is another powerful criterion, thanks to the recent work of Pavlov-Scholbach. Here are several settings. Recall from [
[
3
]
, Definition 1.1] that a map
f:X\rightarrow Y
in
\mathcal{S}
is an
h
-
cofibration
if and only if for every diagram of coCartesian squares in
\mathcal{S}
of the form

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g^{\prime}}
\textstyle{B^{\prime}}

the map
g^{\prime}
is a weak equivalence whenever
g
is one. If
f
is in addition a weak equivalence then it is an
acyclic

h
-
cofibration
.

Let
\overrightarrow{n}=(n_{1},\cdots,n_{k})
be a finite sequence of natural numbers. For a family
s=(s_{1},\cdots,s_{k})
of maps in
\mathcal{S}
, denote by

s^{\square\overrightarrow{n}}:=\square_{i}\,s_{i}^{\square\,n_{i}},

where the subcript
``\square
” refers to the
pushout-product
of maps. The group
\Sigma_{\overrightarrow{n}}:=\bigsqcap_{i}\Sigma_{n_{i}}
acts on
s^{\square\overrightarrow{n}}
in an evident way.

Definition 2.3.5
.

The symmetric monoidal model category
\mathcal{S}
is said to be
symmetric

h
-
monoidal
if for any finite family
s=(s_{1},\cdots,s_{k})
of cofibrations (resp. acyclic cofibrations) and for any object
X\in\mathcal{S}
equipped with a right
\Sigma_{\overrightarrow{n}}
-action, the map
X\otimes_{\Sigma_{\overrightarrow{n}}}s^{\square\overrightarrow{n}}
is an
h
-cofibration (resp. acyclic
h
-cofibration).

Proposition 2.3.6
.

(Pavlov-Scholbach, [
[
41
]
, Theorem 5.10]) Suppose that
\mathcal{S}
is a combinatorial symmetric monoidal model category such that weak equivalences are closed under transfinite compositions. If
\mathcal{S}
is symmetric
h
-monoidal (the acyclic part is sufficient), then the transferred model structure on
\Alg_{\mathcal{P}}(\mathcal{S})
exists for every operad
\mathcal{P}
.

We end this subsection by listing some base categories of interest and discussing how they adapt to the criteria mentioned above.

Examples 2.3.7
.

(i) The base category of most interest is the Cartesian monoidal category of
simplicial sets
,
(\Set_{\Delta},\times)
, equipped with the standard (Kan-Quillen) model structure. The model structure on
\Set_{\Delta}
is combinatorial, with weak equivalences closed under filtered colimits. It admits a fibrant replacement functor given by
\Ex^{\infty}:=\Sing|-|
the composition of the realization and singular functors, and admits a functorial path data given by
(-)^{\Delta^{1}}
. Moreover,
\Set_{\Delta}
is as well symmetric
h
-monoidal, according to [
[
42
]
, §7.1]. We hence get that
\Set_{\Delta}
satisfies the conditions of both the propositions
2.3.4
and
2.3.6
.

(ii) The second one is the monoidal category of
simplicial

R
-
modules
,
(\sMod_{R},\otimes)
, with
R
being a commutative ring, equipped with the standard model structure transferred from that of
\Set_{\Delta}
. As well as simplicial sets, simplicial
R
-modules satisfy the conditions of both the propositions
2.3.4
and
2.3.6
. Indeed, note first that
\sMod_{R}
is combinatorial, has weak equivalences being closed under filtered colimits and moreover, it is a fibrant model category (i.e., all the objects are fibrant). A functorial path data for
\sMod_{R}
is given by
(-)^{R\{\Delta^{1}\}}
. It is as well symmetric
h
-monoidal, according to [
[
42
]
, §7.3].

(iii) Let
k
be a commutative ring that contains
\mathbb{Q}
the field of rational numbers. Consider the monoidal category of
dg

k
-
modules
,
(\mathcal{C}(\textbf{k}),\otimes)
, equipped with the projective model structure. This is also a combinatorial fibrant model category, with weak equivalences closed under filtered colimits. There is a functorial path data for
\mathcal{C}(\textbf{k})
given by
(-)\otimes\Omega^{*}(\Delta^{1})
where
\Omega^{*}(\Delta^{1})
is the
Sullivan’s dg algebra of differentials
on the interval
\Delta^{1}\in\Set_{\Delta}
(see [
[
19
]
, 5.3]). So we get that
\mathcal{C}(\textbf{k})
satisfies the conditions of Proposition
2.3.4
. Moreover,
\mathcal{C}(\textbf{k})
is symmetric
h
-monoidal, according to
[
42
]
.

(iv) For
k
as above, consider the monoidal category of
connective dg
k
-modules
,
(\mathcal{C}_{\geqslant 0}(\textbf{k}),\otimes)
, equipped with the projective model structure. This model category is combinatorial and its weak equivalences are closed under filtered colimits. Moreover, all its objects are fibrant. However, as far as we know,
\mathcal{C}_{\geqslant 0}(\textbf{k})
does not adapt to the conditions of Proposition
2.3.4
. The only thing missed is the existence of a functorial path data. Despite this,
\mathcal{C}_{\geqslant 0}(\textbf{k})
is symmetric
h
-monoidal, as well as the case above.

(v) More generally, let
R
be a commutative monoid in
(\mathcal{C}_{\geqslant 0}(\textbf{k}),\otimes)
with
k
being a commutative ring containing
\mathbb{Q}
. Consider the monoidal category of
R
-modules,

(\Mod_{R},-\otimes_{R}-)
, equipped with the projective model structure. This is a combinatorial fibrant model category, with weak equivalences closed under filtered colimits. As well as
\mathcal{C}_{\geqslant 0}(\textbf{k})
, the category
\Mod_{R}
does not admit a functorial path data, yet it satisfies the conditions of Proposition
2.3.6
instead. To see the latter, one just needs to verify the symmetric
h
-monoidality. This is in fact transferred from the symmetric
h
-monoidality of
\mathcal{C}_{\geqslant 0}(\textbf{k})
(cf. [
[
42
]
, Theorem 5.9]).

In conclusion, we see that all the base categories listed above are nice enough so that the transferred model structure on
\mathcal{P}
-algebras exists for every operad
\mathcal{P}
. Thus for every category
\mathcal{M}\in\mathbb{A}
(
2.3.1
), the transferred model structure on
\mathcal{M}
exists as well.

2.4
Dwyer-Kan and canonical model structures on enriched operads

Let
\mathcal{S}
be a
monoidal model category
and let
\Cat(\mathcal{S})
denote the category of (small)
\mathcal{S}
-enriched categories. For each
\mathcal{C}\in\Cat(\mathcal{S})
, the
homotopy category of

\mathcal{C}
, denoted by
\Ho(\mathcal{C})
, is the ordinary category whose objects are the same as those of
\mathcal{C}
and whose hom-sets are given by

\textrm{Hom}_{\Ho(\mathcal{C})}(x,y):=\textrm{Hom}_{\Ho(\mathcal{S})}(1_{\mathcal{S}},\Map_{\mathcal{C}}(x,y)).

By convention, a map
f:\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
in
\Cat(\mathcal{S})
is a
levelwise weak equivalence
(resp.
fibration, trivial fibration
, etc) if for every
x,y\in\Ob(\mathcal{C})
the map
\Map_{\mathcal{C}}(x,y)\longrightarrow\Map_{\mathcal{D}}(f(x),f(y))
is a weak equivalence (resp. fibration, trivial fibration, etc) in
\mathcal{S}
.

Definition 2.4.1
.

A map
f:\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
in
\Cat(\mathcal{S})
is called a
Dwyer-Kan equivalence
if it is a levelwise weak equivalence and such that the induced functor
\Ho(f):\Ho(\mathcal{C})\longrightarrow\Ho(\mathcal{D})
between homotopy categories is essentially surjective.

Definition 2.4.2
.

The
Dwyer-Kan model structure on

\Cat(\mathcal{S})
is the one whose weak equivalences are the Dwyer-Kan equivalences and whose trivial fibrations are the levelwise trivial fibrations surjective on objects. (See, e.g.,
[
40
]
,
[
35
]
).

Definition 2.4.3
.

(Berger-Moerdijk
[
7
]
) The
canonical model structure on

\Cat(\mathcal{S})
is the one whose fibrant objects are the levelwise fibrant categories and whose trivial fibrations are the same as those of the Dwyer-Kan model structure.

By extending the two above, G. Caviglia
[
8
]
established both the Dwyer-Kan and canonical model structures on
\Op(\mathcal{S})
. Suppose further that
\mathcal{S}
is a symmetric monoidal model category. By convention, a map
f:\mathcal{P}\rightarrow\mathcal{Q}
in
\Op(\mathcal{S})
is called a
levelwise weak equivalence
(resp.
fibration, trivial fibration
, etc) if for every sequence
(c_{1},\cdots,c_{n};c)
of colors of
\mathcal{P}
, the induced map

\mathcal{P}(c_{1},\cdots,c_{n};c)\longrightarrow\mathcal{Q}(f(c_{1}),\cdots,f(c_{n});f(c))

is a weak equivalence (resp. fibration, trivial fibration, etc) in
\mathcal{S}
.

The
homotopy category of

\mathcal{P}
is defined to be
\Ho(\mathcal{P}):=\Ho(\mathcal{P}_{1})
the homotopy category of its underlying category.

Definition 2.4.4
.

A map
f:\mathcal{P}\rightarrow\mathcal{Q}
in
\Op(\mathcal{S})
is called a
Dwyer-Kan equivalence
if it is a levelwise weak equivalence and such that the induced functor
\Ho(f):\Ho(\mathcal{P})\longrightarrow\Ho(\mathcal{Q})
between homotopy categories is essentially surjective or alternatively, if
f
is a levelwise weak equivalence and the underlying map
f_{1}:\mathcal{P}_{1}\longrightarrow\mathcal{Q}_{1}
is a Dwyer-Kan equivalence in
\Cat(\mathcal{S})
.

Definition 2.4.5
.

The
Dwyer-Kan model structure on

\Op(\mathcal{S})
is the one whose weak equivalences are the Dwyer-Kan equivalences and whose trivial fibrations are the levelwise trivial fibrations surjective on colors.

Definition 2.4.6
.

The
canonical model structure on

\Op(\mathcal{S})
is the one whose fibrant objects are the levelwise fibrant operads and whose trivial fibrations are the same as those of the Dwyer-Kan model structure.

Remark 2.4.7
.

As originally introduced by G. Caviglia, a map in
\Op(\mathcal{S})
is a fibration (resp. weak equivalence) with respect to the canonical model structure if and only if it is a levelwise fibration (resp. weak equivalence) and such that its underlying map in
\Cat(\mathcal{S})
is a fibration (resp. weak equivalence) with respect to that model structure. (See [
[
8
]
, Definition 4.5] and [
[
7
]
, §2.2]).

Following up his work, we give a set of conditions on the base category
\mathcal{S}
assuring the existence of the canonical model structure.

Proposition 2.4.8
.

(Caviglia,
[
8
]
) Let
\mathcal{S}
be a combinatorial symmetric monoidal model category such that:

(S1) the class of weak equivalences is closed under filtered colimits,

(S2) either (a)
\mathcal{S}
admits a symmetric monoidal fibrant replacement functor and a functorial path data, or (b)
\mathcal{S}
is symmetric
h
-monoidal,

(S3) the monoidal unit is cofibrant, and

(S4) the model structure is right proper.

Then
\Op(\mathcal{S})
admits the canonical model structure, which is as well right proper and combinatorial. Moreover, this model structure coincides then with the Dwyer-Kan model structure.

Proof.

The combinatoriality of
\mathcal{S}
implies that it is strongly cofibrantly generated and besides that, implies the existence of a set of
generating intervals
in the sense of
[
7
]
(cf. Lemma 1.12 of loc. cit.). On other hand, by propositions
2.3.4
and
2.3.6
, the condition (S2) ensures the existence of the transferred model structure on
\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})
for every set
C
. Then by [
[
8
]
, Theorem 4.22 (1)],
\Op(\mathcal{S})
admits the canonical model structure, which is combinatorial as well. The right properness follows by Proposition 5.3 of loc. cit.

For the second claim, observe first that the condition (S1), together with the combinatoriality of
\mathcal{S}
, implies that
\mathcal{S}
is
compactly generated
in the sense of [
[
7
]
, Definition 1.2]. Combining this fact with (S2), we get that
\mathcal{S}
is
adequate
in the sense of [
[
7
]
, Definition 1.1]. The latter fact, along with the conditions (S3) and (S4), proves that the classes of Dwyer-Kan and canonical weak equivalences in
\Cat(\mathcal{S})
coincide (cf.
[
7
]
, propositions 2.20 and 2.24). Thus, by Remark
2.4.7
these two classes in
\Op(\mathcal{S})
coincide as well. Combining the latter with the fact that the two model structures have the same trivial fibrations, we deduce that they indeed coincide.
∎

Remark 2.4.9
.

Under the same assumptions as in Proposition
2.4.8
, the canonical model structure on
\Cat(\mathcal{S})
automatically exists and coincides with the Dwyer-Kan model structure.

Example 2.4.10
.

Some typical base categories satisfying the conditions of Proposition
2.4.8
are presented in Examples
2.3.7
.

2.5
Tangent categories and Quillen cohomology

This subsection, based on the works of
[
24
,
22
]
, contains the most important concepts appearing throughout the paper. Basically,
tangent category
comes after a procedure of taking the stabilization of a model category of interest. Note that under our setting, stabilizations exist only as semi model categories. Despite this, the needed results from those papers remain valid.

Definition 2.5.1
.

A model category
M
is said to be
weakly pointed
if it contains a
weak zero object
, i.e., an object which is both homotopy initial and terminal.

Let
M
be a weakly pointed model category and let
X
be an (
\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}
)-diagram in
M
. The
diagonal squares
of
X
are of the form

\textstyle{X_{n,n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X_{n,n+1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X_{n+1,n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X_{n+1,n+1}}

Definition 2.5.2
.

An (
\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}
)-diagram in
M
is called

(1) a
prespectrum
if all its off-diagonal entries are weak zero objects in
M
,

(2) an
\Omega
-spectrum
if it is a prespectrum and all its diagonal squares are homotopy Cartesian,

(3) a
suspension spectrum
if it is a prespectrum and all its diagonal squares are homotopy coCartesian.

The projective model category of (
\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}
)-diagrams in
M
will be denoted by
\textbf{M}^{\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}}_{\proj}
.

Definition 2.5.3
.

(
[
24
]
) Let
M
be a weakly pointed model category. A map
f:X\rightarrow Y
in
\textbf{M}^{\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}}
is said to be a
stable equivalence
if for every
\Omega
-spectrum
Z
the induced map between derived mapping spaces

\Map^{\der}_{\textbf{M}^{\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}}_{\proj}}(Y,Z)\longrightarrow\Map^{\der}_{\textbf{M}^{\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}}_{\proj}}(X,Z)

is a weak equivalence. (Note that a stable equivalence between
\Omega
-spectra is always a levelwise weak equivalence).

Following [
[
24
]
, Lemma 2.1.6], the
\Omega
-spectra in
M
can be characterized as the
local objects
against a certain set of maps. Inspired by Definition 2.1.3 of loc. cit., we give the following definition, which is valid due to
[
4
]
.

Definition 2.5.4
.

Let
M
be a weakly pointed combinatorial model category such that the domains of generating cofibrations are cofibrant. The
stabilization of

M
, denoted by
\Sp(\textbf{M})
, is defined to be the left Bousfield localization of
\textbf{M}^{\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}}_{\proj}
with
\Omega
-spectra as the local objects. Explicitly,
\Sp(\textbf{M})
is a cofibrantly generated semi model category whose

- weak equivalences are the stable equivalences, and whose

- (generating) cofibrations are the same as those of
\textbf{M}^{\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}}_{\proj}
.

In particular, fibrant objects of
\Sp(\textbf{M})
are precisely the levelwise fibrant
\Omega
-spectra.

Remark 2.5.5
.

When
M
is in addition left proper then the stabilization
\Sp(\textbf{M})
exists as a (full) model category. Nevertheless, we do not require the left properness throughout the paper.

Definition 2.5.6
.

(
[
24
]
) A model (or semi-model) category
M
is said to be
stable
if the following equivalent conditions hold:

(1) The
underlying

\infty
-
category

\textbf{M}_{\infty}
(cf.,
[
15
,
25
]
) is stable in the sense of
[
37
]
.

(2)
M
is weakly pointed and such that a square in
M
is homotopy coCartesian if and only if it is homotopy Cartesian.

(3)
M
is weakly pointed and such that the adjunction
\Sigma:\Ho(\textbf{M})\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\Ho(\textbf{M}):\Omega
of suspension-desuspension functors is an adjoint equivalence.

Theorem 2.5.7
.

(Y. Harpaz, J. Nuiten and M. Prasma
[
24
]
) Let
M
and
N
be two weakly pointed combinatorial model categories such that the domains of their generating cofibrations are cofibrant.

1.

There is a Quillen adjunction
\Sigma^{\infty}\colon\textbf{M}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr}}}\Sp(\textbf{M})\penalty\mspace{6muplus1mu}{:}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu\mathpunct{}\mspace{2mu}\Omega^{\infty}
where
\Omega^{\infty}(X)=X_{0,0}
and
\Sigma^{\infty}({A})
is the constant diagram with value
A
.

2.

The induced functor
(\Omega^{\infty})_{\infty}:\Sp(\textbf{M})_{\infty}\longrightarrow\textbf{M}_{\infty}
exhibits
\Sp(\textbf{M})_{\infty}
as the stabilization of
\textbf{M}_{\infty}
in the sense of
[
37
]
.

3.

The stabilization
\Sp(\textbf{M})
is stable. Furthermore, if
M
is already stable then the adjunction
\Sigma^{\infty}\colon\textbf{M}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr}}}\Sp(\textbf{M})\penalty\mspace{6muplus1mu}{:}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu\mathpunct{}\mspace{2mu}\Omega^{\infty}
is a Quillen equivalence.

4.

A Quillen adjunction
\sF\colon\textbf{M}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr}}}\textbf{N}\penalty\mspace{6muplus1mu}{:}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu\mathpunct{}\mspace{2mu}\sG
lifts to a Quillen adjunction

\Sp(\sF)\colon\Sp(\textbf{M})\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr}}}\Sp(\textbf{N})\penalty\mspace{6muplus1mu}{:}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu\mathpunct{}\mspace{2mu}\Sp(\sG)

which is given on underlying categories by the adjunction
\sF^{\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}}\dashv\sG^{\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}}
. Moreover, if
\sF\dashv\sG
is a Quillen equivalence then
\Sp(\sF)\dashv\Sp(\sG)
is one. (We will sometimes write
\sF^{\Sp}
instead
\Sp(\sF)
).

Let
\mathcal{I}
be a diagram category. Then the left Quillen functor
\sF:\textbf{M}\longrightarrow\textbf{N}
gives rise to a left Quillen functor

[\mathcal{I},\Sp(\sF)]:\Fun(\mathcal{I},\Sp(\textbf{M}))\longrightarrow\Fun(\mathcal{I},\Sp(\textbf{N}))

between projective model categories given by post-composing with
\Sp(\sF):\Sp(\textbf{M})\longrightarrow\Sp(\textbf{N})
.

Observation 2.5.8
.

Suppose that
\sF
preserves weak equivalences. Then
\Sp(\sF)
also preserves weak equivalences and therefore, so does the functor
[\mathcal{I},\Sp(\sF)]
.

Proof.

Since
\sF
preserves weak equivalences, so does the functor
\sF^{\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}}:\textbf{M}^{\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}}_{\proj}\longrightarrow\textbf{N}^{\mathbb{N}\times\mathbb{N}}_{\proj}
. The proof is then straightforward using the definition of stable equivalences.
∎

Notations 2.5.9
.

(i) Let
\mathcal{C}
be a category containing a terminal object
*
. The
pointed category
associated to
\mathcal{C}
is given by
\mathcal{C}_{*}:=\mathcal{C}_{*/}
the category of objects under
*
.

(ii) Suppose that
\mathcal{C}
contains an initial object
\emptyset
. The
augmented category
associated to
\mathcal{C}
is given by
\mathcal{C}^{\aug}:=\mathcal{C}_{/\emptyset}
the category of objects over
\emptyset
.

(iii) Let
\mathcal{C}
be a category containing an object
X
. We will denote by
\mathcal{C}_{X//X}:=(\mathcal{C}_{/X})_{*}
the pointed category associated to the over category
\mathcal{C}_{/X}
. More explicitly, objects of
\mathcal{C}_{X//X}
are the diagrams
X\overset{f}{\longrightarrow}A\overset{g}{\longrightarrow}X
in
\mathcal{C}
such that
gf=\Id_{X}
. Alternatively,
\mathcal{C}_{X//X}
is the same as
(\mathcal{C}_{X/})^{\aug}
the augmented category associated to the under category
\mathcal{C}_{X/}
.

Note that a morphism
f:X\rightarrow Y
in
\mathcal{C}
gives rise to a canonical adjunction
f_{!}\colon\mathcal{C}_{X//X}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr}}}\mathcal{C}_{Y//Y}\penalty\mspace{6muplus1mu}{:}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu\mathpunct{}\mspace{2mu}f^{*}
defined by

\displaystyle f_{!}(X\rightarrow A\rightarrow X)=(Y\longrightarrow A\,\underset{X}{\bigsqcup}\,Y\longrightarrow Y)\;\;\text{and}

\displaystyle f^{*}(Y\rightarrow B\rightarrow Y)=(X\longrightarrow B\times_{Y}X\longrightarrow X).

It can be shown that if
M
is a combinatorial model category such that the domains of generating cofibrations are cofibrant, then so is the transferred model structure on
\textbf{M}_{A//A}
(see Hirschhorn’s
[
26
]
). This makes the following definition valid.

Definition 2.5.10
.

Let
M
be a combinatorial model category such that the domains of generating cofibrations are cofibrant and let
A
be an object of
M
. The
tangent category
to
M
at
A
, denoted by
\mathcal{T}_{A}\textbf{M}
, is defined to be the stabilization of
\textbf{M}_{A//A}
:

\mathcal{T}_{A}\textbf{M}:=\Sp(\textbf{M}_{A//A}).

(2.5.1)

There is a Quillen adjunction
\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}\colon\textbf{M}_{/A}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr}}}\mathcal{T}_{A}\textbf{M}\penalty\mspace{6muplus1mu}{:}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu\mathpunct{}\mspace{2mu}\Omega^{\infty}_{+}
given by the composition

\textbf{M}_{/A}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle A\sqcup(-)\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\forget\cr}}}\textbf{M}_{A//A}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\Sigma^{\infty}\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\Omega^{\infty}\cr}}}\mathcal{T}_{A}\textbf{M}.

Namely, for each
B\in\textbf{M}_{/A}
, then we have
\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}(B)=\Sigma^{\infty}(A\longrightarrow A\sqcup B\longrightarrow A)
, while for each
X\in\mathcal{T}_{A}\textbf{M}
, we have
\Omega^{\infty}_{+}(X)=(X_{0,0}\longrightarrow A)
.

The following notion keeps a central role within the framework of Quillen cohomology theory.

Definition 2.5.11
.

Let
M
be a combinatorial model category such that the domains of generating cofibrations are cofibrant and let
A
be an object of
M
. The
cotangent complex
of
A
, denoted by
\rL_{A}
, is defined to be the derived suspension spectrum of
A
, i.e.,
\rL_{A}:=\mathbb{L}\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}(A)\in\mathcal{T}_{A}\textbf{M}
.

By Theorem
2.5.7
(iv), a given map
f:A\rightarrow B
in
M
gives rise to a Quillen adjunction between tangent categories

f_{!}^{\Sp}\colon\mathcal{T}_{A}\textbf{M}=\Sp(\textbf{M}_{A//A})\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr}}}\Sp(\textbf{M}_{B//B})=\mathcal{T}_{B}\textbf{M}\penalty\mspace{6muplus1mu}{:}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu\mathpunct{}\mspace{2mu}f^{*}_{\Sp}.

Moreover, there is a commutative square of left Quillen functors

\textstyle{\textbf{M}_{/A}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}}
\textstyle{\textbf{M}_{/B}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathcal{T}_{A}\textbf{M}}
\scriptstyle{f_{!}^{Sp}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{T}_{B}\textbf{M}}

(2.5.2)

Remark 2.5.12
.

For a weak equivalence
A\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}B
between fibrant-cofibrant objects in
M
, one can show that the induced adjunction
\textbf{M}_{A//A}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\textbf{M}_{B//B}
is a Quillen equivalence. Accordingly, the tangent categories to
M
are homotopy-invariant with respect to the class of such objects. In other words, the tangent category
\mathcal{T}_{A}\textbf{M}
has the right type as soon as
A
is fibrant-cofibrant. Note also that the requirement for fibrancy (resp. cofibrancy) on
A
can be omitted when
M
is in addition right (resp. left) proper. This follows from the fact that, when
M
is right (resp. left) proper, the model category
\textbf{M}_{/X}
(resp.
\textbf{M}_{X/}
) has already the right type for every object
X
.

Definition 2.5.13
.

Let
M
be a combinatorial model category such that the domains of generating cofibrations are cofibrant and let
f:A\rightarrow B
be a map in
M
. We will denote by

\rL_{B/A}:=\hocofib\,[\,\mathbb{L}\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}(f)\longrightarrow\rL_{B}\,]

the homotopy cofiber of the map
\mathbb{L}\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}(f)\longrightarrow\rL_{B}
in
\mathcal{T}_{B}\textbf{M}
, and refer to
\rL_{B/A}
as the
relative cotangent complex
of
f
.

Notice that the map
\mathbb{L}\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}(f)\longrightarrow\rL_{B}
can be identified with the map
f_{!}^{\Sp}(\rL_{A})\longrightarrow\rL_{B}
, due to the commutativity of the square (
2.5.2
).

Remark 2.5.14
.

Suppose that
A
is cofibrant. Take a factorization
A\longrightarrow B^{\cof}\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}B
in
M
of the map
f
into a cofibration followed by a weak equivalence. In particular,
B^{\cof}
is a cofibrant resolution of
B
in
M
. Consider the coCartesian square

\textstyle{B\sqcup A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B\sqcup B^{\cof}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces B}
\textstyle{B\,\underset{A}{\bigsqcup}\,B^{\cof}}

When regarded as a coCartesian square in
\textbf{M}_{B//B}
, this square is homotopy coCartesian because the map
A\longrightarrow B^{\cof}
is a cofibration between cofibrant objects in
M
and in addition,
B
is a zero object in
\textbf{M}_{B//B}
. Applying the functor
\Sigma^{\infty}:\textbf{M}_{B//B}\longrightarrow\mathcal{T}_{B}\textbf{M}
to this square, we obtain a homotopy cofiber sequence in
\mathcal{T}_{B}\textbf{M}
of the form

\Sigma^{\infty}(B\sqcup A)\longrightarrow\Sigma^{\infty}(B\sqcup B^{\cof})\longrightarrow\Sigma^{\infty}(B\,\underset{A}{\bigsqcup}\,B^{\cof}).

In this sequence, the first term is a model for
\mathbb{L}\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}(f)
, while the second term is nothing but
\rL_{B}
. Thus by definition
\Sigma^{\infty}(B\,\underset{A}{\bigsqcup}\,B^{\cof})
is a model for the relative cotangent complex
\rL_{B/A}
.

Definition 2.5.15
.

(
[
22
]
, Definition 2.2.1) Let
M
be a combinatorial model category such that the domains of generating cofibrations are cofibrant and let
X
be a fibrant object of
M
. Suppose given an object
M\in\mathcal{T}_{X}\textbf{M}
, regarded as the
spectrum of coefficients
. For each
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, the
n
’th Quillen cohomology group of
X
with coefficients in
M
is defined to be

\sH^{n}_{Q}(X;M):=\pi_{0}\Map^{\der}_{\mathcal{T}_{X}\textbf{M}}(\rL_{X},M[n])

where
M[n]:=\Sigma^{n}M
, i.e., the
n
-suspension of
M
in
\mathcal{T}_{X}\textbf{M}
.

Remark 2.5.16
.

By the Quillen adjunction
\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}\colon\textbf{M}_{/X}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr}}}\mathcal{T}_{X}\textbf{M}\penalty\mspace{6muplus1mu}{:}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu\mathpunct{}\mspace{2mu}\Omega^{\infty}_{+}
, there is a canonical weak equivalence

\Map^{\der}_{\textbf{M}_{/X}}(X,{\mathbb{R}}\Omega^{\infty}_{+}M[n])\simeq\Map^{\der}_{\mathcal{T}_{X}\textbf{M}}(\rL_{X},M[n]).

In particular, we have that

\sH^{n}_{Q}(X;M)=\pi_{0}\Map^{\der}_{\mathcal{T}_{X}\textbf{M}}(\rL_{X},M[n])\cong\pi_{0}\Map^{\der}_{\textbf{M}_{/X}}(X,{\mathbb{R}}\Omega^{\infty}_{+}M[n]).

Remark 2.5.17
.

Quillen cohomology is homotopy-invariant with respect to the class of fibrant objects. Indeed, a weak equivalence
f:X\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}Y
between fibrant objects induces a right Quillen equivalence
\textbf{M}_{/Y}\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\textbf{M}_{/X}
. Therefore, for any fibrant object
M\in\mathcal{T}_{Y}\textbf{M}
we get a canonical weak equivalence

\Map^{\der}_{\textbf{M}_{/Y}}(Y,\Omega^{\infty}_{+}M[n])\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\Map^{\der}_{\textbf{M}_{/X}}(X,f^{*}\Omega^{\infty}_{+}M[n])=\Map^{\der}_{\textbf{M}_{/X}}(X,\Omega^{\infty}_{+}(f_{\Sp}^{*}M)[n]).

This induces an isomorphism
\sH^{n}_{Q}(Y;M)\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}\sH^{n}_{Q}(X;f_{\Sp}^{*}M)
, by Remark
2.5.16
.

The above remark explains the requirement for fibrancy on objects. Nonetheless, as in Remark
2.5.12
, this requirement can be disregarded when
M
is in addition right proper.

3
Conventions

We first recall from
[
24
]
the following definition, which itself is inspired by [
[
37
]
, Definition 6.1.1.6].

Definition 3.0.1
.

A model category
M
is said to be
differentiable
if the derived colimit functor
\mathbb{L}\colim:\textbf{M}^{\mathbb{N}}\longrightarrow\textbf{M}
preserves finite homotopy limits. A Quillen adjunction
\mathcal{L}:\textbf{M}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\textbf{N}:\mathcal{R}
is said to be
differentiable
if both
M
and
N
are differentiable and the right derived functor
\mathbb{R}\mathcal{R}
preserves sequential homotopy colimits.

Conventions 3.0.2
.

Let
\mathcal{S}
be a combinatorial symmetric monoidal model category such that the domains of generating cofibrations are cofibrant.

\bullet
We will say that
\mathcal{S}
is
sufficient
if it satisfies the conditions (S1)-(S4) of Proposition
2.4.8
and in addition:

(S5)
\mathcal{S}
is differentiable, and

(S6) the unit
1_{\mathcal{S}}
is
homotopy compact
in the sense that the functor
\pi_{0}\Map^{\h}_{\mathcal{S}}(1_{\mathcal{S}},-)
sends filtered homotopy colimits to colimits of sets.

\bullet
Moreover,
\mathcal{S}
is said to be
abundant
if it is sufficient, and in addition:

(S7)
\mathcal{S}
satisfies the Lurie’s
invertibility hypothesis
[
[
35
]
, Definition A.3.2.12].

\bullet
We will say that
\mathcal{S}
is
stably sufficient
(resp.
stably abundant
) if it is sufficient (resp. abundant), and such that:

(S8)
\mathcal{S}
is a stable model category containing a strict zero object.

In particular, when
\mathcal{S}
is sufficient, we will work on the canonical model structure on
\Op(\mathcal{S})
(and also,
\Cat(\mathcal{S})
), which coincides with the Dwyer-Kan model structure by Proposition
2.4.8
. On other hand, for any category

\mathcal{M}\,\in\,\{\Coll_{C}(\mathcal{S}),\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S}),\LMod(\mathcal{P}),\RMod(\mathcal{P}),\BMod(\mathcal{P}),\IbMod(\mathcal{P}),\Alg_{\mathcal{P}}(\mathcal{S}),\Mod_{\mathcal{P}}^{A}\},

we will work on the transferred model structure on
\mathcal{M}
(see
\lx@sectionsign
2.3
).

Remark 3.0.3
.

Requiring the domains of generating cofibrations to be cofibrant is necessary for the existence of various types of operadic tangent category (cf.
\lx@sectionsign
2.5
).

Remark 3.0.4
.

The conditions (S5)-(S6) support the differentiability of
\Op(\mathcal{S})
and
\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})
(cf. Lemma
4.1.3
), which is needed for proving Proposition
4.1.4
.

Remark 3.0.5
.

The condition (S7) allows us to inherit [
[
22
]
, Proposition 3.2.1] for the work of
\lx@sectionsign
5.2
. Roughly speaking, the invertibility hypothesis requires that, for any
\mathcal{C}\in\Cat(\mathcal{S})
containing a morphism
f
, localizing
\mathcal{C}
at
f
does not change the homotopy type of
\mathcal{C}
as long as
f
is already an isomorphism in
\Ho(\mathcal{C})
. This condition is in fact quite common in practice. According to
[
32
]
, if
\mathcal{S}
is a combinatorial monoidal model category satisfying (S1) and such that every object is cofibrant then
\mathcal{S}
satisfies the invertibility hypothesis. It also holds for dg modules over a commutative ring by [
[
54
]
, Corollary 8.7], and for any simplicial monoidal model category, according to [
[
13
]
, Theorem 0.9].

Example 3.0.6
.

While all the base categories mentioned in Examples
2.3.7
are abundant, only
\mathcal{C}(\textbf{k})
is stably abundant.

4
Operadic tangent categories

Throughout this section, we will assume that
\mathcal{S}
is a base category that is (at least)
sufficient
in the sense of Conventions
3.0.2
. Moreover, let
\mathcal{P}
be a fixed
C
-colored operad in
\mathcal{S}
.

Note that, as well as every type of monoid, there is a
restriction functor

\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{P}/}\longrightarrow\BMod(\mathcal{P})_{\mathcal{P}/}
, which admits a left adjoint called the
induction functor
. Combined with (
2.1.1
) and (
2.2.1
), this yields a sequence of adjunctions

\IbMod(\mathcal{P})_{\mathcal{P}/}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\BMod(\mathcal{P})_{\mathcal{P}/}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{P}/}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\Op(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{P}/}.

(4.0.1)

The latter induces a sequence of adjunctions connecting the associated augmented categories

\IbMod(\mathcal{P})_{\mathcal{P}//\mathcal{P}}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\BMod(\mathcal{P})_{\mathcal{P}//\mathcal{P}}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{P}//\mathcal{P}}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\Op(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{P}//\mathcal{P}}.

Observe that each of the right adjoints in this sequence preserves fibrations and weak equivalences. So all the adjunctions above are Quillen adjunctions. We thus obtain a sequence of Quillen adjunctions connecting the associated tangent categories (cf. Theorem
2.5.7
(iv)):

\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\IbMod(\mathcal{P})\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\BMod(\mathcal{P})\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op(\mathcal{S})\,.

(4.0.2)

Recall that the operad
\mathcal{P}\in\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})
is said to be
\Sigma
-cofibrant
if its underlying
C
-collection is cofibrant as an object of
\Coll_{C}(\mathcal{S})
. Our goal in this section is to prove that the adjunctions in the above sequence are all Quillen equivalences when
\mathcal{P}
is
\Sigma
-cofibrant. The work may require making use of the
Comparison theorem

[
23
]
, which we now recall.

Let
M
be a symmetric monoidal model category and let
\mathcal{O}
be an
M
-enriched operad. We denote by
\mathcal{O}_{\leqslant 1}
the operad obtained from
\mathcal{O}
by removing the operations of arity
>1
. Recall that the collection of nullary operations
\mathcal{O}_{0}
inherits the obvious structure of an
\mathcal{O}
-algebra and then, becomes an initial object in the category
\Alg_{\mathcal{O}}(\textbf{M})
.

Definition 4.0.1
.

The operad
\mathcal{O}
is said to be
admissible
if the transferred model structure on
\Alg_{\mathcal{O}}(\textbf{M})
exists. Furthermore,
\mathcal{O}
is called
stably admissible
(resp.
stably semi admissible
) if it is admissible and the stabilization
\Sp(\Alg^{\aug}_{\mathcal{O}}(\textbf{M}))
exists as a model (resp. semi-model) category, where
\Alg^{\aug}_{\mathcal{O}}(\textbf{M}):=\Alg_{\mathcal{O}}(\textbf{M})_{\mathcal{O}_{0}//\mathcal{O}_{0}}
the augmented category associated to the category
\Alg_{\mathcal{O}}(\textbf{M})=\Alg_{\mathcal{O}}(\textbf{M})_{\mathcal{O}_{0}/}
.

Note that there is a canonical isomorphism
\Alg_{\,\mathcal{O}_{\leqslant 1}}(\textbf{M})\cong\Alg_{\,\mathcal{O}_{1}}(\textbf{M})_{\mathcal{O}_{0}/}
. Now the inclusion of operads
\varphi:\mathcal{O}_{\leqslant 1}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}
induces a Quillen adjunction

\varphi_{!}^{\aug}\colon\Alg^{\aug}_{\,\mathcal{O}_{\leqslant 1}}(\textbf{M})=\Alg_{\,\mathcal{O}_{1}}(\textbf{M})_{\mathcal{O}_{0}//\mathcal{O}_{0}}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr}}}\Alg_{\mathcal{O}}(\textbf{M})_{\mathcal{O}_{0}//\mathcal{O}_{0}}=\Alg^{\aug}_{\mathcal{O}}(\textbf{M})\penalty\mspace{6muplus1mu}{:}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu\mathpunct{}\mspace{2mu}\varphi^{*}_{\aug}.

Theorem 4.0.2
.

(
Comparison theorem
) [Y. Harpaz, J. Nuiten and M. Prasma
[
23
]
] Let
M
be a differentiable, left proper and combinatorial symmetric monoidal model category and let
\mathcal{O}
be a
\Sigma
-cofibrant stably admissible operad in
M
. Assume either
M
is right proper or
\mathcal{O}_{0}
is fibrant. Then the induced Quillen adjunction between stabilizations

\varphi_{!}^{\Sp}\colon\Sp(\Alg^{\aug}_{\,\mathcal{O}_{\leqslant 1}}(\textbf{M}))\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr}}}\Sp(\Alg^{\aug}_{\mathcal{O}}(\textbf{M}))\penalty\mspace{6muplus1mu}{:}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu\mathpunct{}\mspace{2mu}\varphi^{*}_{\Sp}

is a Quillen equivalence.

Remark 4.0.3
.

In fact, many model categories of interest are not left proper (where
\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})
and
\Op(\mathcal{S})
are typical examples) and as a sequel, their stabilizations may not exist as (full) model categories (cf. Remark
2.5.5
). In loc. cit., the authors were aware of this fact, and made sure to include Corollary 4.1.4 saying that the restriction functor
\varphi^{*}_{\aug}:\Alg^{\aug}_{\mathcal{O}}(\textbf{M})\longrightarrow\Alg^{\aug}_{\,\mathcal{O}_{\leqslant 1}}(\textbf{M})
, under the same assumptions as in Theorem
4.0.2
except the left properness of
M
, induces an equivalence of
relative categories
after taking stabilizations

\varphi^{*}_{\Sp^{\prime}}:\Sp^{\prime}(\Alg^{\aug}_{\mathcal{O}}(\textbf{M}))\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\Sp^{\prime}(\Alg^{\aug}_{\mathcal{O}_{\leqslant 1}}(\textbf{M})).

In particular, when the stabilizations exist as semi model categories then
\varphi_{!}^{\Sp}\dashv\varphi^{*}_{\Sp}
is indeed a Quillen equivalence. So keep in mind that the statement of the Comparison theorem remains valid when
\mathcal{P}
is just stably semi admissible.

4.1
The first Quillen equivalence

The Quillen adjunction
\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{P}}\colon\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{P}/}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr}}}\Op(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{P}/}\penalty\mspace{6muplus1mu}{:}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu\mathpunct{}\mspace{2mu}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{P}}
(
2.1.1
) lifts to a Quillen adjunction between the associated tangent categories

\mathcal{L}^{\Sp}_{\mathcal{P}}\colon\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr}}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op(\mathcal{S})\penalty\mspace{6muplus1mu}{:}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu\mathpunct{}\mspace{2mu}\mathcal{R}^{\Sp}_{\mathcal{P}}.

Our goal in this subsection is to prove that
\mathcal{L}^{\Sp}_{\mathcal{P}}\dashv\mathcal{R}^{\Sp}_{\mathcal{P}}
is a Quillen equivalence. But first, let us start with the following simple observations.

Observations 4.1.1
.

We write
{\mathcal{L}}:\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})\longrightarrow\Op(\mathcal{S})
to denote the obvious embedding functor.

(i) The functor
{\mathcal{L}}
preserves and detects weak equivalences, cofibrations and trivial fibrations.

(ii) The functor
{\mathcal{L}}
preserves and detects cofibrant and fibrant objects.

(iii) The functor
{\mathcal{L}}
preserves and detects cofibrant resolutions.

Proof.

Let
f:\mathcal{P}\rightarrow\mathcal{Q}
be a map between
C
-colored operads.

(i) If
f
is a weak equivalence in
\Op(\mathcal{S})
then it is in particular a levelwise weak equivalence, i.e., a weak equivalence in
\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})
. Conversely, suppose that
f
is a weak equivalence in
\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})
. Since
f
is the identity on colors, the induced map
\Ho(f)
is automatically essentially surjective (cf. Definition
2.4.4
). Thus by definition
f
is a Dwyer-Kan equivalence.

The claim about trivial fibrations immediately follows by definition.

Assume that
f
is a cofibration in
\Op(\mathcal{S})
. We prove that
f
is one in
\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})
. For any given trivial fibration
f^{\prime}:\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
in
\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})
, we have to show that
f
has the left lifting property against
f^{\prime}
. Since
f^{\prime}
is also a trivial fibration in
\Op(\mathcal{S})
, the lifting problem has a solution in
\Op(\mathcal{S})
. But such a lift must be the identity on colors, so it is also a lift in
\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})
. We just showed that
f
is a cofibration in
\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})
. Conversely, if
f
is a cofibration in
\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})
then it is one in
\Op(\mathcal{S})
, just by the fact that the embedding
\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{P}}:\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{P}/}\longrightarrow\Op(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{P}/}
is a left Quillen functor.

(ii) The claim about the fibrancy immediately follows by definition. We now prove the claim about cofibrancy. It can be shown that
{\mathcal{L}}
detects cofibrant objects, similar to how we verify it on cofibrations. For the converse direction, since
{\mathcal{L}}
already preserves cofibrations, we just need to show that the initial
C
-colored operad
\mathcal{I}_{C}
is also cofibrant as an object of
\Op(\mathcal{S})
. Notice that a map in
\Op(\mathcal{S})
from
\mathcal{I}_{C}
to an operad
\mathcal{O}
is fully characterized by a map from
C
to the set of colors of
\mathcal{O}
. The claim hence follows by the fact that any trivial fibration in
\Op(\mathcal{S})
has a surjective underlying map between colors.

(iii) This follows by the two above.
∎

To prove the adjunction
\mathcal{L}^{\Sp}_{\mathcal{P}}\dashv\mathcal{R}^{\Sp}_{\mathcal{P}}
is a Quillen equivalence, we will make use of [
[
24
]
, Corollary 2.4.9]. To be able to use this tool, we must show that the Quillen adjunction

\mathcal{L}^{\aug}_{\mathcal{P}}\colon\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{P}//\mathcal{P}}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr}}}\Op(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{P}//\mathcal{P}}\penalty\mspace{6muplus1mu}{:}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu\mathpunct{}\mspace{2mu}\mathcal{R}^{\aug}_{\mathcal{P}}

is differentiable (cf. Definition
3.0.1
).

Remark 4.1.2
.

By convention, the base category
\mathcal{S}
is differentiable, with weak equivalences closed under sequential colimits. It implies that the (underived) functor
\colim:\mathcal{S}^{\mathbb{N}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{S}
already preserves homotopy Cartesian squares and homotopy terminal objects. An analogue holds for the functor
\colim:\Cat(\mathcal{S})^{\mathbb{N}}\longrightarrow\Cat(\mathcal{S})
, due to Remark
3.0.4
and [
[
22
]
, Lemma 3.1.10] (saying that weak equivalences in
\Cat(\mathcal{S})
are closed under sequential colimits).

Lemma 4.1.3
.

The Quillen adjunction
\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{P}}\colon\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{P}/}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr}}}\Op(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{P}/}\penalty\mspace{6muplus1mu}{:}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu\mathpunct{}\mspace{2mu}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{P}}
is differentiable. Consequently, the induced Quillen adjunction

\mathcal{L}^{\aug}_{\mathcal{P}}:\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{P}//\mathcal{P}}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\Op(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{P}//\mathcal{P}}:\mathcal{R}^{\aug}_{\mathcal{P}}

is differentiable as well.

Proof.

Note first that sequential colimits of
\mathcal{S}
-enriched categories are created in
\mathcal{S}
on each level (cf. [
[
22
]
, Lemma 3.1.10]). We claim that an analogue holds for enriched operads. Consider a sequence of objects in
\Op(\mathcal{S})

\mathcal{P}^{(0)}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}^{(1)}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}^{(2)}\longrightarrow\cdots.

We construct an operad
\mathcal{P}
as follows. Let us take
\sCol(\mathcal{P}):=\colim_{n}\,\sCol(\mathcal{P}^{(n)})
where
\sCol(-)
refers to set of colors. For each
\overline{c}:=(c_{1},\cdots,c_{{m}};c)\in\Seq(\sCol(\mathcal{P}))
, we pick
n_{0}
large enough such that
\overline{c}\in\Seq(\sCol(\mathcal{P}^{(n_{0})}))
. Then we take
\mathcal{P}(\overline{c}):=\colim_{n\geqslant n_{0}}\,\mathcal{P}^{(n)}(\overline{c})
. The operad structures on the terms
\mathcal{P}^{(n)}
’s together induce an operad structure on
\mathcal{P}
. It can then be verified that
\mathcal{P}\cong\colim_{n}\mathcal{P}^{(n)}
. In particular, by construction the underlying category
\mathcal{P}_{1}
is isomorphic to
\colim_{n}\mathcal{P}^{(n)}_{1}
.

We now claim that weak equivalences in
\Op(\mathcal{S})
are closed under sequential colimits. Indeed, recall that a map
f
in
\Op(\mathcal{S})
is a weak equivalence if and only if it is a levelwise weak equivalence and such that the map
f_{1}
between underlying categories is a weak equivalence in
\Cat(\mathcal{S})
(cf. Remark
2.4.7
). Hence, the claim follows by the above paragraph and by the fact that weak equivalences in
\mathcal{S}
and
\Cat(\mathcal{S})
are closed under sequential colimits.

Next we claim that a square in
\Op(\mathcal{S})
is homotopy Cartesian if and only if the following two conditions hold:

1.

the induced squares of spaces of operations are homotopy Cartesian in
\mathcal{S}
, and

2.

the induced square of underlying categories is homotopy Cartesian in
\Cat(\mathcal{S})
.

Indeed, observe first that the statement is already correct when we forget the word
``
homotopy”. Combined with Remark
2.4.7
, this observation verifies the claim. Besides that, it is not hard to show that an object of
\Op(\mathcal{S})
is homotopy terminal if and only if all its spaces of operations are homotopy terminal in
\mathcal{S}
.

We now show that
\Op(\mathcal{S})
is differentiable. By the second paragraph, it suffices to verify that the functor
\colim:\Op(\mathcal{S})^{\mathbb{N}}\longrightarrow\Op(\mathcal{S})
preserves homotopy Cartesian squares and homotopy terminal objects. This follows by combining the first and third paragraphs, along with Remark
4.1.2
. In the same manner, one can show that the category
\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})
is also differentiable.

The differentiability of
\Op(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{P}/}
(resp.
\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{P}/}
) follows from that of
\Op(\mathcal{S})
(resp.
\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})
). Moreover, it is clear that the functor
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{P}}
preserves sequential homotopy colimits. Thus the adjunction
\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{P}}\dashv\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{P}}
is differentiable, and hence so is the adjunction
\mathcal{L}^{\aug}_{\mathcal{P}}\dashv\mathcal{R}^{\aug}_{\mathcal{P}}
.
∎

We are now in position to prove the main result of this subsection.

Proposition 4.1.4
.

The adjunction
\mathcal{L}^{\Sp}_{\mathcal{P}}\colon\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr}}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op(\mathcal{S})\penalty\mspace{6muplus1mu}{:}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu\mathpunct{}\mspace{2mu}\mathcal{R}^{\Sp}_{\mathcal{P}}
is a Quillen equivalence.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{Q}\in\Op(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{P}//\mathcal{P}}
be a fibrant object, exhibited by a diagram
\mathcal{P}\rightarrow\mathcal{Q}\rightarrow\mathcal{P}
in
\Op(\mathcal{S})
such that the second map is a fibration. The same argument as in the proof of [
[
22
]
, Lemma 3.1.13] shows that the map between the homotopy pullbacks

\mathcal{P}\times^{\h}_{\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{P}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{P}}(\mathcal{Q})}\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}\times^{\h}_{\mathcal{Q}}\mathcal{P}

is a weak equivalence in
\Op(\mathcal{S})
. (We note here that, since
\Op(\mathcal{S})
is right proper, the requirement for fibrancy on
\mathcal{P}
can be omitted). In particular, the induced map
\Omega\,\mathcal{L}^{\aug}_{\mathcal{P}}\mathcal{R}^{\aug}_{\mathcal{P}}(\mathcal{Q})\longrightarrow\Omega\,\mathcal{Q}
is a weak equivalence in
\Op(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{P}//\mathcal{P}}
. This fact, together with Lemma
4.1.3
, allows us to apply [
[
24
]
, Corollary 2.4.9] to deduce that the derived counit of the Quillen adjunction
\mathcal{L}^{\Sp}_{\mathcal{P}}\dashv\mathcal{R}^{\Sp}_{\mathcal{P}}
is a stable equivalence for every fibrant object.

It remains to show that the derived unit of
\mathcal{L}^{\Sp}_{\mathcal{P}}\dashv\mathcal{R}^{\Sp}_{\mathcal{P}}
is a stable equivalence for any cofibrant object. In fact, we will show that this holds for the larger class of levelwise cofibrant objects. Since
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{P}}^{\aug}\circ\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{P}}^{\aug}
is isomorphic to the identity functor and since
\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{P}}^{\aug}
preserves weak equivalences, the derived unit of
\mathcal{L}_{\mathcal{P}}^{\aug}\dashv\mathcal{R}_{\mathcal{P}}^{\aug}
is a weak equivalence. By the first part of [
[
24
]
, Corollary 2.4.9], the derived unit of
\mathcal{L}^{\Sp}_{\mathcal{P}}\dashv\mathcal{R}^{\Sp}_{\mathcal{P}}
is a stable equivalence for any levelwise cofibrant prespectrum. But every levelwise cofibrant object in
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})
is stably equivalent to a levelwise cofibrant prespectrum (see
[
24
]
, Remark 2.3.6), it therefore suffices to argue that
\mathcal{L}^{\Sp}_{\mathcal{P}}
preserves stable equivalences. But this immediately follows from Observation
2.5.8
.
∎

4.2
The second Quillen equivalence

The next step is to prove the following.

Proposition 4.2.1
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{P}
is
\Sigma
-cofibrant. Then the adjunction

\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\IbMod(\mathcal{P})\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})

is a Quillen equivalence.

The proof will require a technical lemma. Let us take a cofibrant resolution
f:\mathcal{Q}\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{P}
for
\mathcal{P}\in\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})
such that
f
is a trivial fibration.

Lemma 4.2.2
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{P}
is
\Sigma
-cofibrant. Then the induced adjunctions

\displaystyle\widetilde{f_{!}}:\IbMod(\mathcal{Q})_{\mathcal{Q}//\mathcal{Q}}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\IbMod(\mathcal{P})_{\mathcal{P}//\mathcal{P}}:\widetilde{f_{*}},\;\;\text{and}

\displaystyle\hat{f_{!}}:\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{Q}//\mathcal{Q}}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{P}//\mathcal{P}}:\hat{f_{*}}

are a Quillen equivalence.

Proof.

Let us start with the second one. For an object
\mathcal{O}\in\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{P}//\mathcal{P}}
, recall by definition that
\hat{f_{*}}(\mathcal{O})
is given by the pullback
\mathcal{Q}\times_{\mathcal{P}}\mathcal{O}\in\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{Q}//\mathcal{Q}}
. Note that the map
\hat{f_{*}}(\mathcal{O})\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}
is a trivial fibration, since it is a base change of
f
. In particular,
\hat{f_{*}}
creates weak equivalences. Thus, we just need to show that for any cofibrant object
\mathcal{R}\in\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{Q}//\mathcal{Q}}
exhibited by a sequence
\mathcal{Q}\longrightarrow\mathcal{R}\longrightarrow\mathcal{Q}
such that the first map is a cofibration, the unit map
\mathcal{R}\longrightarrow\hat{f_{*}}\hat{f_{!}}(\mathcal{R})
is a weak equivalence. This is equivalent to saying that the composed map

\mathcal{R}\longrightarrow\hat{f_{*}}\hat{f_{!}}(\mathcal{R})\longrightarrow\hat{f_{!}}(\mathcal{R})

is a weak equivalence. By construction, the latter is given by the induced map
\mathcal{R}\longrightarrow\mathcal{R}\,\underset{\mathcal{Q}}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{P}
, which is indeed a weak equivalence due to the
relative left properness
(cf., e.g.
[
8
,
52
,
18
]
), after having assumed
\mathcal{P}
to be
\Sigma
-cofibrant.

We now show that
\widetilde{f_{!}}\dashv\widetilde{f_{*}}
is a Quillen equivalence. First, by the fact that the map
f:\mathcal{Q}\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{P}
is a weak equivalence between levelwise cofibrant operads, we obtain that the induced adjunction

\IbMod(\mathcal{Q})_{\mathcal{Q}/}\cong\Alg_{\,\textbf{B}^{\mathcal{Q}/}_{\leqslant 1}}(\mathcal{S})\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\Alg_{\,\textbf{B}^{\mathcal{P}/}_{\leqslant 1}}(\mathcal{S})\cong\IbMod(\mathcal{P})_{\mathcal{P}/}

(4.2.1)

is a Quillen equivalence (see Construction
2.2.21
). Now,
for an object
M\in\IbMod(\mathcal{P})_{\mathcal{P}//\mathcal{P}}
we have
\widetilde{f_{*}}(M)=\mathcal{Q}\times_{\mathcal{P}}M\in\IbMod(\mathcal{Q})_{\mathcal{Q}//\mathcal{Q}}
where
\mathcal{P}
and
M
are considered as infinitesimal
\mathcal{Q}
-bimodules, with the structure induced by
f
. Therefore, similarly to the case above, the map
\widetilde{f_{*}}(M)\longrightarrow M
is a trivial fibration in
\IbMod(\mathcal{Q})
, and hence,
\widetilde{f_{*}}
also creates weak equivalences. Thus, along with the previous observation, the same argument as in the case above can be applied to complete the proof.
∎

Proof of Proposition
4.2.1
.

In the first step, let us assume further that
\mathcal{P}
is cofibrant. We regard
\mathcal{P}
as an algebra over
\textbf{O}_{C}
the operad of
C
-colored operads. Then we get a canonical isomorphism
\Alg_{\Env(\textbf{O}_{C},\mathcal{P})}(\mathcal{S})\cong\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{P}/}
between the category of algebras over the enveloping operad
\Env(\textbf{O}_{C},\mathcal{P})
and the category of
C
-colored operads under
\mathcal{P}
(cf. §
2.2
). On other hand, similarly as in the proof of [
[
21
]
, Proposition 3.5], it can be shown that the structure of an infinitesimal
\mathcal{P}
-bimodule is equivalent to that of a
\mathcal{P}
-module over
\textbf{O}_{C}
. So we get an isomorphism of categories
\Alg_{\Env(\textbf{O}_{C},\mathcal{P})_{1}}(\mathcal{S})\cong\IbMod(\mathcal{P})
(cf. Remark
2.2.14
).

The symmetric groups act freely on
\textbf{O}_{C}
. In particular,
\textbf{O}_{C}
is
\Sigma
-cofibrant. Moreover, since
\mathcal{P}
is cofibrant, it implies that
\Env(\textbf{O}_{C},\mathcal{P})
is
\Sigma
-cofibrant as well (cf. [
[
41
]
, Lemma 6.1]). This allows us to apply the Comparison theorem
4.0.2
for
\Env(\textbf{O}_{C},\mathcal{P})
. The first paragraph shows that the functor

\Alg^{\aug}_{\Env(\textbf{O}_{C},\mathcal{P})_{\leqslant 1}}(\mathcal{S})\longrightarrow\Alg_{\Env(\textbf{O}_{C},\mathcal{P})}^{\aug}(\mathcal{S})

coincides with the functor
\IbMod(\mathcal{P})_{\mathcal{P}//\mathcal{P}}\longrightarrow\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{P}//\mathcal{P}}
. From this, we obtain that the adjunction
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\IbMod(\mathcal{P})\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})
is a Quillen equivalence.

Finally, in order to show that the statement already holds true when
\mathcal{P}
is
\Sigma
-cofibrant, we just need to make use of the above lemma.
∎

4.3
The third Quillen equivalence

Recall from §
2.2
that the category
\BMod(\mathcal{P})_{\mathcal{P}/}
can be represented as the category of algebras over the operad
\textbf{B}^{\mathcal{P}/}
, whose underlying category
\textbf{B}^{\mathcal{P}/}_{1}
agrees with
\textbf{Ib}^{\mathcal{P}}
(cf. Constructions
2.2.17
and
2.2.21
). The following is the last piece for the proof of Theorem
4.4.1
.

Proposition 4.3.1
.

The adjunction
\IbMod(\mathcal{P})_{\mathcal{P}/}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\BMod(\mathcal{P})_{\mathcal{P}/}
induces a Quillen equivalence of the associated tangent categories

\overset{\simeq}{\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\IbMod(\mathcal{P})\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\BMod(\mathcal{P})}

whenever
\mathcal{P}
is
\Sigma
-cofibrant.

Proof.

When
\mathcal{P}
is
\Sigma
-cofibrant, by construction
\textbf{B}^{\mathcal{P}/}
is also
\Sigma
-cofibrant. We are now applying the Comparison theorem
4.0.2
, along with noting Remark
4.0.3
, to the operad
\textbf{B}^{\mathcal{P}/}
. The key point is that the adjunction
\Alg^{\aug}_{\,\textbf{B}^{\mathcal{P}/}_{\leqslant 1}}(\mathcal{S})\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\Alg^{\aug}_{\,\textbf{B}^{\mathcal{P}/}}(\mathcal{S})
which arises from the inclusion
(\textbf{B}^{\mathcal{P}/})_{\leqslant 1}\longrightarrow\textbf{B}^{\mathcal{P}/}
is the same as the adjunction of induction-restriction functors

\IbMod(\mathcal{P})_{\mathcal{P}//\mathcal{P}}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\BMod(\mathcal{P})_{\mathcal{P}//\mathcal{P}}.

Thus the adjunction
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\IbMod(\mathcal{P})\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\BMod(\mathcal{P})
is indeed a Quillen equivalence.
∎

4.4
Main statements

Combining the propositions
4.1.4
,
4.2.1
and
4.3.1
, we obtain the main result of this section stated as follows.

Theorem 4.4.1
.

The adjunctions in the sequence

\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\IbMod(\mathcal{P})\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\BMod(\mathcal{P})\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op(\mathcal{S})

(4.4.1)

are all Quillen equivalences provided that
\mathcal{P}
is
\Sigma
-cofibrant.

We are also interested in the case of a stable base category. The same argument as in the proof of [
[
23
]
, Lemma 2.2.3] proves the following.

Lemma 4.4.2
.

Suppose further that
\mathcal{S}
is stably sufficient. Let
M\in\IbMod(\mathcal{P})
be a levelwise cofibrant infinitesimal
\mathcal{P}
-bimodule. Then the adjunction

(-)\sqcup M:\IbMod(\mathcal{P})\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\IbMod(\mathcal{P})_{M//M}:\ker

is a Quillen equivalence, where the functor
\ker
is defined by sending
[M\rightarrow P\rightarrow M]
to
P\times_{M}0
, while the left adjoint takes
N\in\IbMod(\mathcal{P})
to
[M\overset{i_{0}}{\longrightarrow}M\sqcup N\overset{\Id_{M}+0}{\xrightarrow{\hskip 28.45274pt}}M]
.

Theorem 4.4.3
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{S}
is stably sufficient and
\mathcal{P}
is
\Sigma
-cofibrant. The sequence (
4.4.1
) is then prolonged to a sequence of Quillen equivalences of the form

\IbMod(\mathcal{P})\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle(-)\sqcup\mathcal{P}\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\ker\cr}}}\IbMod(\mathcal{P})_{\mathcal{P}//\mathcal{P}}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\Sigma^{\infty}\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\Omega^{\infty}\cr}}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\IbMod(\mathcal{P})\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\simeq\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\BMod(\mathcal{P})\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\simeq\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\simeq\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op(\mathcal{S})

(4.4.2)

Proof.

The category
\IbMod(\mathcal{P})
is stable since
\mathcal{S}
is stable (cf. [
[
22
]
, Remark 2.2.2] and Proposition
2.2.18
) and thus, the category
\IbMod(\mathcal{P})_{\mathcal{P}//\mathcal{P}}
is stable as well. So by Theorem
2.5.7
the adjunction

\Sigma^{\infty}:\IbMod(\mathcal{P})_{\mathcal{P}//\mathcal{P}}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\IbMod(\mathcal{P}):\Omega^{\infty}

is a Quillen equivalence. The statement hence follows by Lemma
4.4.2
and Theorem
4.4.1
.
∎

5
Quillen cohomology of enriched operads

This section contains the central results of the paper. As the main goal, we give an explicit description of the cotangent complex of enriched operads. Moreover, we prove the existence of a long exact sequence relating Quillen cohomology and reduced Quillen cohomology of a given operad.

5.1
An extra condition

Let
\mathcal{S}
be a symmetric monoidal model category such that the transferred model structure on operadic algebras is available and let
\mathcal{P}
be a
C
-colored operad in
\mathcal{S}
.

Notation 5.1.1
.

We denote by
\BMod(\mathcal{P})^{*}:=\BMod(\mathcal{P})_{\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P}/}
the category of
\mathcal{P}
-bimodules under
\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P}
, which represents the free
\mathcal{P}
-bimodule generated by
\mathcal{I}_{C}
, and refer to
\BMod(\mathcal{P})^{*}
as the category of
pointed
\mathcal{P}
-bimodules
.

Observe that the composition
\mu:\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}
exhibits
\mathcal{P}
itself as a pointed
\mathcal{P}
-bimodule. Let
\mathcal{P}\sqcup\mathcal{P}\overset{f+g}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{Q}
be a map in
\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})
. Then
\mathcal{Q}
inherits a
\mathcal{P}
-bimodule structure with the left (resp. right)
\mathcal{P}
-action induced by
f
(resp.
g
). This determines a restriction functor

\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{P}\sqcup\mathcal{P}/}\longrightarrow\BMod(\mathcal{P})^{*},

which admits a left adjoint denoted by
\E:\BMod(\mathcal{P})^{*}\longrightarrow\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{P}\sqcup\mathcal{P}/}
. Observe that
\E
sends
\mathcal{P}\in\BMod(\mathcal{P})^{*}
to itself
\mathcal{P}\in\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{P}\sqcup\mathcal{P}/}
equipped with the fold map
\Id_{\mathcal{P}}+\Id_{\mathcal{P}}:\mathcal{P}\sqcup\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}
.

We recall a hypothesis introduced by Dwyer-Hess in
[
14
]
.

Definition 5.1.2
.

(
Goodness hypothesis
) We will say that the operad
\mathcal{P}\in\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})
is
good
if the left derived functor

\mathbb{L}\E:\BMod(\mathcal{P})^{*}\longrightarrow\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{P}\sqcup\mathcal{P}/}

sends
\mathcal{P}
to itself
\mathcal{P}
. Moreover, the base category
\mathcal{S}
is said to be
good
if every cofibrant operad in
\mathcal{S}
is good.

For example, in loc. cit., the authors verified that every
nonsymmetric simplicial operad
is good. Expanding upon their result, we shall now prove that the category
\Set_{\Delta}
is good (when working in the context of symmetric operads). Moreover, we show that the category
\sMod_{R}
with
R
being any commutative ring is also good.

Proposition 5.1.3
.

The category
\Set_{\Delta}
of simplicial sets is good. Namely, for every set of colors
C
and for every cofibrant simplicial
C
-colored operad
\mathcal{P}
, the left derived functor

\mathbb{L}\E:\BMod(\mathcal{P})^{*}\longrightarrow\Op_{C}(\Set_{\Delta})_{\mathcal{P}\sqcup\mathcal{P}/}

of
\E
sends
\mathcal{P}
to itself
\mathcal{P}
.

The proof first requires constructing a nice cofibrant resolution for
\mathcal{P}
as an object of
\BMod(\mathcal{P})^{*}
. For this, we will follow Rezk’s [
[
48
]
,
\lx@sectionsign
3.7.2]. (Nonetheless, note that the operadic model structures considered in loc. cit. are different from ours, so it should be used carefully). Let
\mathcal{M}
be a simplicial model category. The
diagonal
(or
realization
) functor
\diag:\mathcal{M}^{\Delta^{\op}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}
is by definition the left adjoint to the functor
\mathcal{M}\longrightarrow\mathcal{M}^{\Delta^{\op}}
taking each
X\in\mathcal{M}
to the simplicial object
[n]\mapsto X^{\Delta^{n}}
. For each
Y_{\bullet}\in\mathcal{M}^{\Delta^{\op}}
, one defines the
latching object

\rL_{n}Y_{\bullet}
as the coequalizer in
\mathcal{M}
of the form

\underset{0\leqslant i<j\leqslant n}{\bigsqcup}Y_{n-1}\;\mathrel{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{\hbox to\dimexpr\@tempdima+1em{#}\cr 0.00002pt{\rightarrowfill\cr\kern 2.15277pt\cr 0.00002pt{\rightarrowfill\cr}}}\limits^{\!}_{\!}}\;\underset{0\leqslant k\leqslant n}{\bigsqcup}Y_{n}\longrightarrow\rL_{n}Y_{\bullet}}}

in which one of the two maps sends the
(i,j)
summand to the
j
’th summand by
s_{i}
while the other sends the
(i,j)
summand to the
i
’th summand by
s_{j-1}
. By convention, one takes
\rL_{-1}Y_{\bullet}:=\emptyset
. By construction, there is a unique map
\rL_{n}Y_{\bullet}\longrightarrow Y_{n+1}
factoring the map
s_{k}:Y_{n}\longrightarrow Y_{n+1}
for every
k=0,\cdots,n
. One then constructs a filtration
\diag(Y_{\bullet})=\colim_{n}\diag_{n}Y_{\bullet}
built up inductively by taking
\diag_{0}Y_{\bullet}:=Y_{0}
and then, for each
n\geqslant 1
, taking the pushout:

\textstyle{d_{n}Y_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Delta^{n}\otimes Y_{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\diag_{n-1}Y_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\diag_{n}Y_{\bullet}}

(5.1.1)

in which

d_{n}Y_{\bullet}:=\Delta^{n}\otimes\rL_{n-1}Y_{\bullet}\bigsqcup_{\partial\Delta^{n}\otimes\rL_{n-1}Y_{\bullet}}\partial\Delta^{n}\otimes Y_{n}.

As a consequence, if for every
n\geqslant 0
the
latching map

\rL_{n-1}Y_{\bullet}\longrightarrow Y_{n}
is a cofibration then
\diag(Y_{\bullet})
is cofibrant. More generally, we have the following lemma.

Lemma 5.1.4
.

Let
X_{\bullet}\longrightarrow Y_{\bullet}
be a map of simplicial objects in
\mathcal{M}
. Suppose that for every
n\geqslant 0
the (relative) latching map

X_{n}\bigsqcup_{\rL_{n-1}X_{\bullet}}\rL_{n-1}Y_{\bullet}\longrightarrow Y_{n}

(5.1.2)

is a cofibration. Then the induced map
\diag(X_{\bullet})\longrightarrow\diag(Y_{\bullet})
is a cofibration as well.

Proof.

By the filtrations of
\diag(X_{\bullet})
and
\diag(Y_{\bullet})
mentioned above, the map
\diag(X_{\bullet})\longrightarrow\diag(Y_{\bullet})
is a cofibration as soon as the map
\diag_{n}X_{\bullet}\longrightarrow\diag_{n}Y_{\bullet}
is one for every
n\geqslant 0
. Note first that when
n=0
the map (
5.1.2
) coincides with the map
\diag_{0}X_{\bullet}\longrightarrow\diag_{0}Y_{\bullet}
. Let us assume by induction that the map
\diag_{n-1}X_{\bullet}\longrightarrow\diag_{n-1}Y_{\bullet}
is a cofibration. Then, factor
\diag_{n}X_{\bullet}\longrightarrow\diag_{n}Y_{\bullet}
as

\diag_{n}X_{\bullet}\longrightarrow\diag_{n}X_{\bullet}\bigsqcup_{\diag_{n-1}X_{\bullet}}\diag_{n-1}Y_{\bullet}\overset{\varphi}{\longrightarrow}\diag_{n}Y_{\bullet}.

By the inductive assumption, the first map in this composition is a cofibration. Hence, it remains to show that
\varphi
is a cofibration. Let us denote by
L_{n-1}(X_{\bullet},Y_{\bullet}):=X_{n}\bigsqcup_{\rL_{n-1}X_{\bullet}}\rL_{n-1}Y_{\bullet}
. We can then form a canonical map
