\Delta^{n}\otimes L_{n-1}(X_{\bullet},Y_{\bullet})\bigsqcup_{\partial\Delta^{n}\otimes L_{n-1}(X_{\bullet},Y_{\bullet})}\partial\Delta^{n}\otimes Y_{n}\longrightarrow\Delta^{n}\otimes Y_{n},

which is a cofibration by the pushout-product axiom. Moreover, it can be shown that this map is isomorphic to the canonical map

d_{n}Y_{\bullet}\bigsqcup_{d_{n}X_{\bullet}}\Delta^{n}\otimes X_{n}\longrightarrow\Delta^{n}\otimes Y_{n}.

(5.1.3)

Now, consider the following commutative cube
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{n}X_{\bullet}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{n}\otimes X_{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{n}Y_{\bullet}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{n}\otimes Y_{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\diag_{n-1}X_{\bullet}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\diag_{n}X_{\bullet}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\diag_{n-1}Y_{\bullet}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\diag_{n}Y_{\bullet}}

whose the front and back squares are coCartesian. Applying the pasting law of pushouts iteratively, we find that
\varphi
is a cobase change of the map (
5.1.3
), which is a cofibration. We therefore get the conclusion.
∎

For any simplicial operad
\mathcal{P}
, the category
\BMod(\mathcal{P})
admits a simplicial model structure transferred from that on the category of symmetric sequences (see [
[
48
]
, Propositions 3.1.5, 3.2.8]). One constructs the Hochschild resolution of
\mathcal{P}
as follows.

Construction 5.1.5
.

Let
\sH_{\bullet}\mathcal{P}:\Delta^{\op}\longrightarrow\BMod(\mathcal{P})
be the simplicial object of
\mathcal{P}
-bimodules with
\sH_{n}\mathcal{P}:=\mathcal{P}^{\circ(n+2)}
, the face map
d_{i}:\sH_{n}\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow\sH_{n-1}\mathcal{P}
given by using the composition
\mu:\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}
to combine the factors
i+1
and
i+2
in
\sH_{n}\mathcal{P}
and with the degeneracy map
s_{i}
given by inserting the unit operations of
\mathcal{P}
between the factors
i+1
and
i+2
. The realization
\diag(\sH_{\bullet}\mathcal{P})\in\BMod(\mathcal{P})
has
n
-simplices being those of
\sH_{n}\mathcal{P}
. The map
\mu
induces a canonical map of simplicial objects
\sH_{\bullet}\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}
. According to [
[
48
]
, Corollary 3.7.6], the induced map
\psi:\diag(\sH_{\bullet}\mathcal{P})\longrightarrow\diag(\mathcal{P})=\mathcal{P}
is a weak equivalence of
\mathcal{P}
-bimodules, and exhibits
\diag(\sH_{\bullet}\mathcal{P})
as the
Hochschild resolution of

\mathcal{P}\in\BMod(\mathcal{P})
.

On other hand, since
\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P}=\sH_{0}\mathcal{P}
, there is a unique map of simplicial objects
\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow\sH_{\bullet}\mathcal{P}
, which is the identity on degree 0. Now, the diagonal functor determines a map
\rho:\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow\diag(\sH_{\bullet}\mathcal{P})
of
\mathcal{P}
-bimodules. Moreover, the composition
\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P}\overset{\rho}{\longrightarrow}\diag(\sH_{\bullet}\mathcal{P})\overset{\psi}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{P}
agrees with
\mu:\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}
.

Lemma 5.1.6
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{P}
is a
\Sigma
-cofibrant simplicial operad. The map
\psi
exhibits
\diag(\sH_{\bullet}\mathcal{P})
as a cofibrant resolution for
\mathcal{P}\in\BMod(\mathcal{P})
. Moreover, the map
\rho:\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow\diag(\sH_{\bullet}\mathcal{P})
is a cofibration of
\mathcal{P}
-bimodules. In particular,
\diag(\sH_{\bullet}\mathcal{P})
is also a cofibrant resolution for
\mathcal{P}
when regarded as a pointed
\mathcal{P}
-bimodule.

Proof.

For the first statement, it suffices to show that the latching map
\rL_{n-1}\sH_{\bullet}\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow\sH_{n}\mathcal{P}
is a cofibration for every
n\geqslant 0
. To this end, one can follow up the argument as in the proof of [
[
48
]
, Corollary 3.7.6]. Besides that, one will need to make use of the fact that, for two maps
f
and
g
of symmetric sequences in a sufficiently nice symmetric monoidal model category, the pushout-product of
f
with
g
is a cofibration as soon as both of them are one and in addition, the domain of
g
is cofibrant (cf. [
[
18
]
, Lemma 11.5.1]).

To prove that the map
\rho
is a cofibration, we make use of Lemma
5.1.4
. Note that since
\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P}
is considered as a constant simplicial object, the latching map (
5.1.2
) is simply given by the map
\rL_{n-1}\sH_{\bullet}\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow\sH_{n}\mathcal{P}
when
n\geqslant 1
and the identity map
\Id_{\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P}}
when
n=0
.
∎

Remark 5.1.7
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a
\Sigma
-cofibrant simplicial operad and let
A
be any
\mathcal{P}
-algebra. The
Hochschild resolution of

A
is given by the realization of the simplicial
\mathcal{P}
-algebra
\sH^{\mathcal{P}}_{\bullet}A
with
\sH^{\mathcal{P}}_{n}A=\mathcal{P}^{\circ(n+1)}\circ A
. The augmentation map
\diag(\sH^{\mathcal{P}}_{\bullet}A)\longrightarrow A
is a weak equivalence by [
[
48
]
, Corollary 3.7.4], and indeed exhibits
\diag(\sH^{\mathcal{P}}_{\bullet}A)
as a cofibrant resolution for
A
. To see this, one just needs to show that the latching map
\rL_{n-1}\sH^{\mathcal{P}}_{\bullet}A\longrightarrow\sH_{n}^{\mathcal{P}}A
is a cofibration for every
n\geqslant 0
. This is an analogue of the first statement in the above lemma.

Proof of Proposition
5.1.3
.

The idea will be to first argue that it suffices to treat only the case where
\mathcal{P}
is the symmetrization of a discrete free nonsymmetric operad.

Step 1.
Let us first follow the arguments as in [
[
14
]
,
\lx@sectionsign
5]. Applying the functor
\E
to
\sH_{\bullet}\mathcal{P}
degreewise, one obtains a simplicial object
\E\sH_{\bullet}\mathcal{P}
of operads under
\mathcal{P}\sqcup\mathcal{P}
. The realization
\diag(\E\sH_{\bullet}\mathcal{P})
is then an operad under
\mathcal{P}\sqcup\mathcal{P}
. Since
\E(\mathcal{P})\cong\mathcal{P}
in
\Op_{C}(\Set_{\Delta})_{\mathcal{P}\sqcup\mathcal{P}/}
, there is a canonical map
\varphi_{\mathcal{P}}:\diag(\E\sH_{\bullet}\mathcal{P})\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}
of operads under
\mathcal{P}\sqcup\mathcal{P}
.
One observes that there is a canonical isomorphism
\diag(\E\sH_{\bullet}\mathcal{P})\cong\E(\diag(\sH_{\bullet}\mathcal{P}))
of operads under
\mathcal{P}\sqcup\mathcal{P}
and over
\mathcal{P}
(cf. [
[
14
]
, Proposition 5.3]). Since
\E(\diag(\sH_{\bullet}\mathcal{P}))
is a model for
\mathbb{L}\E(\mathcal{P})
by Lemma
5.1.6
, it remains to show that the map
\varphi_{\mathcal{P}}:\diag(\E\sH_{\bullet}\mathcal{P})\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}
is a weak equivalence of operads. Now, observe that
\E\sH_{n}\mathcal{P}\cong\mathcal{P}\sqcup\mathcal{F}_{*}(\mathcal{P}^{\circ n})\sqcup\mathcal{P}
where

\mathcal{F}_{*}:\Coll_{C}(\Set_{\Delta})_{\mathcal{I}_{C}/}\longrightarrow\Op_{C}(\Set_{\Delta})

refers to the free-operad functor on
pointed
C
-collections
. Consequently, if
\mathcal{Q}\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{P}
is a weak equivalence between cofibrant operads then the induced map
\E\sH_{n}\mathcal{Q}\longrightarrow\E\sH_{n}\mathcal{P}
is also a weak equivalence. Therefore, by the
realization lemma
(see e.g., [
[
20
]
,
\lx@sectionsign
4.1]),
\varphi_{\mathcal{Q}}
is a weak equivalence if and only if
\varphi_{\mathcal{P}}
is one. Applying the realization lemma in the other direction, we get that
\varphi_{\mathcal{P}}
is a weak equivalence as soon as the map
\varphi_{\mathcal{P}_{n}}
is one for every
n\geqslant 0
(where
\mathcal{P}_{n}
denotes the operad of
n
-simplices of
\mathcal{P}
).

Now, consider
\mathcal{P}
as an
\textbf{O}_{C}
-algebra with
\textbf{O}_{C}
being the operad of simplicial
C
-colored operads. Remark
5.1.7
suggests that we can make use of
\mathcal{P}^{c}:=\diag(\sH^{\textbf{O}_{C}}_{\bullet}\mathcal{P})
as (another) cofibrant model for
\mathcal{P}
. By the above paragraph, it suffices to verify that the map
\varphi_{\mathcal{P}^{c}}
is a weak equivalence and to this end, we just need to show that the map
\varphi_{\mathcal{P}^{c}_{n}}
is a weak equivalence for every
n
. By construction, we have

\mathcal{P}^{c}_{n}=((\textbf{O}_{C})^{\circ(n+1)}\circ\mathcal{P})_{n}=(\textbf{O}_{C})^{\circ(n+1)}\circ\mathcal{P}_{n}.

(The second identification is because of the fact that
\textbf{O}_{C}
is a discrete operad). In particular,
\mathcal{P}^{c}_{n}
is a discrete free
\textbf{O}_{C}
-algebra. This is in fact equivalent to saying that
\mathcal{P}^{c}_{n}
is the symmetrization of a discrete free nonsymmetric operad.

Step 2.
By the first step, we can assume without loss of generality that
\mathcal{P}
is the symmetrization of a discrete free nonsymmetric operad. Nevertheless, for the remainder we just need to assume that
\mathcal{P}
is the symmetrization of a nonsymmetric operad
\mathcal{Q}
, i.e.,
\mathcal{P}=\Sym(\mathcal{Q})
(see §
2.1
). Observe that the symmetrization functor lifts to the functors

\displaystyle\Sym:\BMod(\mathcal{Q})^{*}\longrightarrow\BMod(\Sym(\mathcal{Q}))^{*},\;\;\text{and}

\displaystyle\Sym:\nsOp_{C}(\Set_{\Delta})_{\mathcal{Q}\sqcup\mathcal{Q}/}\longrightarrow\Op_{C}(\Set_{\Delta})_{\Sym(\mathcal{Q})\sqcup\Sym(\mathcal{Q})/},

which are left adjoints to the associated forgetful functors. Moreover, we have a commutative square of left Quillen functors

\textstyle{\BMod(\mathcal{Q})^{*}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\E\;\;\;\;\;\;}
\scriptstyle{\Sym}
\textstyle{\nsOp_{C}(\Set_{\Delta})_{\mathcal{Q}\sqcup\mathcal{Q}/}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Sym}
\textstyle{\BMod(\Sym(\mathcal{Q}))^{*}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\E\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;}
\textstyle{\Op_{C}(\Set_{\Delta})_{\Sym(\mathcal{Q})\sqcup\Sym(\mathcal{Q})/}}

(for this, it suffices to verify the commutativity of the associated square of right adjoints). According to [
[
14
]
, Proposition 5.4], we have that
\mathbb{L}\E\mathcal{Q}\cong\mathcal{Q}
. Thus, by the commutativity of the above square and by the fact that the symmetrization functor preserves weak equivalences, we obtain the expected identification
\mathbb{L}\E\Sym(\mathcal{Q})\cong\Sym(\mathcal{Q})
.
∎

Let
R
be any commutative ring. Hochschild resolutions work in the context of simplicial
R
-modules under a slightly different setting. Let
\mathcal{P}\in\Op_{C}(\sMod_{R})
be given such that the unit map
\mathcal{I}_{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}
is a cofibration of symmetric sequences. Then the composition

\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow\diag(\sH_{\bullet}\mathcal{P})\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{P}

exhibits
\diag(\sH_{\bullet}\mathcal{P})
as a cofibrant resolution for
\mathcal{P}\in\BMod(\mathcal{P})^{*}
. Moreover, for
A
a levelwise cofibrant
\mathcal{P}
-algebra, the augmentation map
\diag(\sH^{\mathcal{P}}_{\bullet}A)\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}A
exhibits
\diag(\sH^{\mathcal{P}}_{\bullet}A)
as a cofibrant resolution for
A
.

Proposition 5.1.8
.

The category
\sMod_{R}
is good. Namely, for every set of colors
C
and for every cofibrant object
\mathcal{P}\in\Op_{C}(\sMod_{R})
, the left derived functor

\mathbb{L}\E:\BMod(\mathcal{P})^{*}\longrightarrow\Op_{C}(\sMod_{R})_{\mathcal{P}\sqcup\mathcal{P}/}

of
\E
sends
\mathcal{P}
to itself
\mathcal{P}
.

Proof.

As discussed above, we can make use of
\diag(\sH_{\bullet}\mathcal{P})
as a cofibrant resolution for
\mathcal{P}\in\BMod(\mathcal{P})^{*}
and
\diag(\sH^{\textbf{O}_{C}}_{\bullet}\mathcal{P})
as (another) cofibrant model for
\mathcal{P}\in\Op_{C}(\sMod_{R})
. (Here we note that since the operad
\textbf{O}_{C}
is discrete and
\Sigma
-cofibrant, its unit map is indeed a cofibration). Then using the same argument as in the first step of the proof of Proposition
5.1.3
, we may assume that
\mathcal{P}
is the symmetrization of a discrete free nonsymmetric operad, or alternatively,
\mathcal{P}
is the free operad generated by a discrete free symmetric sequence. In particular, there exists a (discrete) cofibrant simplicial operad
\mathcal{Q}\in\Op_{C}(\Set_{\Delta})
such that
R\{\mathcal{Q}\}=\mathcal{P}
where
R\{-\}
signifies the canonical left Quillen functor
\Op_{C}(\Set_{\Delta})\longrightarrow\Op_{C}(\sMod_{R})
. Accordingly, we may complete the proof just by applying Proposition
5.1.3
.
∎

In what follows, we provide additional examples for the goodness hypothesis.

Example 5.1.9
.

Combined with the
operadic Dold-Kan correspondence
obtained in
[
27
]
, the above proposition implies that the category
\mathcal{C}_{\geqslant 0}({\textbf{k}})
of connective dg
k
-modules, with
k
being a commutative ring containing
\mathbb{Q}
(see Example
2.3.7
), is also good.

Examples 5.1.10
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{S}
is good and suppose we are given a symmetric monoidal model category
\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}
, and together with a
weak monoidal Quillen adjunction

\mathcal{F}:\mathcal{S}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}:\sU
(cf.
[
51
]
). Then for every cofibrant operad
\mathcal{P}
in
\mathcal{S}
, it is straightforward to show that
\mathcal{F}(\mathcal{P})
is a good operad in
\widetilde{\mathcal{S}}
as well. Due to this, we may include more examples for the goodness hypothesis. For instance, every
spectral operad
(i.e. an operad enriched over symmetric spectra) that comes from a cofibrant simplicial (or topological) operad is good. Moreover, due to the above example, every operad in
\mathcal{C}({\textbf{k}})
that comes from a cofibrant operad in
\mathcal{C}_{\geqslant 0}({\textbf{k}})
is good as well.

5.2
Cotangent complex of enriched operads

Suppose we are given a base category
\mathcal{S}
that is (at least) sufficient in the sense of
\lx@sectionsign
3
, and let
\mathcal{P}
be a fixed
C
-colored operad in
\mathcal{S}
.

Notations 5.2.1
.

To avoid confusion, in the remainder of this section, we will use the symbols

\bigsqcup\;,\;\;\underset{\bullet}{\bigsqcup}\;,\;\;\underset{\ast}{\bigsqcup}\;\;\;\text{and}\;\;\;\underset{\circ}{\bigsqcup}

to denote the coproduct operations in
\Op(\mathcal{S})
,
\BMod(\mathcal{P})
,
\BMod(\mathcal{P})^{*}
and
\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})
, respectively. On other hand, the pushouts will always be understood from the context.

Notations 5.2.2
.

We let
\rL_{\mathcal{P}}\in\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op(\mathcal{S})
and
\rL_{\mathcal{P}}^{b}\in\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\BMod(\mathcal{P})
respectively denote the
cotangent complexes of

\mathcal{P}
when regarded as an object of
\Op(\mathcal{S})
and
\BMod(\mathcal{P})
. Besides that, we denote by
\rL^{\red}_{\mathcal{P}}\in\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})
the cotangent complex of
\mathcal{P}
when regarded as an object of
\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})
.

Conventions 5.2.3
.

From now on, by
Quillen cohomology of

\mathcal{P}
we will mean the Quillen cohomology of
\mathcal{P}
when regarded as an object of
\Op(\mathcal{S})
, which is therefore classified by
\rL_{\mathcal{P}}
. On other hand, by
reduced Quillen cohomology of

\mathcal{P}
we will mean the Quillen cohomology of
\mathcal{P}
when regarded as an object of
\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})
, which is classified by
\rL^{\red}_{\mathcal{P}}
.

By Theorem
4.4.1
, when
\mathcal{P}
is
\Sigma
-cofibrant, we have a sequence of Quillen equivalences connecting the tangent categories:

\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\IbMod(\mathcal{P})\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\simeq\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\BMod(\mathcal{P})\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\simeq\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\simeq\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op(\mathcal{S}).

Notations 5.2.4
.

We will write
\mathcal{F}^{ib}_{\mathcal{P}}\dashv\mathcal{U}^{ib}_{\mathcal{P}}
for the adjunction
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\IbMod(\mathcal{P})\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op(\mathcal{S})
and write
\mathcal{F}^{b}_{\mathcal{P}}\dashv\mathcal{U}^{b}_{\mathcal{P}}
for the adjunction
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\BMod(\mathcal{P})\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op(\mathcal{S})
.

In order to get the desired formula of Quillen cohomology of
\mathcal{P}
, we will describe the derived image of
\rL_{\mathcal{P}}\in\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op(\mathcal{S})
under the right Quillen equivalence
\mathcal{U}^{ib}_{\mathcal{P}}:\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op(\mathcal{S})\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\IbMod(\mathcal{P})
. As the first step, we will show that the derived image of
\rL_{\mathcal{P}}
in
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\BMod(\mathcal{P})
is up to a shift weakly equivalent to
\rL_{\mathcal{P}}^{b}
. Our result therefore extends [
[
22
]
, Proposition 3.2.1], albeit through a different approach. For our approach, the operad
\mathcal{P}
is technically required to be good in the sense of Definition
5.1.2
. After having done that first step, it remains to compute the derived image of
\rL_{\mathcal{P}}^{b}
in
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\IbMod(\mathcal{P})
.

As discussed above, we first wish to prove the following.

Proposition 5.2.5
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{S}
is abundant (see Conventions
3.0.2
) and
\mathcal{P}
is a good cofibrant operad (see Definition
5.1.2
). Then under the left Quillen equivalence

\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}}^{b}:\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\BMod(\mathcal{P})\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op(\mathcal{S}),

\rL^{b}_{\mathcal{P}}\in\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\BMod(\mathcal{P})
is identified with
\rL_{\mathcal{P}}[1]\in\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op(\mathcal{S})
.

The proof will require several technical lemmas.

Given two
\mathcal{S}
-enriched categories
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{D}
, the tensor product
\mathcal{C}\otimes\mathcal{D}
is by definition the
\mathcal{S}
-enriched category whose set of objects is
\Ob(\mathcal{C}\otimes\mathcal{D}):=\Ob(\mathcal{C})\times\Ob(\mathcal{D})
and such that for every
c,c^{\prime}\in\Ob(\mathcal{C})
and
d,d^{\prime}\in\Ob(\mathcal{D})
we have

\Map_{\mathcal{C}\otimes\mathcal{D}}((c,d),(c^{\prime},d^{\prime})):=\Map_{\mathcal{C}}(c,c^{\prime})\otimes\Map_{\mathcal{D}}(d,d^{\prime}).

Recall that the category of
\mathcal{C}
-bimodules is isomorphic to
\Fun(\mathcal{C}^{\op}\otimes\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S})
the category of
\mathcal{S}
-valued enriched functors on
\mathcal{C}^{\op}\otimes\mathcal{C}
. Under this identification, the functor

\Map_{\mathcal{C}}:\mathcal{C}^{\op}\otimes\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{S},(x,y)\mapsto\Map_{\mathcal{C}}(x,y)

is exactly
\mathcal{C}
viewed as a bimodule over itself.

Lemma 5.2.6
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{S}
is abundant and let
\mathcal{C}\in\Op(\mathcal{S})
be a levelwise cofibrant operad that is concentrated in arity 1. Then there is a weak equivalence
\theta_{\mathcal{C}}:\mathcal{F}^{b}_{\mathcal{C}}(\rL^{b}_{\mathcal{C}})\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\rL_{\mathcal{C}}[1]
in
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{C}}\Op(\mathcal{S})
.

Proof.

We also regard
\mathcal{C}
as an
\mathcal{S}
-enriched category. The proof is then straightforward by observing that the category
\Cat(\mathcal{S})
is already a
``
neighborhood” of
\mathcal{C}
in
\Op(\mathcal{S})
. This idea is expressed as follows. There is a commutative square of left Quillen functors

\textstyle{\mathcal{T}_{\Map_{\mathcal{C}}}\Fun(\mathcal{C}^{\op}\otimes\mathcal{C},\mathcal{S})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{F}^{b}_{\Map_{\mathcal{C}}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{C}}\BMod(\mathcal{C})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{F}^{b}_{\mathcal{C}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{C}}\Cat(\mathcal{S})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{C}}\Op(\mathcal{S})}

The horizontal functors are the obvious embedding functors, which preserve cotangent complexes, while the functor
\mathcal{F}^{b}_{\Map_{\mathcal{C}}}
is the left Quillen equivalence appearing in [
[
22
]
, Theorem 3.1.14]. According to Proposition 3.2.1 of loc. cit., there is a weak equivalence
\overline{\theta}_{\mathcal{C}}:\mathcal{F}^{b}_{\Map_{\mathcal{C}}}(\rL_{\Map_{\mathcal{C}}})\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\rL_{\mathcal{C}}[1]
in
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{C}}\Cat(\mathcal{S})
. Finally, the expected weak equivalence
\theta_{\mathcal{C}}
is given by the image of
\overline{\theta}_{\mathcal{C}}
under the embedding functor
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{C}}\Cat(\mathcal{S})\longrightarrow\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{C}}\Op(\mathcal{S})
.
∎

In what follows, we consider the case where
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{I}_{C}
the initial
C
-colored operad and describe the weak equivalence
\theta_{\mathcal{I}_{C}}:\mathcal{F}^{b}_{\mathcal{I}_{C}}(\rL^{b}_{\mathcal{I}_{C}})\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\rL_{\mathcal{I}_{C}}[1]
of the above lemma. Let us pick up several notations of [
[
22
]
, §3.2]. We denote by
*
the category which has a single object whose endomorphism object is
1_{\mathcal{S}}
. Moreover, let
[1]_{\mathcal{S}}
denote the category with objects
0,1
and mapping spaces
\Map_{[1]_{\mathcal{S}}}(0,1)=1_{\mathcal{S}},\Map_{[1]_{\mathcal{S}}}(1,0)=\emptyset
and
\Map_{[1]_{\mathcal{S}}}(0,0)=\Map_{[1]_{\mathcal{S}}}(1,1)=1_{\mathcal{S}}
. Localizing
[1]_{\mathcal{S}}
at the unique non-trivial morphism
0\rightarrow 1
gives us the category
[1]_{\mathcal{S}}^{\sim}
, which is the same as
[1]_{\mathcal{S}}
except that
\Map_{[1]^{\sim}_{\mathcal{S}}}(1,0)=1_{\mathcal{S}}
. By construction, the canonical map
[1]^{\sim}_{\mathcal{S}}\longrightarrow*
is a weak equivalence. Take a factorization

[1]_{\mathcal{S}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{E}\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}[1]_{\mathcal{S}}^{\sim}

of the canonical map
[1]_{\mathcal{S}}\longrightarrow[1]_{\mathcal{S}}^{\sim}
into a cofibration followed by a trivial fibration. We now obtain a sequence of maps

*\bigsqcup*\longrightarrow[1]_{\mathcal{S}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{E}\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}[1]_{\mathcal{S}}^{\sim}\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}*

such that the first two maps are cofibrations, while the others are weak equivalences. Tensoring with
\mathcal{I}_{C}
(viewed as an
\mathcal{S}
-enriched category) produces a sequence of maps in
\Cat(\mathcal{S})

\mathcal{I}_{C}\bigsqcup\mathcal{I}_{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{I}_{C}\otimes[1]_{\mathcal{S}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{I}_{C}\otimes\mathcal{E}\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{I}_{C}\otimes[1]_{\mathcal{S}}^{\sim}\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{I}_{C}.

The last two maps are weak equivalences, while the others are again cofibrations because
\mathcal{I}_{C}
is discrete.

By the above discussion, the pushout
\mathcal{I}_{C}\,\underset{\mathcal{I}_{C}\sqcup\mathcal{I}_{C}}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{I}_{C}\otimes\mathcal{E}
is a cofibrant model for
\mathcal{I}_{C}\,\underset{\mathcal{I}_{C}\sqcup\mathcal{I}_{C}}{\overset{\h}{\bigsqcup}}\,\mathcal{I}_{C}\in\Op(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{I}_{C}//\mathcal{I}_{C}}
, and hence
\rL_{\mathcal{I}_{C}}[1]
is given by

\rL_{\mathcal{I}_{C}}[1]\overset{\defi}{=}\Sigma^{\infty}(\mathcal{I}_{C}\,\underset{\mathcal{I}_{C}\sqcup\mathcal{I}_{C}}{\overset{\h}{\bigsqcup}}\,\mathcal{I}_{C})\simeq\Sigma^{\infty}(\mathcal{I}_{C}\,\underset{\mathcal{I}_{C}\sqcup\mathcal{I}_{C}}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{I}_{C}\otimes\mathcal{E}).

On the other hand, note that
\BMod(\mathcal{I}_{C})
is isomorphic to
\Coll_{C}(\mathcal{S})
the category of
C
-collections, so we find that
\mathcal{F}^{b}_{\mathcal{I}_{C}}(\rL^{b}_{\mathcal{I}_{C}})=\Sigma^{\infty}(\Fr(\mathcal{I}_{C}))
where
\Fr
is the free functor
\Coll_{C}(\mathcal{S})\longrightarrow\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})
and
\Fr(\mathcal{I}_{C})
is regarded as an object in
\Op(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{I}_{C}//\mathcal{I}_{C}}
. Moreover, we can show that
\Fr(\mathcal{I}_{C})
is the same as the pushout
\mathcal{I}_{C}\underset{\mathcal{I}_{C}\sqcup\mathcal{I}_{C}}{\bigsqcup}\mathcal{I}_{C}\otimes[1]_{\mathcal{S}}
. Finally, we find the expected map

\theta_{\mathcal{I}_{C}}:\mathcal{F}^{b}_{\mathcal{I}_{C}}(\rL^{b}_{\mathcal{I}_{C}})=\Sigma^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{I}_{C}\underset{\mathcal{I}_{C}\sqcup\mathcal{I}_{C}}{\bigsqcup}\mathcal{I}_{C}\otimes[1]_{\mathcal{S}}\right)\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\Sigma^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{I}_{C}\underset{\mathcal{I}_{C}\sqcup\mathcal{I}_{C}}{\bigsqcup}\mathcal{I}_{C}\otimes\mathcal{E}\right)=\rL_{\mathcal{I}_{C}}[1]

(5.2.1)

canonically induced by the map
[1]_{\mathcal{S}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{E}
.

Consider
\mu:\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}
as a map in
\BMod(\mathcal{P})
and the unit map
\eta:\mathcal{I}_{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}
as a map in
\Op(\mathcal{S})
. Recall that
\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}(\mu)
is the image of
\mu
under the left Quillen functor
\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}:\BMod(\mathcal{P})_{/\mathcal{P}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\BMod(\mathcal{P})
. Note that
\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}(\mu)
has already the right type since
\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P}\in\BMod(\mathcal{P})
is cofibrant. Also,
\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}(\eta)
is the image of
\eta
under the left Quillen functor
\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}:\Op(\mathcal{S})_{/\mathcal{P}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op(\mathcal{S})
and has the right type.

Lemma 5.2.7
.

There is a weak equivalence
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}}^{b}(\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}(\mu))\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}(\eta)[1]
in
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op(\mathcal{S})
.

Proof.

The map
\eta:\mathcal{I}_{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}
gives rise to a commutative square of left Quillen functors

\textstyle{\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{I}_{C}}\BMod(\mathcal{I}_{C})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{I}_{C}}^{b}}
\scriptstyle{\eta^{b}_{!}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{I}_{C}}\Op(\mathcal{S})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta^{op}_{!}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\BMod(\mathcal{P})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}}^{b}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op(\mathcal{S})}

(5.2.2)

Let us start with the cotangent complex
\rL^{b}_{\mathcal{I}_{C}}\in\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{I}_{C}}\BMod(\mathcal{I}_{C})
. Note that the functor
\BMod(\mathcal{I}_{C})\longrightarrow\BMod(\mathcal{P})
agrees with the free
\mathcal{P}
-bimodule functor
\Coll_{C}(\mathcal{S})\longrightarrow\BMod(\mathcal{P})
, which sends
\mathcal{I}_{C}
to
\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P}
. Thus we find that the functor
\eta^{b}_{!}
sends
\rL^{b}_{\mathcal{I}_{C}}
to
\Sigma^{\infty}(\mathcal{P}\,\underset{\bullet}{\bigsqcup}\,(\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P}))\in\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\BMod(\mathcal{P})
, which is a model for
\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}(\mu)
. The commutativity of (
5.2.2
) shows that

\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}}^{b}(\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}(\mu))\simeq\eta^{op}_{!}\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{I}_{C}}^{b}(\rL^{b}_{\mathcal{I}_{C}}).

On other hand, by the discussion after Definition
2.5.13
we have
\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}(\eta)[1]=\eta^{op}_{!}(\rL_{\mathcal{I}_{C}}[1])
. Using
\Sigma^{\infty}(\mathcal{I}_{C}\,\underset{\mathcal{I}_{C}\sqcup\mathcal{I}_{C}}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{I}_{C}\otimes\mathcal{E})
as a cofibrant model for
\rL_{\mathcal{I}_{C}}[1]
as discussed above, we find the desired weak equivalence given by

\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}}^{b}(\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}(\mu))=\eta^{op}_{!}\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{I}_{C}}^{b}(\rL^{b}_{\mathcal{I}_{C}})\overset{\eta^{op}_{!}(\theta_{\mathcal{I}_{C}})}{\underset{\simeq}{\xrightarrow{\hskip 36.98866pt}}}\eta^{op}_{!}(\rL_{\mathcal{I}_{C}}[1])=\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}(\eta)[1]

where
\theta_{\mathcal{I}_{C}}
is the weak equivalence (
5.2.1
).
∎

Remark 5.2.8
.

It is necessary to give an explicit description of the map
\eta^{op}_{!}(\theta_{\mathcal{I}_{C}})
. We have that

\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}}^{b}(\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}(\mu))\simeq\eta^{op}_{!}\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{I}_{C}}^{b}(\rL^{b}_{\mathcal{I}_{C}})=\Sigma^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{P}\,\underset{\mathcal{I}_{C}}{\bigsqcup}\,\Fr(\mathcal{I}_{C})\right)=\Sigma^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{P}\,\underset{\mathcal{I}_{C}\sqcup\mathcal{I}_{C}}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{I}_{C}\otimes[1]_{\mathcal{S}}\right),

and

\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}(\eta)[1]=\eta^{op}_{!}(\rL_{\mathcal{I}_{C}}[1])=\Sigma^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{P}\,\underset{\mathcal{I}_{C}}{\bigsqcup}\;(\mathcal{I}_{C}\,\underset{\mathcal{I}_{C}\sqcup\mathcal{I}_{C}}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{I}_{C}\otimes\mathcal{E})\right)=\Sigma^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{P}\,\underset{\mathcal{I}_{C}\sqcup\mathcal{I}_{C}}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{I}_{C}\otimes\mathcal{E}\right).

Under these identifications, the map
\eta^{op}_{!}(\theta_{\mathcal{I}_{C}})
is given by applying
\Sigma^{\infty}
to the map

\mathcal{P}\,\underset{\mathcal{I}_{C}\sqcup\mathcal{I}_{C}}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{I}_{C}\otimes[1]_{\mathcal{S}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}\,\underset{\mathcal{I}_{C}\sqcup\mathcal{I}_{C}}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{I}_{C}\otimes\mathcal{E}

canonically induced by the map
[1]_{\mathcal{S}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{E}
.

Consider the Quillen adjunction
\mathcal{L}^{\Sp}_{\mathcal{P}}:\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op(\mathcal{S}):\mathcal{R}^{\Sp}_{\mathcal{P}}
.

Lemma 5.2.9
.

The left Quillen functor
\mathcal{L}^{\Sp}_{\mathcal{P}}
sends
\rL^{\red}_{\mathcal{P}}\in\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})
to
\rL_{\mathcal{P}/\mathcal{I}_{C}}
the relative cotangent complex of the unit map
\eta:\mathcal{I}_{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}
(cf.
\lx@sectionsign
2.5
).

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{P}^{c}\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{P}
be a cofibrant resolution of
\mathcal{P}
in
\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})
. By Remark
2.5.14
we have that
\rL_{\mathcal{P}/\mathcal{I}_{C}}=\Sigma^{\infty}(\mathcal{P}\,\underset{\mathcal{I}_{C}}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{P}^{c})
. On other hand, by definition the reduced cotangent complex
\rL^{\red}_{\mathcal{P}}\in\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})
is given by
\Sigma^{\infty}(\mathcal{P}\,\underset{\circ}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{P}^{c})
the constant spectrum on
\mathcal{P}\,\underset{\circ}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{P}^{c}\in\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})
considered as a
C
-colored operad over and under
\mathcal{P}
. Moreover, since
\mathcal{I}_{C}
is an initial object in
\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})
, it implies that the coproduct
\mathcal{P}\,\underset{\circ}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{P}^{c}
, when regarded as an object in
\Op(\mathcal{S})
, coincides with the pushout
\mathcal{P}\,\underset{\mathcal{I}_{C}}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{P}^{c}\in\Op(\mathcal{S})
. We hence get the conclusion.
∎

Suppose that
\mathcal{P}
is cofibrant. Then
\rL_{\mathcal{P}/\mathcal{I}_{C}}
is simply given by
\Sigma^{\infty}(\mathcal{P}\,\underset{\mathcal{I}_{C}}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{P})
. Nevertheless, we will need two more models for this. Let
\mathcal{R}
and
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
be in
\Op(\mathcal{S})
with

\mathcal{R}=\mathcal{P}\,\underset{\mathcal{I}_{C}}{\bigsqcup}\,(\mathcal{I}_{C}\otimes[1]_{\mathcal{S}})\;\;\text{and}\;\;\mathcal{R}^{\prime}=\mathcal{P}\,\underset{\mathcal{I}_{C}}{\bigsqcup}\,(\mathcal{I}_{C}\otimes\mathcal{E}).

We then form a diagram of coCartesian squares in
\Op(\mathcal{S})
as follows

\textstyle{\mathcal{I}_{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{0}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{P}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{I}_{C}\,\bigsqcup\,\mathcal{I}_{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{P}\,\bigsqcup\,\mathcal{I}_{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{P}\,\bigsqcup\,\mathcal{P}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{I}_{C}\otimes[1]_{\mathcal{S}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{R}\,\underset{\mathcal{I}_{C}}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{P}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{I}_{C}\otimes\mathcal{E}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\,\underset{\mathcal{I}_{C}}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{P}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\mathcal{I}_{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{P}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{P}\,\underset{\mathcal{I}_{C}}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{P}}

(5.2.3)

The middle column is nothing but the image of the first column through the left adjoint functor
\Op(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{I}_{C}//\mathcal{I}_{C}}\longrightarrow\Op(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{P}//\mathcal{P}}
induced by
\eta:\mathcal{I}_{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}
. We consider
\mathcal{R}
and
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
as objects in
\Op(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{P}//\mathcal{P}}
via that way. The three squares on the right hand side are considered as coCartesian squares in
\Op(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{P}//\mathcal{P}}
. Moreover, note that all the arrows in this diagram are cofibrations, except the three bottom vertical maps, which are all weak equivalences (the last two ones are a homotopy cobase change of the weak equivalence
\mathcal{I}_{C}\otimes\mathcal{E}\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{I}_{C}
).

Lemma 5.2.10
.

The map
\mathcal{R}\longrightarrow\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
induces a weak equivalence of spectrum objects in
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op(\mathcal{S})
:

\theta_{\mathcal{P}/\mathcal{I}_{C}}:\Sigma^{\infty}(\mathcal{R}\,\underset{\mathcal{I}_{C}}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{P})\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\Sigma^{\infty}(\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\,\underset{\mathcal{I}_{C}}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{P}).

Moreover, the two are both weakly equivalent to the relative cotangent complex
\rL_{\mathcal{P}/\mathcal{I}_{C}}
.

Proof.

By the weak equivalence
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\,\underset{\mathcal{I}_{C}}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{P}\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{P}\,\underset{\mathcal{I}_{C}}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{P}
of objects in
\Op(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{P}//\mathcal{P}}
, the constant spectrum
\Sigma^{\infty}(\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\,\underset{\mathcal{I}_{C}}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{P})
is indeed a model for
\rL_{\mathcal{P}/\mathcal{I}_{C}}
. It remains to show that
\theta_{\mathcal{P}/\mathcal{I}_{C}}
is a weak equivalence. For this, we will follow the argument as in the proof of [
[
22
]
, Proposition 3.2.1]. It hence suffices to show that the map
\mathcal{R}\,\underset{\mathcal{I}_{C}}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\,\underset{\mathcal{I}_{C}}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{P}
is (-1)-cotruncated in
\Op(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{P}//\mathcal{P}}
. Since the latter is a homotopy cobase change in
\Op(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{P}//\mathcal{P}}
of the map
\mathcal{R}\longrightarrow\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
, we just need to show that
\mathcal{R}\longrightarrow\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
is (-1)-cotruncated in
\Op(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{P}//\mathcal{P}}
. Moreover, since the map
\mathcal{R}\longrightarrow\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
agrees with the image of the map
\mathcal{I}_{C}\otimes[1]_{\mathcal{S}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{I}_{C}\otimes\mathcal{E}
through the left Quillen functor
\Op(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{I}_{C}//\mathcal{I}_{C}}\longrightarrow\Op(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{P}//\mathcal{P}}
, the proof will be completed after showing that the latter map is (-1)-cotruncated in
\Op(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{I}_{C}//\mathcal{I}_{C}}
. For this, it suffices to show that the fold map

\mathcal{I}_{C}\otimes\mathcal{E}\,\underset{\mathcal{I}_{C}\otimes[1]_{\mathcal{S}}}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{I}_{C}\otimes\mathcal{E}\longrightarrow\mathcal{I}_{C}\otimes\mathcal{E}

is a weak equivalence in
\Op(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{I}_{C}//\mathcal{I}_{C}}
or in
\Cat(\mathcal{S})
, alternatively. This follows by the fact that the fold map
\mathcal{E}\,\underset{[1]_{\mathcal{S}}}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{E}\longrightarrow\mathcal{E}
is a weak equivalence in
\Cat(\mathcal{S})
, due to the convention that
\mathcal{S}
satisfies the invertibility hypothesis (cf.
\lx@sectionsign
3
).
∎

We will denote by
\rL^{b}_{\mathcal{P}/\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P}}\in\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\BMod(\mathcal{P})
the relative cotangent complex of the map
\mu:\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}
regarded as a map in
\BMod(\mathcal{P})
. Take a factorization
\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}^{b}\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{P}
in
\BMod(\mathcal{P})
of the map
\mu
into a cofibration followed by a weak equivalence. By Remark
2.5.14
we have that
\Sigma^{\infty}(\mathcal{P}\underset{\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P}}{\bigsqcup}\mathcal{P}^{b})
is a (cofibrant) model for
\rL^{b}_{\mathcal{P}/\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P}}
. The following lemma illustrates how we can make use of the goodness hypothesis.

Lemma 5.2.11
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{P}
is cofibrant and is good in the sense of Definition
5.1.2
. Then the left Quillen functor
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}}^{b}:\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\BMod(\mathcal{P})\longrightarrow\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op(\mathcal{S})
sends
\rL^{b}_{\mathcal{P}/\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P}}\in\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\BMod(\mathcal{P})
to
\rL_{\mathcal{P}/\mathcal{I}_{C}}[1]
.

Proof.

We first describe the image of
\rL^{b}_{\mathcal{P}/\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P}}
through the functor
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\BMod(\mathcal{P})\longrightarrow\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})
. It will suffice to describe the image of
\mathcal{P}\underset{\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P}}{\bigsqcup}\mathcal{P}^{b}
through the functor
\BMod(\mathcal{P})_{\mathcal{P}//\mathcal{P}}\longrightarrow\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{P}//\mathcal{P}}
, which is the same as the composed functor

\BMod(\mathcal{P})_{\mathcal{P}//\mathcal{P}}\cong\BMod(\mathcal{P})^{*}_{\mathcal{P}//\mathcal{P}}\overset{\widetilde{\E}}{\longrightarrow}(\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{P}\,\underset{\circ}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{P}/})_{\mathcal{P}//\mathcal{P}}\cong\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{P}//\mathcal{P}}

where
\widetilde{\E}
is the functor induced by
\E:\BMod(\mathcal{P})^{*}\longrightarrow\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{P}\,\underset{\circ}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{P}/}
(see
\lx@sectionsign
5.1
). Note that the pushout
\mathcal{P}\underset{\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P}}{\bigsqcup}\mathcal{P}^{b}
, when regarded as an object in
\BMod(\mathcal{P})^{*}
, is exactly
\mathcal{P}\,\underset{\ast}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{P}^{b}
the coproduct of
\mathcal{P}
with
\mathcal{P}^{b}
as pointed
\mathcal{P}
-bimodules. Denote by
\mathcal{Q}\in\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{P}\,\underset{\circ}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{P}/}
the image of
\mathcal{P}^{b}
under
\E
. So the underlying
C
-colored operad of
\E(\mathcal{P}\,\underset{\ast}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{P}^{b})
is given by the pushout
\mathcal{Q}\,\underset{\mathcal{P}\,\underset{\circ}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{P}}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{P}
. Since
\mathcal{P}
is good, it implies that
\mathcal{Q}
is a cofibrant resolution of
\mathcal{P}
when considered as an object in
\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{P}\,\underset{\circ}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{P}/}
and hence, the latter pushout is a model for
\Sigma(\mathcal{P}\,\underset{\circ}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{P})
the suspension of
\mathcal{P}\,\underset{\circ}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{P}
considered as an object in
\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{P}//\mathcal{P}}
. Thus we find that the derived image of
\rL^{b}_{\mathcal{P}/\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P}}
in
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})
is given by
\Sigma^{\infty}(\Sigma(\mathcal{P}\,\underset{\circ}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{P}))
, which is nothing but
\rL^{\red}_{\mathcal{P}}[1]
the suspension of
\rL^{\red}_{\mathcal{P}}
. Finally, we deduce by using Lemma
5.2.9
, which proves that the derived image of
\rL^{\red}_{\mathcal{P}}[1]
through the functor
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})\longrightarrow\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op(\mathcal{S})
is exactly
\rL_{\mathcal{P}/\mathcal{I}_{C}}[1]
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
5.2.5
.

By the definition of relative cotangent complex, we have two cofiber sequences in
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op(\mathcal{S})
:

\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}}^{b}(\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}(\mu))\longrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}}^{b}(\rL^{b}_{\mathcal{P}})\longrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}}^{b}(\rL^{b}_{\mathcal{P}/\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P}})\;\;\;\text{and}\;\;\;\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}(\eta)\longrightarrow\rL_{\mathcal{P}}\longrightarrow\rL_{\mathcal{P}/\mathcal{I}_{C}}.

Let us consider the corresponding shifted cofiber sequences:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}}^{b}(\rL^{b}_{\mathcal{P}/\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P}})[-1]\overset{\alpha}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}}^{b}(\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}(\mu))\longrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}}^{b}(\rL^{b}_{\mathcal{P}}),\;\;\text{and}

\displaystyle\rL_{\mathcal{P}/\mathcal{I}_{C}}\overset{\beta}{\longrightarrow}\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}(\eta)[1]\longrightarrow\rL_{\mathcal{P}}[1].

The map
\alpha
is described as follows. From Remark
5.2.8
we have that

\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}}^{b}(\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}(\mu))\simeq\Sigma^{\infty}(\mathcal{P}\,\underset{\mathcal{I}_{C}\sqcup\mathcal{I}_{C}}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{I}_{C}\otimes[1]_{\mathcal{S}}).

On other hand, by Lemma
5.2.11
we have an identification
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}}^{b}(\rL^{b}_{\mathcal{P}/\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P}})[-1]\simeq\rL_{\mathcal{P}/\mathcal{I}_{C}}
. From Lemma
5.2.10
, we have two models for
\rL_{\mathcal{P}/\mathcal{I}_{C}}
given by
\Sigma^{\infty}(\mathcal{R}\,\underset{\mathcal{I}_{C}}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{P})
and
\Sigma^{\infty}(\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\,\underset{\mathcal{I}_{C}}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{P})
. We will exhibit the first one as a model for
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}}^{b}(\rL^{b}_{\mathcal{P}/\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P}})[-1]
:

\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}}^{b}(\rL^{b}_{\mathcal{P}/\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P}})[-1]\simeq\Sigma^{\infty}(\mathcal{R}\,\underset{\mathcal{I}_{C}}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{P})=\Sigma^{\infty}\left((\mathcal{P}\,\bigsqcup\,\mathcal{P})\underset{\mathcal{I}_{C}\sqcup\mathcal{I}_{C}}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{I}_{C}\otimes[1]_{\mathcal{S}}\right).

Then by construction,
\alpha
is given by the map between constant spectra

\alpha:\Sigma^{\infty}\left((\mathcal{P}\,\bigsqcup\,\mathcal{P})\underset{\mathcal{I}_{C}\sqcup\mathcal{I}_{C}}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{I}_{C}\otimes[1]_{\mathcal{S}}\right)\longrightarrow\Sigma^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{P}\,\underset{\mathcal{I}_{C}\sqcup\mathcal{I}_{C}}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{I}_{C}\otimes[1]_{\mathcal{S}}\right)

canonically induced by the fold map
\mathcal{P}\,\bigsqcup\,\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}
.

We now describe the map
\beta
. Again by Remark
5.2.8
, we have
\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}(\eta)[1]\simeq\Sigma^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{P}\,\underset{\mathcal{I}_{C}\sqcup\mathcal{I}_{C}}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{I}_{C}\otimes\mathcal{E}\right)
. Furthermore, when using
\Sigma^{\infty}(\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\,\underset{\mathcal{I}_{C}}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{P})=\Sigma^{\infty}\left((\mathcal{P}\,\bigsqcup\,\mathcal{P})\underset{\mathcal{I}_{C}\sqcup\mathcal{I}_{C}}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{I}_{C}\otimes\mathcal{E}\right)
as a model for
\rL_{\mathcal{P}/\mathcal{I}_{C}}
, then by construction
\beta
is given by the map between constant spectra

\beta:\Sigma^{\infty}\left((\mathcal{P}\,\bigsqcup\,\mathcal{P})\underset{\mathcal{I}_{C}\sqcup\mathcal{I}_{C}}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{I}_{C}\otimes\mathcal{E}\right)\longrightarrow\Sigma^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{P}\,\underset{\mathcal{I}_{C}\sqcup\mathcal{I}_{C}}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{I}_{C}\otimes\mathcal{E}\right)

canonically induced by the fold map
\mathcal{P}\,\bigsqcup\,\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}
again.

We now obtain a commutative square in
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op(\mathcal{S})

\textstyle{\Sigma^{\infty}\left((\mathcal{P}\,\bigsqcup\,\mathcal{P})\underset{\mathcal{I}_{C}\sqcup\mathcal{I}_{C}}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{I}_{C}\otimes[1]_{\mathcal{S}}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\;\;\;\;\;\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\theta_{\mathcal{P}/\mathcal{I}_{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\Sigma^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{P}\,\underset{\mathcal{I}_{C}\sqcup\mathcal{I}_{C}}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{I}_{C}\otimes[1]_{\mathcal{S}}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\eta^{op}_{!}(\theta_{\mathcal{I}_{C}})}
\textstyle{\Sigma^{\infty}\left((\mathcal{P}\,\bigsqcup\,\mathcal{P})\underset{\mathcal{I}_{C}\sqcup\mathcal{I}_{C}}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{I}_{C}\otimes\mathcal{E}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\;\;\;\;\beta}
\textstyle{\Sigma^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{P}\,\underset{\mathcal{I}_{C}\sqcup\mathcal{I}_{C}}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{I}_{C}\otimes\mathcal{E}\right)}

in which the left and right vertical maps are the weak equivalences of lemmas
5.2.10
and
5.2.7
, respectively. It hence induces a weak equivalence between homotopy cofibers of the maps
\alpha
and
\beta
. We thus obtain a natural weak equivalence
\theta_{\mathcal{P}}:{\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}}^{b}}(\rL^{b}_{\mathcal{P}})\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\rL_{\mathcal{P}}[1]
in
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op(\mathcal{S})
as expected.
∎

In the next step, we will describe the cotangent complex
\rL^{b}_{\mathcal{P}}\in\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\BMod(\mathcal{P})
. We will need the following lemma.

Lemma 5.2.12
.

The forgetful functor
\sU:\BMod(\mathcal{P})\longrightarrow\LMod(\mathcal{P})
is a left Quillen functor provided that
\mathcal{P}
is
\Sigma
-cofibrant.

Proof.

The functor
\sU
is given by forgetting the right
\mathcal{P}
-action, which is linear. It implies that
\sU
preserves colimits. By the adjoint functor theorem and by the combinatoriality of
\mathcal{S}
,
\sU
is indeed a left adjoint.

Since
\sU
preserves weak equivalences, the proof will be completed after showing that it preserves cofibrations. To this end, we first prove that every cofibration in
\RMod(\mathcal{P})
has underlying map in
\Coll_{C}(\mathcal{S})
being a cofibration as well. Observe that the model structure on
\RMod(\mathcal{P})
admits a set of generating cofibrations given by
\{i\circ\mathcal{P}:M\circ\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow N\circ\mathcal{P}\}_{i}
where
i:M\rightarrow N
ranges over the set of those of
\Coll_{C}(\mathcal{S})
. Since the forgetful functor
\RMod(\mathcal{P})\longrightarrow\Coll_{C}(\mathcal{S})
is a left adjoint, it suffices to show that each map
i\circ\mathcal{P}:M\circ\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow N\circ\mathcal{P}
is a cofibration in
\Coll_{C}(\mathcal{S})
. Let
\emptyset_{C}
denote the initial
C
-collection, which agrees with
\emptyset_{\mathcal{S}}
on every level. Then, factor the map
i\circ\mathcal{P}
as

M\circ\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow N\circ\emptyset_{C}\underset{M\circ\emptyset_{C}}{\bigsqcup}M\circ\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow N\circ\mathcal{P}.

Since
i
is a cofibration, the map
i\circ\emptyset_{C}:M\circ\emptyset_{C}\longrightarrow N\circ\emptyset_{C}
is a cofibration as well. So the first map of the above composition is a cofibration. The second map is also a cofibration by [
[
18
]
, Lemma 11.5.1], along with the
\Sigma
-cofibrancy of
\mathcal{P}
. We just showed that
i\circ\mathcal{P}
is indeed a cofibration.

It can be shown that the model structure on
\BMod(\mathcal{P})
admits a set of generating cofibrations given by
\{\mathcal{P}\circ j:\mathcal{P}\circ K\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}\circ L\}_{j}
where
j:K\rightarrow L
ranges over the set of those of
\RMod(\mathcal{P})
. It therefore suffices to show that each map
\mathcal{P}\circ j:\mathcal{P}\circ K\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}\circ L
is a cofibration in
\LMod(\mathcal{P})
. This is now clear since
\mathcal{P}\circ(-)
represents the free left
\mathcal{P}
-module functor
\Coll_{C}(\mathcal{S})\longrightarrow\LMod(\mathcal{P})
, which is a left Quillen functor, and since
j
is a cofibration in
\Coll_{C}(\mathcal{S})
as indicated above.
∎

Suppose that
\mathcal{P}
is
\Sigma
-cofibrant. Properly, a model for
\rL^{b}_{\mathcal{P}}
is given by
\Sigma^{\infty}(\mathcal{P}\,\underset{\bullet}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{P}^{b})
with
\mathcal{P}^{b}
being a cofibrant resolution of
\mathcal{P}
in
\BMod(\mathcal{P})
. But the map
\mathcal{P}\,\underset{\bullet}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{P}^{b}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}\,\underset{\bullet}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{P}
is a weak equivalence of
\mathcal{P}
-bimodules due to Lemma
5.2.12
, so we may exhibit
\Sigma^{\infty}(\mathcal{P}\,\underset{\bullet}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{P})
as a model for
\rL^{b}_{\mathcal{P}}
. According to [
[
24
]
, Corollary 2.3.3],
\rL^{b}_{\mathcal{P}}=\Sigma^{\infty}(\mathcal{P}\,\underset{\bullet}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{P})
admits a
suspension spectrum replacement
simply given by fixing
\mathcal{P}\,\underset{\bullet}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{P}
as its value at the bidegree
(0,0)
, and hence the value at the bidegree
(n,n)
is given by
\Sigma^{n}(\mathcal{P}\,\underset{\bullet}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{P})
the
n
-suspension of
\mathcal{P}\,\underset{\bullet}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{P}
in
\BMod(\mathcal{P})_{\mathcal{P}//\mathcal{P}}
. We would like to exhibit this suspension spectrum as a model for
\rL^{b}_{\mathcal{P}}
and to describe the
\mathcal{P}
-bimodule
\Sigma^{n}(\mathcal{P}\,\underset{\bullet}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{P})
for every
n\geqslant 0
.

Notations 5.2.13
.

(i) For each
n\geqslant 0
, we denote by
\sS^{n}:=\Sigma^{n}(1_{\mathcal{S}}\sqcup 1_{\mathcal{S}})\in\mathcal{S}
with the suspension
\Sigma(-)
taken in
\mathcal{S}_{1_{\mathcal{S}}//1_{\mathcal{S}}}
, and refer to
\sS^{n}
as the
pointed

n
-sphere
in
\mathcal{S}
.

(ii) Furthermore, we write
\sS_{C}^{n}
for the
C
-collection which has
\sS_{C}^{n}(c;c)=\sS^{n}
for every
c\in C
and agrees with
\emptyset_{\mathcal{S}}
on the other levels. Note that for every
n\geqslant 0
,
\sS_{C}^{n}
is weakly equivalent to
\Sigma^{n}(\sS_{C}^{0})
the
n
-suspension of
\sS_{C}^{0}\in\Coll_{C}(\mathcal{S})_{\mathcal{I}_{C}//\mathcal{I}_{C}}
.

We can now describe
\Sigma^{n}(\mathcal{P}\,\underset{\bullet}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{P})
. By Lemma
5.2.12
, the underlying left
\mathcal{P}
-module of
\Sigma^{n}(\mathcal{P}\,\underset{\bullet}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{P})
is weakly equivalent to

\Sigma^{n}(\mathcal{P}\,\underset{\diamond}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{P})\in\LMod(\mathcal{P})_{\mathcal{P}//\mathcal{P}}
where
``\underset{\diamond}{\bigsqcup}
” refers to the coproduct operation in
\LMod(\mathcal{P})
. The good thing is that
\mathcal{P}
is free (generated by
\mathcal{I}_{C}
) as a left module over itself. So we get that
\mathcal{P}\,\underset{\diamond}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{P}\in\LMod(\mathcal{P})
is isomorphic to
\mathcal{P}\circ\sS_{C}^{0}
the free left
\mathcal{P}
-module generated by
\sS_{C}^{0}
. We have further that

\Sigma^{n}(\mathcal{P}\,\underset{\diamond}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{P})\simeq\Sigma^{n}(\mathcal{P}\circ\sS_{C}^{0})\simeq\mathcal{P}\circ\sS_{C}^{n}.

In particular, for each
C
-sequence
\overline{c}:=(c_{1},\cdots,c_{m};c)
, we have

\Sigma^{n}(\mathcal{P}\,\underset{\diamond}{\bigsqcup}\,\mathcal{P})\,(\overline{c})\simeq\mathcal{P}\circ\sS_{C}^{n}\,(\overline{c})=\mathcal{P}(\overline{c})\otimes(\sS^{n})^{\otimes m}.

Notations 5.2.14
.

(i) We denote by
\widetilde{\rL}_{\mathcal{P}}\in\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\IbMod(\mathcal{P})
the derived image of
\rL^{b}_{\mathcal{P}}\in\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\BMod(\mathcal{P})
under the right Quillen equivalence
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\BMod(\mathcal{P})\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\IbMod(\mathcal{P})
(cf. Theorem
4.4.1
).

(ii) Furthermore, recall from Theorem
4.4.3
that when
\mathcal{S}
is stably sufficient, we have a sequence of right Quillen equivalences

\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\IbMod(\mathcal{P})\overset{\Omega^{\infty}}{\underset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}}\IbMod(\mathcal{P})_{\mathcal{P}//\mathcal{P}}\overset{\ker}{\underset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}}\IbMod(\mathcal{P}).

In this situation, we will denote by
\overline{\rL}_{\mathcal{P}}:=\mathbb{R}(\ker\circ\,\Omega^{\infty})(\widetilde{\rL}_{\mathcal{P}})
.

In what follows, we give the explicit descriptions of
\widetilde{\rL}_{\mathcal{P}}
and
\overline{\rL}_{\mathcal{P}}
.

\bullet
It is not hard to see that the Quillen adjunction
\IbMod(\mathcal{P})_{\mathcal{P}//\mathcal{P}}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\BMod(\mathcal{P})_{\mathcal{P}//\mathcal{P}}
is differentiable (see Definition
3.0.1
). Thus by [
[
24
]
, Corollary 2.4.8],
\widetilde{\rL}_{\mathcal{P}}
is simply given by the underlying prespectrum of infinitesimal
\mathcal{P}
-bimodules of
\rL^{b}_{\mathcal{P}}
, and so we get
(\widetilde{\rL}_{\mathcal{P}})_{n,n}\simeq\mathcal{P}\circ\sS_{C}^{n}
for every
n\geqslant 0
.

\bullet
Suppose further that
\mathcal{S}
is stably sufficient. By [
[
24
]
, Remark 2.4.7], we have a weak equivalence
\mathbb{R}\Omega^{\infty}(\widetilde{\rL}_{\mathcal{P}})\simeq\hocolim_{n}\Omega^{n}(\widetilde{\rL}_{\mathcal{P}})_{n,n}
. Moreover, we find that

\mathbb{R}(\ker\circ\,\Omega^{\infty})(\widetilde{\rL}_{\mathcal{P}})\simeq(\hocolim_{n}\Omega^{n}(\mathcal{P}\circ\sS_{C}^{n}))\overset{\h}{\times_{\mathcal{P}}}0\simeq\hocolim_{n}\Omega^{n}[\,(\mathcal{P}\circ\sS_{C}^{n})\overset{\h}{\times_{\mathcal{P}}}0\,]

in which the last desuspension
\Omega(-)
is taken in
\IbMod(\mathcal{P})
. More explicitly, for each
\overline{c}=(c_{1},\cdots,c_{m};c)
we find that

\displaystyle\mathbb{R}(\ker\circ\,\Omega^{\infty})(\widetilde{\rL}_{\mathcal{P}})\,(\overline{c})

\displaystyle\simeq\hocolim_{n}\Omega^{n}[\,(\mathcal{P}(\overline{c})\otimes(\sS^{n})^{\otimes m})\overset{\h}{\times_{\mathcal{P}(\overline{c})}}0\,]

\displaystyle\simeq\mathcal{P}(\overline{c})\otimes\hocolim_{n}\Omega^{n}[\,(\sS^{n})^{\otimes m}\overset{\h}{\times_{1_{\mathcal{S}}}}0\,]

in which the last desuspension
\Omega(-)
is now taken in
\mathcal{S}
. Here the second weak equivalence is because of the fact that homotopy pullbacks in
\mathcal{S}
are also homotopy pushouts and that the functor
\mathcal{P}(\overline{c})\otimes(-)
preserves colimits.

Combining the above computations with Proposition
5.2.5
, we obtain the following statement.

Theorem 5.2.15
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{S}
is abundant and that
\mathcal{P}
is a good cofibrant operad. Then the right Quillen equivalence

\mathcal{U}^{ib}_{\mathcal{P}}:\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op(\mathcal{S})\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\IbMod(\mathcal{P})

identifies the cotangent complex
\rL_{\mathcal{P}}
to
\widetilde{\rL}_{\mathcal{P}}[-1]\in\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\IbMod(\mathcal{P})
in which
\widetilde{\rL}_{\mathcal{P}}
is the prespectrum with
(\widetilde{\rL}_{\mathcal{P}})_{n,n}=\mathcal{P}\circ\sS_{C}^{n}
for
n\geq 0
. When
\mathcal{S}
is in addition stably abundant, then under the right Quillen equivalence

\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op(\mathcal{S})\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\IbMod(\mathcal{P}),

the cotangent complex
\rL_{\mathcal{P}}
is identified with
\overline{\rL}_{\mathcal{P}}[-1]
with
\overline{\rL}_{\mathcal{P}}\in\IbMod(\mathcal{P})
being given by

\overline{\rL}_{\mathcal{P}}(\overline{c})=\mathcal{P}(\overline{c})\otimes\hocolim_{n}\Omega^{n}[\,(\sS^{n})^{\otimes m}\times_{1_{\mathcal{S}}}^{\h}0\,]

for each
C
-sequence
\overline{c}:=(c_{1},\cdots,c_{m};c)
.

By the definition of Quillen cohomology groups
2.5.15
, we obtain the following conclusion.

Corollary 5.2.16
.

Suppose we are given an object
M\in\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\IbMod(\mathcal{P})
. Under the same assumptions as in Theorem
5.2.15
, the
n
’th Quillen cohomology group of
\mathcal{P}
with coefficients in
M
is computed by the formula

\sH^{n}_{Q}(\mathcal{P};M)\cong\pi_{0}\Map^{\h}_{\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\IbMod(\mathcal{P})}(\widetilde{\rL}_{\mathcal{P}}[-1],M[n])\cong\pi_{0}\Map^{\h}_{\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\IbMod(\mathcal{P})}(\widetilde{\rL}_{\mathcal{P}},M[n+1]).

Suppose further that
\mathcal{S}
is stably abundant. For a given object
M\in\IbMod(\mathcal{P})
, the
n
’th Quillen cohomology group of
\mathcal{P}
with coefficients in
M
is given by

\sH^{n}_{Q}(\mathcal{P};M)\cong\pi_{0}\Map^{\h}_{\IbMod(\mathcal{P})}(\overline{\rL}_{\mathcal{P}}[-1],M[n])\cong\pi_{0}\Map^{\h}_{\IbMod(\mathcal{P})}(\overline{\rL}_{\mathcal{P}},M[n+1]).

Remark 5.2.17
.

Suppose that the base category
\mathcal{S}
is abundant, and in addition, is good in the sense of Definition
5.1.2
. Then Proposition
5.2.5
remains valid for any operad
\mathcal{P}
that is
\Sigma
-cofibrant. Indeed, we first take
f:\mathcal{Q}\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{P}
to be a cofibrant resolution of
\mathcal{P}
in
\Op(\mathcal{S})
such that
f
is a map in
\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})
(cf. Observations
4.1.1
). In particular,
\mathcal{Q}
is good. The map
f
gives rise to a commutative square of left Quillen equivalences

\textstyle{\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{Q}}\BMod(\mathcal{Q})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{Q}}^{b}}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{Q}}\Op(\mathcal{S})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\BMod(\mathcal{P})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}}^{b}}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op(\mathcal{S})}

(5.2.4)

It is then not hard to see that the vertical functors preserve cotangent complexes and thus, the statement of Proposition
5.2.5
holds for
\mathcal{P}
. Consequently, Theorem
5.2.15
and moreover, all the statements in the next subsection hold for
\mathcal{P}
as well.

5.3
Comparison between Quillen cohomology and reduced Quillen cohomology

Let
\mathcal{S}
and
\mathcal{P}
be as in the statement of Theorem
5.2.15
. The unit map
\eta:\mathcal{I}_{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}
gives rise to the Quillen adjunctions

\displaystyle\eta_{!}^{ib}:\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{I}_{C}}\IbMod(\mathcal{I}_{C})\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\IbMod(\mathcal{P}):\eta^{*}_{ib},\;\;\text{and}

\displaystyle\eta_{!}^{op}:\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{I}_{C}}\Op(\mathcal{S})\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op(\mathcal{S}):\eta^{*}_{op}.

Moreover, there is a commutative diagram of Quillen adjunctions of the form
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{I}_{C}}\IbMod(\mathcal{I}_{C})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{I}_{C}}\Op(\mathcal{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\IbMod(\mathcal{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op(\mathcal{S})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{I}_{C}}^{ib}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{!}^{ib}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\perp}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{U}_{\mathcal{I}_{C}}^{ib}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{!}^{op}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}^{ib}_{\mathcal{P}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta^{*}_{ib}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\downvdash}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{U}^{ib}_{\mathcal{P}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdash}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta^{*}_{op}}

The following is an analogue of [
[
22
]
, Corollary 3.2.9].

Lemma 5.3.1
.

There is a homotopy cofiber sequence in
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\IbMod(\mathcal{P})
of the form

\mathbb{R}\mathcal{U}^{ib}_{\mathcal{P},C}(\rL_{\mathcal{P}}^{\red})\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}\eta_{!}^{ib}\,(\widetilde{\rL}_{\mathcal{I}_{C}})\longrightarrow\widetilde{\rL}_{\mathcal{P}}

(5.3.1)

where
\mathcal{U}^{ib}_{\mathcal{P},C}
is the right Quillen equivalence
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op_{C}(\mathcal{S})\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\IbMod(\mathcal{P})
appearing in Theorem
4.4.1
.

Proof.

Theorem
5.2.15
proves the existence of weak equivalences
\widetilde{\theta}_{\mathcal{I}_{C}}:\widetilde{\rL}_{\mathcal{I}_{C}}[-1]\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{R}\mathcal{U}_{\mathcal{I}_{C}}^{ib}(\rL_{\mathcal{I}_{C}})
and
\widetilde{\theta}_{\mathcal{P}}:\widetilde{\rL}_{\mathcal{P}}[-1]\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{R}\mathcal{U}^{ib}_{\mathcal{P}}(\rL_{\mathcal{P}})
in
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{I}_{C}}\IbMod(\mathcal{I}_{C})
and
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\IbMod(\mathcal{P})
, respectively. Applying
\mathbb{L}\eta_{!}^{op}
to
\widetilde{\theta}_{\mathcal{I}_{C}}^{ad}:\mathbb{L}\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{I}_{C}}^{ib}\,(\widetilde{\rL}_{\mathcal{I}_{C}})\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\rL_{\mathcal{I}_{C}}[1]
(i.e., the adjoint of
\widetilde{\theta}_{\mathcal{I}_{C}}
), and taking then the adjoint of the result, we obtain a weak equivalence in
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\IbMod(\mathcal{P})
of the form
\mathbb{L}\eta_{!}^{ib}\,(\widetilde{\rL}_{\mathcal{I}_{C}})\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{R}\mathcal{U}^{ib}_{\mathcal{P}}\,\mathbb{L}\eta_{!}^{op}(\rL_{\mathcal{I}_{C}}[1])
.

On other hand, by the definition of relative cotangent complex, there is a cofiber sequence in
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op(\mathcal{S})
of the form

\rL_{\mathcal{P}/\mathcal{I}_{C}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}\eta_{!}^{op}(\rL_{\mathcal{I}_{C}})[1]\longrightarrow\rL_{\mathcal{P}}[1].

By applying
\mathbb{R}\mathcal{U}^{ib}_{\mathcal{P}}
to the latter and by the first paragraph, we get a cofiber sequence in
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\IbMod(\mathcal{P})
:

\mathbb{R}\mathcal{U}^{ib}_{\mathcal{P}}(\rL_{\mathcal{P}/\mathcal{I}_{C}})\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}\eta_{!}^{ib}\,(\widetilde{\rL}_{\mathcal{I}_{C}})\longrightarrow\widetilde{\rL}_{\mathcal{P}}.

Lemma
5.2.9
shows that there is a weak equivalence
\mathbb{R}\mathcal{U}^{ib}_{\mathcal{P},C}(\rL_{\mathcal{P}}^{\red})\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{R}\mathcal{U}^{ib}_{\mathcal{P}}(\rL_{\mathcal{P}/\mathcal{I}_{C}})
in
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\IbMod(\mathcal{P})
. So we get the desired cofiber sequence.
∎

We end this section with the following statement.

Theorem 5.3.2
.

Given an object
M\in\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\IbMod(\mathcal{P})
, there is a long exact sequence of abelian groups of the form

\cdots\longrightarrow\sH^{n-1}_{Q}(\mathcal{P};M)\longrightarrow\sH^{n}_{Q,r}(\mathcal{P};M)\longrightarrow\sH^{n}_{Q,\red}(\mathcal{P};M)\longrightarrow\sH^{n}_{Q}(\mathcal{P};M)\longrightarrow\sH^{n+1}_{Q,r}(\mathcal{P};M)\longrightarrow\cdots

where
\sH^{\bullet}_{Q,r}(\mathcal{P};-)
refers to Quillen cohomology group of
\mathcal{P}
when regarded as a right module over itself, while
\sH^{\bullet}_{Q}(\mathcal{P};-)
refers to Quillen cohomology group of
\mathcal{P}
and
\sH^{\bullet}_{Q,\red}(\mathcal{P};-)
refers to reduced Quillen cohomology group of
\mathcal{P}
(cf. Conventions
5.2.3
).

Proof.

In the cofiber sequence of Lemma
5.3.1
, by notation
\mathbb{R}\mathcal{U}^{ib}_{\mathcal{P},C}(\rL_{\mathcal{P}}^{\red})
classifies the reduced Quillen cohomology of
\mathcal{P}
, while
\widetilde{\rL}_{\mathcal{P}}
classifies the Quillen cohomology of
\mathcal{P}
by Corollary
5.2.16
. This cofiber sequence will hence give rise to the desired long exact sequence after having that
\mathbb{L}\eta_{!}^{ib}\,(\widetilde{\rL}_{\mathcal{I}_{C}})
classifies the Quillen cohomology of
\mathcal{P}
when regarded as a right module over itself. To see this, we first consider the Quillen adjunction
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\RMod(\mathcal{P})\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\IbMod(\mathcal{P})
which is induced by the free-forgetful adjunction
\RMod(\mathcal{P})\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\IbMod(\mathcal{P})
. We denote by
\rL^{r}_{\mathcal{P}}\in\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\RMod(\mathcal{P})
the cotangent complex of
\mathcal{P}
when regarded as a right module over itself. It will suffice to prove that the derived image of
\rL^{r}_{\mathcal{P}}
in
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\IbMod(\mathcal{P})
is weakly equivalent to
\mathbb{L}\eta_{!}^{ib}\,(\widetilde{\rL}_{\mathcal{I}_{C}})
. For this last claim, observe first that
\eta_{!}^{ib}
is the same as the functor
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{I}_{C}}\Coll_{C}(\mathcal{S})\longrightarrow\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\IbMod(\mathcal{P})
induced by the free functor
\Coll_{C}(\mathcal{S})\longrightarrow\IbMod(\mathcal{P})
. Moreover, under the identification
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{I}_{C}}\IbMod(\mathcal{I}_{C})\simeq\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{I}_{C}}\Coll_{C}(\mathcal{S})
, the object
\widetilde{\rL}_{\mathcal{I}_{C}}
is nothing but the cotangent complex of
\mathcal{I}_{C}
when regarded as an object of
\Coll_{C}(\mathcal{S})
, whose derived image through the left Quillen functor
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{I}_{C}}\Coll_{C}(\mathcal{S})\longrightarrow\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\RMod(\mathcal{P})
is exactly
\rL^{r}_{\mathcal{P}}
. The proof is therefore completed.
∎

Remark 5.3.3
.

The gap between reduced Quillen cohomology and Quillen cohomology of
\mathcal{P}
is really accessible because the abelian groups
\sH^{\bullet}_{Q,r}(\mathcal{P};-)
are computable. Indeed, when considering
\mathcal{P}
as a right module over itself, it is free generated by
\mathcal{I}_{C}
. So we have a canonical isomorphism

\sH^{n}_{Q,r}(\mathcal{P};M)\cong\sH^{n}_{Q,col}(\mathcal{I}_{C};\overline{M})

where the right hand side is the
n
’th Quillen cohomology group of
\mathcal{I}_{C}
regarded as a
C
-collection, with coefficients in the derived image of
M
in
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{I}_{C}}\Coll_{C}(\mathcal{S})
. When
\mathcal{S}
is further stably abundant, the cotangent complex of
\mathcal{I}_{C}
is identified with
\mathcal{I}_{C}
itself under the right Quillen equivalence
\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{I}_{C}}\Coll_{C}(\mathcal{S})\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\Coll_{C}(\mathcal{S})
(cf. Theorem
4.4.3
). Therefore, in this situation we have that

\displaystyle\sH^{n}_{Q,col}(\mathcal{I}_{C};\overline{M})

\displaystyle\cong\pi_{0}\Map^{\h}_{\Coll_{C}(\mathcal{S})}(\mathcal{I}_{C},\mathbb{R}\ker\Omega^{\infty}(\Sigma^{n}\overline{M}))

\displaystyle\cong\bigsqcap_{c\in C}\pi_{0}\Map^{\h}_{\mathcal{S}}(1_{\mathcal{S}},\Sigma^{n}(\overline{M}(c;c)\times^{\h}_{1_{\mathcal{S}}}0)).

6
Quillen cohomology of simplicial operads

The category
\Set_{\Delta}
of simplicial sets, together with the standard (Kan-Quillen) model structure, is abundant in the sense of Conventions
3.0.2
, and moreover, is good in the sense of Definition
5.1.2
(cf. Example
2.3.7
and Proposition
5.1.3
). So we may inherit the results of
\lx@sectionsign
5.2
for
\Sigma
-cofibrant simplicial operads (see also Remark
5.2.17
).

In the first subsection, we revisit the
straightening
and
unstraightening
constructions, according to [
[
35
]
, §2.2.1]. Given a simplicial (co)presheaf
\mathcal{F}
over a simplicial category
\mathcal{C}
, the unstraightening of
\mathcal{F}
is in particular a simplicial set over
\sN\mathcal{C}
. We shall first describe the simplices of the unstraightening of
\mathcal{F}
, and then describe its
spaces of right morphisms
.

According to the work of Y. Harpaz, J. Nuiten and M. Prasma (
[
22
]
), the cotangent complex of an
\infty
-category (or a fibrant simplicial category) can be represented as a spectrum valued functor on its
twisted arrow
\infty
-category
(see Theorem
1.2.6
). In the second subsection, we introduce the construction of twisted arrow
\infty
-categories of simplicial operads. For a fibrant simplicial operad
\mathcal{P}
, the twisted arrow
\infty
-category
\Tw(\mathcal{P})
is given by the unstraightening of the simplicial copresheaf
\mathcal{P}:\textbf{Ib}^{\mathcal{P}}\longrightarrow\Set_{\Delta}
, which encodes the data of
\mathcal{P}
as an infinitesimal bimodule over itself (see
\lx@sectionsign
2.2
).

In the last subsection, for the main purpose we will show that the cotangent complex of a simplicial operad can be represented as a spectrum valued functor on its twisted arrow
\infty
-category. The cotangent complex of the little discs operads will be also particularly regarded.

6.1
Unstraightening of simplicial (co)presheaves

We denote by
\mathfrak{C}[-]:\Set_{\Delta}\longrightarrow\Cat(\Set_{\Delta})
the
rigidification functor
, which is left adjoint to the simplicial nerve functor
\sN
. Recall by definition that
\mathfrak{C}[\Delta^{n}]
is the simplicial category whose set of objects is
[n]=\{0,\cdots,n\}
and whose mapping spaces are given by
\Map_{\mathfrak{C}[\Delta^{n}]}(i,j):=\sN\rP_{i,j}
the nerve of the poset

\rP_{i,j}=\{A\,|\,\{i,j\}\subseteq A\subseteq[i,j]\},

with
[i,j]:=\{i,i+1,\cdots,j\}
. (In particular,
\Map_{\mathfrak{C}[\Delta^{n}]}(i,j)=\emptyset
when
i>j
). The composition in
\mathfrak{C}[\Delta^{n}]
is induced by the maps
\rP_{i,j}\times\rP_{j,k}\longrightarrow\rP_{i,k}
taking
(A,B)
to
A\cup B
.

Depending if one wants to
``
unstraighten” simplicial presheaves or copresheaves, one would need to use the
contravariant
or
covariant unstraightening
functor, respectively. Suppose we are given a simplicial set
S
and a simplicial category
\mathcal{C}
. Let
\phi:\mathfrak{C}[S]\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}
be a simplicial functor. For each simplicial set
X
over
S
, one takes two simplicial categories

\mathcal{M}_{X}^{\triangleleft}:=\mathfrak{C}[X^{\triangleleft}]\underset{\mathfrak{C}[X]}{\bigsqcup}\mathcal{C}\;\;\;\text{and}\;\;\;\mathcal{M}_{X}^{\triangleright}:=\mathfrak{C}[X^{\triangleright}]\underset{\mathfrak{C}[X]}{\bigsqcup}\mathcal{C}

where
X^{\triangleleft}
and
X^{\triangleright}
are respectively the left and right cones of
X
. We will always use the letter
``v
” for the cone point, for both left and right cones.

The
covariant straightening functor
associated to
\phi

\St_{\phi}^{\triangleleft}:(\Set_{\Delta})_{/S}\longrightarrow\Fun(\mathcal{C},\Set_{\Delta})

is defined by sending each simplicial set
X
over
S
to the functor
\Map_{\mathcal{M}_{X}^{\triangleleft}}(v,-):\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\Set_{\Delta}
. The functor
\St_{\phi}^{\triangleleft}
admits a right adjoint denoted by
\Un_{\phi}^{\triangleleft}
and called the
covariant unstraightening functor
. In fact, the adjunction
\St_{\phi}^{\triangleleft}\dashv\Un_{\phi}^{\triangleleft}
forms a Quillen adjunction when one endows the category
\Fun(\mathcal{C},\Set_{\Delta})
with the projective model structure and
(\Set_{\Delta})_{/S}
with the
covariant model structure
. Moreover, this adjunction is a Quillen equivalence as long as
\phi
is a weak equivalence of simplicial categories. (See
[
35
]
, Chapter 3 for more details).

Dually, the
contravariant straightening functor
associated to
\phi

\St_{\phi}^{\triangleright}:(\Set_{\Delta})_{/S}\longrightarrow\Fun(\mathcal{C}^{\op},\Set_{\Delta})

is defined by sending each simplicial set
X
over
S
to the functor
\Map_{\mathcal{M}_{X}^{\triangleright}}(-,v):\mathcal{C}^{\op}\longrightarrow\Set_{\Delta}
. We denote by
\Un_{\phi}^{\triangleright}
the right adjoint of
\St_{\phi}^{\triangleright}
and refer to it as the
contravariant unstraightening functor
. The adjunction
\St_{\phi}^{\triangleright}\dashv\Un_{\phi}^{\triangleright}
forms a Quillen adjunction when one endows the category
\Fun(\mathcal{C}^{\op},\Set_{\Delta})
with the projective model structure and
(\Set_{\Delta})_{/S}
with the
contravariant model structure
, and moreover,
\St_{\phi}^{\triangleright}\dashv\Un_{\phi}^{\triangleright}
becomes a Quillen equivalence if
\phi
is a weak equivalence.

Notations 6.1.1
.

(i) We are mainly interested in the case where
S=\sN\mathcal{C}
and
\phi
is given by the counit map
\varepsilon_{\mathcal{C}}:\mathfrak{C}[\sN\mathcal{C}]\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}
. The corresponding covariant (resp. contravariant) straightening-unstraightening adjunction will be then denoted by
\St_{\mathcal{C}}^{\triangleleft}\dashv\Un_{\mathcal{C}}^{\triangleleft}
(resp.
\St_{\mathcal{C}}^{\triangleright}\dashv\Un_{\mathcal{C}}^{\triangleright}
).

(ii) When
\mathcal{C}=\mathfrak{C}[S]
and
\phi
is the identity functor, the corresponding covariant (resp. contravariant) straightening-unstraightening adjunction will be denoted by
\St_{S}^{\triangleleft}\dashv\Un_{S}^{\triangleleft}
(resp.
\St_{S}^{\triangleright}\dashv\Un_{S}^{\triangleright}
).

Remark 6.1.2
.

Note that
\Un_{\mathcal{C}}^{\triangleleft}
is the same as the composed functor

\Fun(\mathcal{C},\Set_{\Delta})\overset{\varepsilon_{\mathcal{C}}^{*}}{\xrightarrow{\hskip 19.91684pt}}\Fun(\mathfrak{C}[\sN\mathcal{C}],\Set_{\Delta})\overset{\Un_{\sN\mathcal{C}}^{\triangleleft}}{\xrightarrow{\hskip 19.91684pt}}(\Set_{\Delta})_{/\sN\mathcal{C}},

(this follows from
[
35
]
, 2.2.1.1(2)). The same statement holds for the contravariant case.

Let
\mathcal{F}:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\Set_{\Delta}
be a simplicial functor. We wish to understand the structure of
\Un_{\mathcal{C}}^{\triangleleft}(\mathcal{F})
as a simplicial set over
\sN\mathcal{C}
. For this, we will follow
[
49
]
, yet in the opposite convention. For each
n\in\mathbb{N}
, one establishes a simplicial functor

\mathfrak{D}^{\triangleleft}_{\Delta^{n}}:\mathfrak{C}[\Delta^{n}]\longrightarrow\Set_{\Delta}

given by sending each
i\in[n]
to
\mathfrak{D}^{\triangleleft}_{\Delta^{n}}(i):=\sN\rP^{\triangleleft}(i)
the nerve of the poset

\rP^{\triangleleft}(i):=\{A\,|\,\{i\}\subseteq A\subseteq[0,i]\,\}.

The structure maps of simplicial functor are defined by applying the union operation of subsets in an obvious way. Moreover, for each map
\delta:\Delta^{m}\longrightarrow\Delta^{n}
, one defines a natural transformation

\mathfrak{D}^{\triangleleft}_{\delta}:\mathfrak{D}^{\triangleleft}_{\Delta^{m}}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{D}^{\triangleleft}_{\Delta^{n}}\circ\mathfrak{C}[\delta]

of the simplicial functors
\mathfrak{C}[\Delta^{m}]\longrightarrow\Set_{\Delta}
given at each
i\in[m]
by the map
\mathfrak{D}^{\triangleleft}_{\delta}(i):\mathfrak{D}^{\triangleleft}_{\Delta^{m}}(i)\longrightarrow\mathfrak{D}^{\triangleleft}_{\Delta^{n}}(\delta i)
which is induced by the map of posets

\rP^{\triangleleft}(i)\longrightarrow\rP^{\triangleleft}(\delta i)\;,\;S\mapsto\delta(S).

Construction 6.1.3
.

The data of an
n
-simplex of
\Un_{\mathcal{C}}^{\triangleleft}(\mathcal{F})
consist of

(i) an
n
-simplex
\varphi\in\sN(\mathcal{C})
, i.e., a functor
\varphi:\mathfrak{C}[\Delta^{n}]\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}
, and

(ii) a natural transformation
t:\mathfrak{D}^{\triangleleft}_{\Delta^{n}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{F}\circ\varphi
between simplicial functors
\mathfrak{C}[\Delta^{n}]\longrightarrow\Set_{\Delta}
.

For each map
\delta:\Delta^{m}\longrightarrow\Delta^{n}
, the corresponding structure map
\Un_{\mathcal{C}}^{\triangleleft}(\mathcal{F})_{n}\longrightarrow\Un_{\mathcal{C}}^{\triangleleft}(\mathcal{F})_{m}
is given by sending each pair
(\varphi,t)\in\Un_{\mathcal{C}}^{\triangleleft}(\mathcal{F})_{n}
to the pair

\left(\mathfrak{C}[\Delta^{m}]\overset{\mathfrak{C}[\delta]}{\xrightarrow{\hskip 19.91684pt}}\mathfrak{C}[\Delta^{n}]\overset{\varphi}{\xrightarrow{\hskip 19.91684pt}}\mathcal{C}\,\;,\,\;\mathfrak{D}^{\triangleleft}_{\Delta^{m}}\overset{\mathfrak{D}^{\triangleleft}_{\delta}}{\xrightarrow{\hskip 19.91684pt}}\mathfrak{D}^{\triangleleft}_{\Delta^{n}}\circ\mathfrak{C}[\delta]\overset{t\circ\Id}{\xrightarrow{\hskip 19.91684pt}}\mathcal{F}\circ\varphi\circ\mathfrak{C}[\delta]\right).

Proof.

Observe that for each map
\varphi_{z}:\Delta^{n}\longrightarrow\sN\mathcal{C}
there is a commutative square of right adjoint functors

\textstyle{\Fun(\mathcal{C},\Set_{\Delta})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\;\;\;\;\;\;\Un_{\mathcal{C}}^{\triangleleft}}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{z}^{*}}
\textstyle{(\Set_{\Delta})_{/\sN\mathcal{C}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{z}^{*}}
\textstyle{\Fun(\mathfrak{C}[\Delta^{n}],\Set_{\Delta})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\Un_{\Delta^{n}}^{\triangleleft}}
\textstyle{(\Set_{\Delta})_{/\Delta^{n}}}

(this follows by combining the two parts of [
[
35
]
, Proposition 2.2.1.1], along with Remark
6.1.2
). This implies the existence of a Cartesian square of the form

\textstyle{\Un^{\triangleleft}_{\Delta^{n}}(\mathcal{F}\circ\varphi_{z})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Delta^{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{z}}
\textstyle{\Un_{\mathcal{C}}^{\triangleleft}(\mathcal{F})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\sN\mathcal{C}.}

So the data of an
n
-simplex
z\in\Un_{\mathcal{C}}^{\triangleleft}(\mathcal{F})_{n}
is equivalent to that of a map
\varphi_{z}:\Delta^{n}\longrightarrow\sN\mathcal{C}
and a section
\Delta^{n}\longrightarrow\Un^{\triangleleft}_{\Delta^{n}}(\mathcal{F}\circ\varphi_{z})
