. By adjunction, the latter is identified with a natural transformation
\St^{\triangleleft}_{\Delta^{n}}(\Id_{\Delta^{n}})\longrightarrow\mathcal{F}\circ\varphi_{z}
. But by definition
\St^{\triangleleft}_{\Delta^{n}}(\Id_{\Delta^{n}})=\Map_{\mathfrak{C}[(\Delta^{n})^{\triangleleft}]}(v,-)
. Hence, it remains to establish for each
n
an isomorphism

\mathfrak{D}^{\triangleleft}_{\Delta^{n}}\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\Map_{\mathfrak{C}[(\Delta^{n})^{\triangleleft}]}(v,-)

between simplicial functors
\mathfrak{C}[\Delta^{n}]\longrightarrow\Set_{\Delta}
compatible with simplicial maps
\Delta^{m}\longrightarrow\Delta^{n}
. For this, note that under the identification
(\Delta^{n})^{\triangleleft}\cong\Delta^{n+1}
, the functor
\Map_{\mathfrak{C}[(\Delta^{n})^{\triangleleft}]}(v,-)
is isomorphic to the functor
[n]\ni i\mapsto\Map_{\mathfrak{C}[\Delta^{n+1}]}(0,i+1)=\sN\rP_{0,i+1}
. For each
i\in[n]
, we define an isomorphism of posets

\rP^{\triangleleft}(i)\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\rP_{0,i+1}\;\;,\;\;A\mapsto\{0\}\sqcup(A+1)

with
A+1:=\{a+1\,|\,a\in A\}
. This indeed gives us the desired isomorphism
\mathfrak{D}^{\triangleleft}_{\Delta^{n}}\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\Map_{\mathfrak{C}[(\Delta^{n})^{\triangleleft}]}(v,-)
.
∎

Remark 6.1.4
.

Unwinding definition, each vertex of
\Un_{\mathcal{C}}^{\triangleleft}(\mathcal{F})
is the choice of an object
x\in\Ob(\mathcal{C})
and a vertex
\mu\in\mathcal{F}(x)
. Let
x,y
be two objects of
\mathcal{C}
. The data of an edge of
\Un_{\mathcal{C}}^{\triangleleft}(\mathcal{F})
from
\mu\in\mathcal{F}(x)
to
\nu\in\mathcal{F}(y)
consist of a vertex
\alpha\in\Map_{\mathcal{C}}(x,y)
and an edge of
\mathcal{F}(y)
of the form
t:\nu\longrightarrow\alpha^{*}(\mu)
where
\alpha^{*}:\mathcal{F}(x)\longrightarrow\mathcal{F}(y)
is the map induced by the simplicial functor structure of
\mathcal{F}:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\Set_{\Delta}
.

Let
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}:\mathcal{C}^{\op}\longrightarrow\Set_{\Delta}
be a simplicial functor. Similarly as in the covariant case, one can get an explicit description of
\Un_{\mathcal{C}}^{\triangleright}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
the contravariant unstraightening of
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
. For each
n\in\mathbb{N}
, one defines a simplicial functor

\mathfrak{D}^{\triangleright}_{\Delta^{n}}:\mathfrak{C}[\Delta^{n}]^{\op}\longrightarrow\Set_{\Delta}

taking each
i\in[n]
to
\mathfrak{D}^{\triangleright}_{\Delta^{n}}(i):=\sN\rP_{n}^{\triangleright}(i)
the nerve of the poset

\rP_{n}^{\triangleright}(i):=\{A\,|\,\{i\}\subseteq A\subseteq[i,n]\,\}.

For each map
\delta:\Delta^{m}\longrightarrow\Delta^{n}
, there is also a natural transformation
\mathfrak{D}^{\triangleright}_{\delta}:\mathfrak{D}^{\triangleright}_{\Delta^{m}}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{D}^{\triangleright}_{\Delta^{n}}\circ\mathfrak{C}[\delta]^{\op}
of the simplicial functors
\mathfrak{C}[\Delta^{m}]^{\op}\longrightarrow\Set_{\Delta}
. The structure maps are defined naturally as well as in the covariant case (see Construction
6.1.3
).

Construction 6.1.5
.

The data of an
n
-simplex of
\Un_{\mathcal{C}}^{\triangleright}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
consist of

(i) an
n
-simplex
\varphi\in\sN(\mathcal{C})
, i.e., a functor
\varphi:\mathfrak{C}[\Delta^{n}]\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}
, and

(ii) a natural transformation
t:\mathfrak{D}^{\triangleright}_{\Delta^{n}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\circ\varphi^{\op}
between simplicial functors
\mathfrak{C}[\Delta^{n}]^{\op}\longrightarrow\Set_{\Delta}
.

The simplicial structure maps of
\Un_{\mathcal{C}}^{\triangleright}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})
are induced by the natural transformations
\mathfrak{D}^{\triangleright}_{\delta}
mentioned above. (See also
[
49
]
).

When
\mathcal{C}
is the terminal category
*\in\Cat(\Set_{\Delta})
, we obtain the self-adjunctions on
\Set_{\Delta}
:

\St_{*}^{\triangleleft}:\Set_{\Delta}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\Set_{\Delta}:\Un_{*}^{\triangleleft}\;\;\;\text{and}\;\;\;\St_{*}^{\triangleright}:\Set_{\Delta}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\Set_{\Delta}:\Un_{*}^{\triangleright}.

Note that
\St_{*}^{\triangleright}\dashv\Un_{*}^{\triangleright}
agrees with the adjunction
|-|_{Q^{\bullet}}\dashv\Sing_{Q^{\bullet}}
of [
[
35
]
, §2.2.2].

Remark 6.1.6
.

For a simplicial set
X
, there is a natural map
X\longrightarrow\Un_{*}^{\triangleright}(X)
, which is in fact a weak equivalence as long as
X
is a Kan complex (see [
[
35
]
, 2.2.2.7, 2.2.2.8]). On other hand, it can be shown that there exists a natural isomorphism
\Un_{*}^{\triangleleft}(X)\cong\Un_{*}^{\triangleright}(X)^{\op}
. So we get a natural map
X^{\op}\longrightarrow\Un_{*}^{\triangleleft}(X)
, which is a weak equivalence when
X
is Kan. In other words, while the (derived) contravariant unstraightening
\Un_{*}^{\triangleright}
is weakly equivalent to the identity functor, the covariant one
\Un_{*}^{\triangleleft}
is weakly equivalent to the opposite functor
X\mapsto X^{\op}
.

We will need the following in the last subsection.

Remark 6.1.7
.

As in [
[
35
]
, Remark 2.2.2.11], for a given object
x\in\mathcal{C}
, there is a canonical isomorphism

\Un_{\mathcal{C}}^{\triangleleft}(\mathcal{F})\times_{\sN\mathcal{C}}\{x\}\cong\Un_{*}^{\triangleleft}(\mathcal{F}(x)).

This follows by the compatibility of the unstraightening functor with taking base change along the map
\{x\}\longrightarrow\sN\mathcal{C}
. Combining with Remark
6.1.6
, we get a weak equivalence

\mathcal{F}(x)^{\op}\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\Un_{\mathcal{C}}^{\triangleleft}(\mathcal{F})\times_{\sN\mathcal{C}}\{x\}

(6.1.1)

whenever
\mathcal{F}(x)
is a Kan complex.

Let
x,y\in\Ob(\mathcal{C})
be two objects of
\mathcal{C}
. Suppose we are given two vertices
\mu\in\mathcal{F}(x)
and
\nu\in\mathcal{F}(y)
regarded as vertices of
\Un_{\mathcal{C}}^{\triangleleft}(\mathcal{F})
(see Remark
6.1.4
). We wish to give a convenient model for
\textrm{Hom}^{\rR}_{\Un_{\mathcal{C}}^{\triangleleft}(\mathcal{F})}(\mu,\nu)
the space of right morphisms from
\mu
to
\nu
in
\Un_{\mathcal{C}}^{\triangleleft}(\mathcal{F})
. Recall by definition that an
n
-simplex of
\textrm{Hom}^{\rR}_{\Un_{\mathcal{C}}^{\triangleleft}(\mathcal{F})}(\mu,\nu)
is an
(n+1)
-simplex
T:\Delta^{n+1}\longrightarrow\Un_{\mathcal{C}}^{\triangleleft}(\mathcal{F})
of
\Un_{\mathcal{C}}^{\triangleleft}(\mathcal{F})
such that
d_{n+1}T
is degenerate on
\mu
and
{\left.\kern-1.2ptT\vphantom{\big|}\right|_{\Delta^{\{n+1\}}}}=\nu
. According to Construction
6.1.3
, the data of
T
consist of:

\bullet
An
(n+1)
-simplex
H:\Delta^{n+1}\longrightarrow\sN\mathcal{C}
satisfying that
d_{n+1}H
is degenerate on
x
and
{\left.\kern-1.2ptH\vphantom{\big|}\right|_{\Delta^{\{n+1\}}}}=y
. In other words,
H
is nothing but an
n
-simplex of
\textrm{Hom}^{\rR}_{\sN\mathcal{C}}(x,y)
.

\bullet
In addition, a natural transformation
t:\mathfrak{D}^{\triangleleft}_{\Delta^{n+1}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{F}\circ H
between simplicial functors
\mathfrak{C}[\Delta^{n+1}]\longrightarrow\Set_{\Delta}
satisfying that for every
i\in\{0,\cdots,n\}
the map

t(i):\mathfrak{D}^{\triangleleft}_{\Delta^{n+1}}(i)\longrightarrow(\mathcal{F}\circ H)(i)=\mathcal{F}(x)

collapses its domain to the vertex
\mu
and that the initial vertex of
t(n+1)
agrees with
\nu
. (Note that
t(n+1)
performs an
(n+1)
-cube in
\mathcal{F}(y)
).

Let us now denote by
\rho_{\mu}
the composed map

\Map_{\mathcal{C}}(x,y)\overset{\mathcal{F}}{\longrightarrow}\Map_{\Set_{\Delta}}(\mathcal{F}(x),\mathcal{F}(y))\overset{ev_{\mu}}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{F}(y)

with
ev_{\mu}
being the evaluation at
\mu
. By [
[
35
]
, 2.2.2.13], there is an isomorphism
\textrm{Hom}^{\rR}_{\sN\mathcal{C}}(x,y)\cong\Un_{*}^{\triangleright}\Map_{\mathcal{C}}(x,y)
. So we can form a canonical map

\textrm{Hom}^{\rR}_{\sN\mathcal{C}}(x,y)\cong\Un_{*}^{\triangleright}\Map_{\mathcal{C}}(x,y)\overset{\Un_{*}^{\triangleright}(\rho_{\mu})}{\xrightarrow{\hskip 28.45274pt}}\Un_{*}^{\triangleright}\mathcal{F}(y)\;.

(6.1.2)

Lemma 6.1.8
.

There is a canonical isomorphism

\textrm{Hom}^{\rR}_{\Un_{\mathcal{C}}^{\triangleleft}(\mathcal{F})}(\mu,\nu)\cong(\Un_{*}^{\triangleright}\mathcal{F}(y))_{\nu/}\times_{\Un_{*}^{\triangleright}\mathcal{F}(y)}\textrm{Hom}^{\rR}_{\sN\mathcal{C}}(x,y)=:\mathbb{P}_{\mu,\nu}.

Proof.

The proof is straightforward using the above analyses.
∎

Suppose that both
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{F}:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\Set_{\Delta}
are fibrant. Then the map
\Un_{\mathcal{C}}^{\triangleleft}(\mathcal{F})\longrightarrow\sN\mathcal{C}
is a left fibration and
\sN\mathcal{C}
is an
\infty
-category. So
\Un_{\mathcal{C}}^{\triangleleft}(\mathcal{F})
is an
\infty
-category as well. Our main interest in this subsection is as follows.

Proposition 6.1.9
.

Let
x,y\in\Ob(\mathcal{C})
be two objects of
\mathcal{C}
. Given two vertices
\mu\in\mathcal{F}(x)
and
\nu\in\mathcal{F}(y)
regarded as vertices of
\Un_{\mathcal{C}}^{\triangleleft}(\mathcal{F})
, there is a homotopy equivalence

\{\nu\}\times_{\mathcal{F}(y)}^{\h}\Map_{\mathcal{C}}(x,y)\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\Map_{\Un_{\mathcal{C}}^{\triangleleft}(\mathcal{F})}(\mu,\nu).

Proof.

We make use of the pullback
\mathcal{F}(y)_{\nu/}\times_{\mathcal{F}(y)}\Map_{\mathcal{C}}(x,y)
as a model for the left hand side. Since
\mathcal{F}(y)
and
\Map_{\mathcal{C}}(x,y)
are Kan complexes, that pullback is Kan as well. By Remark
6.1.6
there is a weak equivalence

\mathcal{F}(y)_{\nu/}\times_{\mathcal{F}(y)}\Map_{\mathcal{C}}(x,y)\longrightarrow\Un_{*}^{\triangleright}(\mathcal{F}(y)_{\nu/})\times_{\Un_{*}^{\triangleright}\mathcal{F}(y)}\Un_{*}^{\triangleright}\Map_{\mathcal{C}}(x,y).

Furthermore, there is a canonical map

\Un_{*}^{\triangleright}(\mathcal{F}(y)_{\nu/})\times_{\Un_{*}^{\triangleright}\mathcal{F}(y)}\Un_{*}^{\triangleright}\Map_{\mathcal{C}}(x,y)\longrightarrow\mathbb{P}_{\mu,\nu}

induced by the canonical embedding
\Un_{*}^{\triangleright}(\mathcal{F}(y)_{\nu/})\longrightarrow(\Un_{*}^{\triangleright}\mathcal{F}(y))_{\nu/}
. The above map is also a weak equivalence, since
\Un_{*}^{\triangleright}(\mathcal{F}(y)_{\nu/})
and
(\Un_{*}^{\triangleright}\mathcal{F}(y))_{\nu/}
are both contractible. We deduce then by using Lemma
6.1.8
.
∎

6.2
Twisted arrow
\infty
-categories of simplicial operads

Let
\mathcal{E}
be an ordinary category. The
(covariant) twisted arrow category

\Tw(\mathcal{E})
is by definition the category whose objects are the morphisms of
\mathcal{E}
and such that maps from
f:x\rightarrow y
to
f^{\prime}:x^{\prime}\rightarrow y^{\prime}
are given by commutative diagrams of the form

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}

(6.2.1)

This classical notion was generalized into the
\infty
-categorical framework due to Lurie [
[
37
]
, §5.2.1]. For an
\infty
-category
\mathcal{D}
, the twisted arrow
\infty
-category
\Tw(\mathcal{D})
is the
\infty
-category whose
n
-simplices are the
(2n+1)
-simplices of
\mathcal{D}
. In particular, objects of
\Tw(\mathcal{D})
are the morphisms of
\mathcal{D}
. A map from
f:x\rightarrow y
to
f^{\prime}:x^{\prime}\rightarrow y^{\prime}
can be also depicted as a diagram of the type (
6.2.1
) commutative up to a chosen homotopy. Furthermore, the twisted arrow
\infty
-category of a fibrant simplicial category
\mathcal{C}
is simply given by
\Tw(\mathcal{C}):=\Tw(\sN\mathcal{C})
. As we will see below,
\Tw(\mathcal{C})
can be represented as the unstraightening of a certain simplicial copresheaf. Due to this fact, we propose the construction of
twisted arrow
\infty
-categories of (fibrant) simplicial operads
.

We will need the following notations and conventions:

\bullet
Let
S
be a simplicial set. We denote by
(\Set_{\Delta}^{\cov})_{/S}
the
covariant model category of simplicial sets over

S
whose cofibrations are monomorphisms and whose fibrant objects are left fibrations over
S
. (See [
[
35
]
, §2.1.4] for more details).

\bullet
Let
\mathcal{C}
be a simplicial category. Recall that the category of
\mathcal{C}
-bimodules is equivalent to the category
\Fun(\mathcal{C}^{\op}\times\mathcal{C},\Set_{\Delta})
. We endow the latter with the projective model structure.

\bullet
For the remainder, we consider only the covariant unstraightening construction. So we will simply write
\Un_{\mathcal{C}}(-)
for the covariant unstraightening of simplicial functors
\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\Set_{\Delta}
.

When
\mathcal{C}
is fibrant, according to [
[
37
]
, Proposition 5.2.1.11] the unstraightening functor

\Un_{\mathcal{C}^{\op}\times\mathcal{C}}:\Fun(\mathcal{C}^{\op}\times\mathcal{C},\Set_{\Delta})\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}(\Set_{\Delta}^{\cov})_{/\sN\mathcal{C}^{\op}\times\sN\mathcal{C}},

(6.2.2)

which is a right Quillen equivalence, identifies the functor

\Map_{\mathcal{C}}:\mathcal{C}^{\op}\times\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\Set_{\Delta}\,,\;(x,y)\mapsto\Map_{\mathcal{C}}(x,y)

to
\Tw(\mathcal{C})
. Alternatively, the unstraightening of
\mathcal{C}
(viewed as a bimodule over itself) is exactly a model for
\Tw(\mathcal{C})
.

Let us now fix
\mathcal{P}
to be a fibrant simplicial
C
-colored operad. Recall from
\lx@sectionsign
4.2
that there is a canonical isomorphism
\IbMod(\mathcal{P})\cong\Fun(\textbf{Ib}^{\mathcal{P}},\Set_{\Delta})
between the categories of infinitesimal
\mathcal{P}
-bimodules and simplicial functors
\textbf{Ib}^{\mathcal{P}}\longrightarrow\Set_{\Delta}
. The unstraightening construction gives a right Quillen equivalence

\Un_{\textbf{Ib}^{\mathcal{P}}}:\Fun(\textbf{Ib}^{\mathcal{P}},\Set_{\Delta})\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}(\Set_{\Delta}^{\cov})_{/\sN(\textbf{Ib}^{\mathcal{P}})}.

(6.2.3)

Consider the functor

\mathcal{P}:\textbf{Ib}^{\mathcal{P}}\longrightarrow\Set_{\Delta}\,,\;(c_{1},\cdots,c_{m};c)\mapsto\mathcal{P}(c_{1},\cdots,c_{m};c),

which encodes the data of
\mathcal{P}
as an infinitesimal bimodule over itself. By assumption, we get a left fibration
\Un_{\textbf{Ib}^{\mathcal{P}}}(\mathcal{P})\longrightarrow\sN(\textbf{Ib}^{\mathcal{P}})
. So
\Un_{\textbf{Ib}^{\mathcal{P}}}(\mathcal{P})
is in particular an
\infty
-category.

Definition 6.2.1
.

The
twisted arrow
\infty
-category of

\mathcal{P}
is given by

\Tw(\mathcal{P}):=\Un_{\textbf{Ib}^{\mathcal{P}}}(\mathcal{P})

the unstraightening of the simplicial functor
\mathcal{P}:\textbf{Ib}^{\mathcal{P}}\longrightarrow\Set_{\Delta}
or alternatively, the unstraightening of
\mathcal{P}
regarded as an infinitesimal bimodule over itself.

Proposition 6.2.2
.

The construction
\Tw(-)
determines a functor from fibrant simplicial operads to
\infty
-categories. Moreover, this functor sends Dwyer-Kan equivalences to
\infty
-categorical equivalences.

Proof.

Let
f:\mathcal{P}\rightarrow\mathcal{Q}
be a map between fibrant simplicial operads. By the compatibility of the unstraightening functor with taking base change along
f:\mathcal{P}\rightarrow\mathcal{Q}
, we obtain a Cartesian square of simplicial sets

\textstyle{\Un_{\textbf{Ib}^{\mathcal{P}}}(f^{*}\mathcal{Q})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Un_{\textbf{Ib}^{\mathcal{Q}}}(\mathcal{Q})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\sN(\textbf{Ib}^{\mathcal{P}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\sN(\textbf{Ib}^{\mathcal{Q}})}

in which
f^{*}\mathcal{Q}
is considered as an infinitesimal
\mathcal{P}
-bimodule (under
\mathcal{P}
) with the structure induced by
f
. Note that this square is also homotopy Cartesian with respect to the Joyal model structure, due to the fact that the right vertical map is a left fibration. The corresponding map
\Tw(f):\Tw(\mathcal{P})\longrightarrow\Tw(\mathcal{Q})
is then given by the composition

\Un_{\textbf{Ib}^{\mathcal{P}}}(\mathcal{P})\longrightarrow\Un_{\textbf{Ib}^{\mathcal{P}}}(f^{*}\mathcal{Q})\longrightarrow\Un_{\textbf{Ib}^{\mathcal{Q}}}(\mathcal{Q}).

Suppose that
f:\mathcal{P}\rightarrow\mathcal{Q}
is a Dwyer-Kan equivalence. Since
f
is in particular a levelwise weak equivalence, it implies that the map
\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow f^{*}\mathcal{Q}
is a weak equivalence of infinitesimal
\mathcal{P}
-bimodules and thus, the map
\Un_{\textbf{Ib}^{\mathcal{P}}}(\mathcal{P})\longrightarrow\Un_{\textbf{Ib}^{\mathcal{P}}}(f^{*}\mathcal{Q})
is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories. On other hand, it is not hard to verify that the induced map
\textbf{Ib}^{f}:\textbf{Ib}^{\mathcal{P}}\longrightarrow\textbf{Ib}^{\mathcal{Q}}
is a Dwyer-Kan equivalence between (fibrant) simplicial categories. So the map
\sN(\textbf{Ib}^{\mathcal{P}})\longrightarrow\sN(\textbf{Ib}^{\mathcal{Q}})
is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories. Combined with the first paragraph, it implies that the map
\Un_{\textbf{Ib}^{\mathcal{P}}}(f^{*}\mathcal{Q})\longrightarrow\Un_{\textbf{Ib}^{\mathcal{Q}}}(\mathcal{Q})
is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories as well. We hence deduce that
\Tw(f):\Tw(\mathcal{P})\longrightarrow\Tw(\mathcal{Q})
is an equivalence.
∎

Due to the previous subsection, we can describe the simplicial structure of
\Tw(\mathcal{P})
as follows.

Construction 6.2.3
.

The data of an
n
-simplex of
\Tw(\mathcal{P})
consist of

(i) an
n
-simplex
\varphi\in\sN(\textbf{Ib}^{\mathcal{P}})
, i.e., a functor
\varphi:\mathfrak{C}[\Delta^{n}]\longrightarrow\textbf{Ib}^{\mathcal{P}}
, and

(ii) a natural transformation
t:\mathfrak{D}^{\triangleleft}_{\Delta^{n}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}\circ\varphi
between simplicial functors
\mathfrak{C}[\Delta^{n}]\longrightarrow\Set_{\Delta}
.

For each map
\delta:\Delta^{m}\longrightarrow\Delta^{n}
, the simplicial structure map
\Tw(\mathcal{P})_{n}\longrightarrow\Tw(\mathcal{P})_{m}
is induced by the natural transformation
\mathfrak{D}^{\triangleleft}_{\delta}:\mathfrak{D}^{\triangleleft}_{\Delta^{m}}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{D}^{\triangleleft}_{\Delta^{n}}\circ\mathfrak{C}[\delta]
, as in Construction
6.1.3
.

Remark 6.2.4
.

Objects of
\Tw(\mathcal{P})
are precisely the
operations
of
\mathcal{P}
(i.e., the vertices of the spaces of operations of
\mathcal{P}
), while morphisms of
\Tw(\mathcal{P})
are given by the
factorizations
. Let
\mu\in\mathcal{P}(c_{1},\cdots,c_{m};c)
and
\nu\in\mathcal{P}(d_{1},\cdots,d_{n};d)
be two operations of
\mathcal{P}
. Denote by
\overline{c}:=(c_{1},\cdots,c_{m};c)
and by
\overline{d}:=(d_{1},\cdots,d_{n};d)
. Explicitly, the data of a morphism
\mu\rightarrow\nu
in
\Tw(\mathcal{P})
consist of

\bullet
a map
f:\langle n\rangle\longrightarrow\langle m\rangle
in
\Fin_{*}
,

\bullet
a tuple of operations
\alpha=(\alpha_{0},\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{m})\in\Map^{f}_{\textbf{Ib}^{\mathcal{P}}}(\overline{c},\overline{d})
, and

\bullet
an edge
t:\nu\longrightarrow\alpha^{*}(\mu)
in
\mathcal{P}(\overline{d})
where
\alpha^{*}:\mathcal{P}(\overline{c})\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}(\overline{d})
is the map determined by the simplicial functor structure of
\mathcal{P}:\textbf{Ib}^{\mathcal{P}}\longrightarrow\Set_{\Delta}
.

It is convenient to depict such a morphism as a diagram of the form

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(c_{1},\cdots,c_{m})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(d_{1},\cdots,d_{n})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(c)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(d)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\alpha_{1}\,\cdots\,\alpha_{m})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{0}}

(6.2.4)

which is commutative up to a chosen homotopy.

Examples 6.2.5
.

When
\mathcal{P}
is discrete then
\Tw(\mathcal{P})
is isomorphic to the nerve of an ordinary category. In this situation, we will identify
\Tw(\mathcal{P})
with the corresponding ordinary category and refer to it as the
twisted arrow category of
\mathcal{P}
. For example, it is not hard to show that the twisted arrow category of the
commutative operad

\Com
is equivalent to
\Fin_{*}^{\op}
. Moreover, the twisted arrow category of the
associative operad

\Ass
is equivalent to the simplex category
\Delta
. We will describe the twisted arrow
\infty
-categories of the little discs operads later.

Remark 6.2.6
.

By construction, there is a canonical left fibration
\Tw(\mathcal{P})\longrightarrow\sN(\text{Ib}^{\mathcal{P}})
given on objects by sending each operation
\mu\in\mathcal{P}(\overline{c})
to
\overline{c}
. Let
M
be an infinitesimal
\mathcal{P}
-bimodule. Then
M
can be identified with a functor
M:\sN(\text{Ib}^{\mathcal{P}})\longrightarrow(\Set_{\Delta})_{\infty}
. Composed with the above map, it induces a functor
M^{\star}:\Tw(\mathcal{P})\longrightarrow(\Set_{\Delta})_{\infty}
. Now, since
\Tw(\mathcal{P})
is by definition the unstraightening of
\mathcal{P}
considered as an infinitesimal bimodule over itself, we get a canonical weak equivalence of spaces:

\lim_{\Tw(\mathcal{P})}M^{\star}\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\Map^{\h}_{\IbMod(\mathcal{P})}(\mathcal{P},M).

Construction
6.2.3
provides us with the simplicial structure of
\Tw(\mathcal{P})
. We will now delve into the
\infty
-categorical structure by describing the mapping spaces in
\Tw(\mathcal{P})
.

Again, let
\mu\in\mathcal{P}(\overline{c})
and
\nu\in\mathcal{P}(\overline{d})
be two operations of
\mathcal{P}
, regarded as objects of
\Tw(\mathcal{P})
. We take
\rho_{\mu}
to be the composite map

\Map_{\textbf{Ib}^{\mathcal{P}}}(\overline{c},\overline{d})\overset{\mathcal{P}}{\longrightarrow}\Map_{\Set_{\Delta}}(\mathcal{P}(\overline{c}),\mathcal{P}(\overline{d}))\overset{ev_{\mu}}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{P}(\overline{d})

where
ev_{\mu}
is the evaluation at
\mu
.

Proposition 6.2.7
.

There is a canonical homotopy equivalence

\{\nu\}\times^{\h}_{\mathcal{P}(\overline{d})}\Map_{\textbf{Ib}^{\mathcal{P}}}(\overline{c},\overline{d})\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\Map_{\Tw(\mathcal{P})}(\mu,\nu).

Proof.

This immediately follows from Proposition
6.1.9
.
∎

Remark 6.2.8
.

Note that there is a canonical map of
\infty
-categories
\Tw(\mathcal{P})\longrightarrow\sN(\Fin_{*}^{\op})
factoring through
\sN(\textbf{Ib}^{\mathcal{P}})
. Consider the induced map
\Map_{\Tw(\mathcal{P})}(\mu,\nu)\longrightarrow\textrm{Hom}_{\Fin_{*}^{\op}}(\langle m\rangle,\langle n\rangle)
. For each map
f:\langle n\rangle\longrightarrow\langle m\rangle
in
\Fin_{*}
, we denote by
\Map^{f}_{\Tw(\mathcal{P})}(\mu,\nu)
the component of
\Map_{\Tw(\mathcal{P})}(\mu,\nu)
over
f
, so that we can write

\Map_{\Tw(\mathcal{P})}(\mu,\nu)=\bigsqcup_{\langle n\rangle\overset{f}{\longrightarrow}\langle m\rangle}\Map^{f}_{\Tw(\mathcal{P})}(\mu,\nu).

By the above proposition, there is a homotopy equivalence

\{\nu\}\times^{\h}_{\mathcal{P}(\overline{d})}\Map^{f}_{\textbf{Ib}^{\mathcal{P}}}(\overline{c},\overline{d})\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\Map^{f}_{\Tw(\mathcal{P})}(\mu,\nu).

For further illustration, we find a class of operads whose twisted arrow
\infty
-categories admit terminal objects.

Definition 6.2.9
.

A simplicial operad is said to be
homotopy unital
if all its spaces of nullary operations are weakly contractible. Furthermore, a simplicial operad is said to be
unitally homotopy connected
if it is homotopy unital with weakly contractible spaces of 1-ary operations.

Proposition 6.2.10
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a fibrant and homotopy unital simplicial operad. Let
d
be a color of
\mathcal{P}
and
\mu_{d}\in\mathcal{P}(d)
a nullary operation of
\mathcal{P}
. Then
\mu_{d}
is a terminal object of
\Tw(\mathcal{P})
if and only if for every color
c
, the space
\mathcal{P}(c;d)
is contractible. Consequently, if
\mathcal{P}
is fibrant and unitally homotopy connected then
\Tw(\mathcal{P})
admits terminal objects being precisely the nullary operations of
\mathcal{P}
.

Proof.

By definition,
\mu_{d}
is a terminal object of
\Tw(\mathcal{P})
precisely if for every operation
\mu\in\mathcal{P}
the mapping space
\Map_{\Tw(\mathcal{P})}(\mu,\mu_{d})
is contractible. Given any operation
\mu\in\mathcal{P}(c_{1},\cdots,c_{m};c)
, it suffices to show that there is a homotopy equivalence of spaces:

\mathcal{P}(c;d)\simeq\Map_{\Tw(\mathcal{P})}(\mu,\mu_{d}).

By Proposition
6.2.7
there is a homotopy equivalence

\{\mu_{d}\}\times^{h}_{\mathcal{P}(d)}\Map_{\textbf{Ib}^{\mathcal{P}}}((c_{1},\cdots,c_{m};c),(d))\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\Map_{\Tw(\mathcal{P})}(\mu,\mu_{d}).

Since
\mathcal{P}(d)
is contractible, the homotopy pullback is equivalent to
\Map_{\textbf{Ib}^{\mathcal{P}}}((c_{1},\cdots,c_{m};c),(d))
. Furthermore, note that

\Map_{\textbf{Ib}^{\mathcal{P}}}((c_{1},\cdots,c_{m};c),(d))=\mathcal{P}(c;d)\times\mathcal{P}(c_{1})\times\cdots\times\mathcal{P}(c_{m}),

which is therefore weakly equivalent to
\mathcal{P}(c;d)
. Thus we obtain the desired homotopy equivalence.
∎

Several interesting examples would emerge upon examination of the case of the little discs operads. Let us recall the definition of these. Let
\{U_{i}\}_{i\in I}
be a finite collection of open subsets of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
and let
V\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
be another open subset. We will say that a map
\underset{i\in I}{\bigsqcup}U_{i}\longrightarrow V
is a
rectilinear embedding
if it is an open embedding and such that each component map
U_{i}\longrightarrow V
is a rectilinear embedding in the usual sense, i.e. it is given by a formula of the form
x\mapsto\lambda x+c
for
\lambda>0
. We now denote by

\sD^{n}:=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\,|\,\lVert x\rVert<1\}

the
open (unit)
n
-disc
. Then by definition, the
little
n
-discs operad

\rE_{n}
is a single-colored topological operad such that

\rE_{n}(k)=\Rect(\underset{k}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n},\sD^{n})

the space of rectilinear embeddings of
k
disjoint open
n
-discs in another open
n
-disc, endowed with the topology as a subspace of the mapping space in
\Top
. The operadic composition is given by the composition of rectilinear embeddings. One may obtain the simplicial version of
\rE_{n}
by applying the singular functor
\Sing:\Top\longrightarrow\Set_{\Delta}
.

Conventions 6.2.11
.

We use the same notation
\rE_{n}
to refer to the simplicial version of the little
n
-discs operad. On other hand, by an
\rE_{\infty}
-
operad
we will mean a fibrant and
\Sigma
-cofibrant model for the simplicial commutative operad
\Com
.

Remark 6.2.12
.

We can show that the functor
\Tw(-)
commutes with filtered homotopy colimits of simplicial operads. As a consequence, we obtain an
\infty
-categorical filtration of the nerve of
\Fin_{*}^{\op}
:

\sN(\Delta)\simeq\Tw(\E_{1})\longrightarrow\Tw(\E_{2})\longrightarrow\cdots\longrightarrow\Tw(\E_{\infty})\simeq\sN(\Fin_{*}^{\op}).

The functor
\Tw(\E_{1})\longrightarrow\Tw(\E_{\infty})
can be represented by the simplicial circle in the following sense. We will use the standard model for
\sS^{1}
which has only two nondegenerate simplices. Moreover, we consider
\sS^{1}
as a finite pointed simplicial set, so that it determines a functor
\sS^{1}:\Delta^{\op}\longrightarrow\Fin_{*}
. This latter corresponds up to weak equivalence to
\Tw(\E_{1})\longrightarrow\Tw(\E_{\infty})
. More concretely,
\sS^{1}
is given on objects by sending
[m]
to
\langle m\rangle
; and such that for each map
f:[m]\longrightarrow[n]
in
\Delta
, the map
\sS^{1}(f):\langle n\rangle\longrightarrow\langle m\rangle
is given by, for each
1\leq j\leq m
, letting

\sS^{1}(f)^{-1}(j):=\{i\,|\,f(j-1)<i\leq f(j)\}.

In the rest of this subsection, we shall give a description of the twisted arrow
\infty
-category of the operad
\E_{n}
. For this, we will need to use the following notations and conventions.

\bullet
We will use the
one point compactification
of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
as a model for the
n
-sphere. Thus we will write
\sS^{n}:=\mathbb{R}^{n}\cup\{\infty\}
, and consider it as a pointed space with base point
``\infty
”.

\bullet
We let
\sD^{n}_{r}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
denote the
n
-dimensional open disc of radius
r
(and with center at
0
). In particular,
\sD^{n}_{1}
is simply written as
\sD^{n}
, as mentioned above.

\bullet
We denote by
\mathfrak{D}^{n}_{r}:=\sS^{n}\setminus\overline{\sD^{n}_{r}}
, i.e. the complement of the closure of
\sD^{n}_{r}
in
\sS^{n}=\mathbb{R}^{n}\cup\{\infty\}
. For simplicity, we will abbreviate
\mathfrak{D}^{n}:=\mathfrak{D}^{n}_{1}
, and consider it as a pointed space with base point
``\infty
”.

\bullet
By convention, a
rectilinear embedding

\mathfrak{D}^{n}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{D}^{n}
is a pointed embedding such that the induced map
\mathfrak{D}^{n}\setminus\{\infty\}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{D}^{n}\setminus\{\infty\}
is an embedding of the form
x\mapsto rx
for some fixed
r\geq 1
. Similarly, a map
\mathfrak{D}^{n}\longrightarrow\sS^{n}
is a
rectilinear embedding
if it is a pointed embedding such that the induced map
\mathfrak{D}^{n}\setminus\{\infty\}\longrightarrow\sS^{n}\setminus\{\infty\}=\mathbb{R}^{n}
is an embedding of the form
x\mapsto rx
for some fixed
r>0
. We will use the same notation
\delta_{r}
to denote these two types of maps. In both cases,
\delta_{r}
maps
\mathfrak{D}^{n}
onto the subspace
\mathfrak{D}^{n}_{r}\subseteq\sS^{n}
.

\bullet
Moreover, a map
\sD^{n}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{D}^{n}
is a
rectilinear embedding
if it comes from a rectilinear embedding
\sD^{n}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{D}^{n}\setminus\{\infty\}
between subsets of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
in the usual sense. Similarly, a map
\sD^{n}\longrightarrow\sS^{n}
is a
rectilinear embedding
if it comes from an actual rectilinear embedding
\sD^{n}\longrightarrow\sS^{n}\setminus\{\infty\}=\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

Construction 6.2.13
.

We construct a topological category, denoted
\Disc_{*}^{+}
, as follows. An object of
\Disc_{*}^{+}
is either (1) a finite coproduct of spaces of the form
\underset{k}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n}
, with
k\geq 0
, and regarded as a pointed space with the base point being given by
\infty\in\mathfrak{D}^{n}
, or (2) the (pointed)
n
-sphere
\sS^{n}=\mathbb{R}^{n}\cup\{\infty\}
. The mapping spaces of
\Disc_{*}^{+}
are given as follows.

\bullet

\Map_{\Disc^{+}_{*}}(\underset{k}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n},\underset{l}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n})
is the space of open embeddings
\underset{k}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n}\longrightarrow\underset{l}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n}
that preserve base points and such that the restriction to each component is a rectilinear embedding.

\bullet

\Map_{\Disc^{+}_{*}}(\underset{k}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n},\sS^{n})
is defined in the same manner as described above.

\bullet

\Map_{\Disc^{+}_{*}}(\sS^{n},\sS^{n})=\{*\}
.

\bullet

\Map_{\Disc^{+}_{*}}(\sS^{n},\underset{k}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n})=\emptyset
.

The composition in
\Disc_{*}^{+}
is defined in an evident way. We will let
\Disc_{*}\subseteq\Disc_{*}^{+}
denote the full subcategory spanned by the collection of objects
\{\underset{k}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n}\}_{k\geq 0}
.

Next, we will use the same notations to denote the
\infty
-categories corresponding to
\Disc_{*}
and
\Disc_{*}^{+}
. Moreover, we denote by

\mathcal{C}:=\Disc_{*}\times_{\Disc^{+}_{*}}(\Disc^{+}_{*})_{/\sS^{n}}

the
\infty
-category formed by the pullback of
\Disc_{*}\longrightarrow\Disc^{+}_{*}
and the projection
(\Disc^{+}_{*})_{/\sS^{n}}\longrightarrow\Disc^{+}_{*}
. In particular, objects of
\mathcal{C}
are the maps in
\Disc_{*}^{+}
of the form
\underset{k}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n}\longrightarrow\sS^{n}
.

Proposition 6.2.14
.

There is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories
\psi:\mathcal{C}^{\op}\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\Tw(\rE_{n})
.

The proof will require two technical lemmas. Let us first elaborate more on the data of
\Disc_{*}^{+}
.

Remark 6.2.15
.

By construction, a map in
\Disc_{*}^{+}
of the form
\underset{k}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{D}^{n}
is the choice of a map
\delta_{r}:\mathfrak{D}^{n}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{D}^{n}
with
r\geq 1
, and together with a rectilinear embedding
(f_{1},\cdots,f_{k}):\underset{k}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\longrightarrow\sD^{n}_{r}\setminus\overline{\sD^{n}}
. In visual terms, this simply shows a picture of
k
disjoint
n
-dimensional (open) discs positioned between the boundaries of
\overline{\sD^{n}}
and
\overline{\sD^{n}_{r}}
. On other hand, the data of a map
\underset{k}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n}\longrightarrow\sS^{n}
consist of a map
\delta_{r}:\mathfrak{D}^{n}\longrightarrow\sS^{n}
with
r>0
, and along with a rectilinear embedding
(f_{1},\cdots,f_{k}):\underset{k}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\longrightarrow\sD^{n}_{r}
. Thus, such a map corresponds to a picture of
k
disjoint
n
-dimensional discs, all contained within
\sD^{n}_{r}
.

Using our notations, we will write
(f_{1},\cdots,f_{k};\delta_{r})
for a map of the first type, and write
[f_{1},\cdots,f_{k};\delta_{r}]
for a typical map
\underset{k}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n}\longrightarrow\sS^{n}
as mentioned in the above remark.

Lemma 6.2.16
.

For every
k\geq 0
, there is a canonical homotopy equivalence

\rho:\Map_{\Disc^{+}_{*}}(\underset{k}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n},\sS^{n})\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\rE_{n}(k).

Proof.

Let us denote by
\Map^{1}_{\Disc^{+}_{*}}(\underset{k}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n},\sS^{n})\subseteq\Map_{\Disc^{+}_{*}}(\underset{k}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n},\sS^{n})
the subspace consisting of those maps of the form
[f_{1},\cdots,f_{k};\delta_{1}]
. We have in fact a retraction

\displaystyle\rho^{1}:\Map_{\Disc^{+}_{*}}(\underset{k}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n},\sS^{n})\longrightarrow\Map^{1}_{\Disc^{+}_{*}}(\underset{k}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n},\sS^{n}),

\displaystyle[f_{1},\cdots,f_{k};\delta_{r}]\mapsto[\frac{1}{r}f_{1},\cdots,\frac{1}{r}f_{k};\delta_{1}].

Moreover, this exhibits the space on the right as a (strong) deformation retract of that on the left via a homotopy defined as follows:

h_{t}:[f_{1},\cdots,f_{k};\delta_{r}]\mapsto[\lambda_{t,r}f_{1},\cdots,\lambda_{t,r}f_{k};\delta_{r\lambda_{t,r}}]

where
\lambda_{t,r}:=\frac{1}{r}[t+(1-t)r]
for every
0\leq t\leq 1
. The map
\rho
is then defined to be the composed map

\Map_{\Disc^{+}_{*}}(\underset{k}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n},\sS^{n})\overset{\rho^{1}}{\underset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}}\Map^{1}_{\Disc^{+}_{*}}(\underset{k}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n},\sS^{n})\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}\rE_{n}(k)

in which the second map is the obvious homeomorphism given by
[f_{1},\cdots,f_{k};\delta_{1}]\mapsto(f_{1},\cdots,f_{k})
.
∎

We will write
\varepsilon:\sD^{n}\longrightarrow\sS^{n}
to denote the actual inclusion. Let
k_{0}\geq 1
be a given integer. The inclusion
\mathfrak{D}^{n}\subseteq\sS^{n}
allows us to identify
\Map_{\Disc^{+}_{*}}(\underset{k_{0}-1}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n},\mathfrak{D}^{n})
with the subspace

\Map^{\bullet}_{\Disc^{+}_{*}}(\underset{k_{0}}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n},\sS^{n})\subseteq\Map_{\Disc^{+}_{*}}(\underset{k_{0}}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n},\sS^{n})

(6.2.5)

containing those maps of the form
[\varepsilon,f_{1},\cdots,f_{k_{0}-1};\delta_{r}]
with
r\geq 1
. We now obtain a composed map

\Map_{\Disc^{+}_{*}}(\underset{k_{0}-1}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n},\mathfrak{D}^{n})\cong\Map^{\bullet}_{\Disc^{+}_{*}}(\underset{k_{0}}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n},\sS^{n})\longrightarrow\Map_{\Disc^{+}_{*}}(\underset{k_{0}}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n},\sS^{n})\overset{\rho}{\underset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}}\rE_{n}(k_{0}).

(6.2.6)

Unwinding the definitions, we see that this composed map is given by the rule

(f_{1},\cdots,f_{k_{0}-1};\delta_{r})\mapsto(\frac{1}{r}\varepsilon,\frac{1}{r}f_{1},\cdots,\frac{1}{r}f_{k_{0}-1}).

Lemma 6.2.17
.

There is a canonical weak equivalence of topological categories

\iota:(\Disc_{*})^{\op}\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\textbf{Ib}^{\rE_{n}}.

Proof.

Since
\rE_{n}
has a single color, the set of objects of
\textbf{Ib}^{\rE_{n}}
is identified with
\mathbb{N}
. Suppose we are given a map
\alpha:\langle k\rangle\longrightarrow\langle l\rangle
in
\Fin_{*}
. Denote by
k_{i}
the cardinality of
\alpha^{-1}(i)
for
i=0,\cdots,l
. Recall by construction that the component of
\Map_{\textbf{Ib}^{\rE_{n}}}(l,k)
over
\alpha
is given by

\Map_{\textbf{Ib}^{\rE_{n}}}^{\alpha}(l,k)=\rE_{n}(k_{0})\times\rE_{n}(k_{1})\times\cdots\times\rE_{n}(k_{l}).

We let
\iota
be defined on objects by sending
\underset{k}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n}
to
k
. Moreover, there is a canonical projection
\Disc_{*}\longrightarrow\Fin_{*}
such that the component of
\Map_{\Disc_{*}}(\underset{k}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n},\,\underset{l}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n})
over
\alpha
is given by

\Map_{\Disc_{*}}^{\alpha}(\underset{k}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n},\,\underset{l}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n})=\Map_{\Disc_{*}}(\underset{k_{0}-1}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n},\,\mathfrak{D}^{n})\times\rE_{n}(k_{1})\times\cdots\times\rE_{n}(k_{l}).

In this way, the structure map
\Map_{\Disc_{*}}^{\alpha}(\underset{k}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n},\,\underset{l}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n})\longrightarrow\Map_{\textbf{Ib}^{\rE_{n}}}^{\alpha}(l,k)
of
\iota
is the map induced by the composed map (
6.2.6
). Verifying the functoriality of
\iota
is then straightforward. We shall complete the proof after showing the latter map is a weak equivalence. For this, it suffices to prove that the inclusion (
6.2.5
) is one. We will denote by

\Map^{\odot}_{\Disc^{+}_{*}}(\underset{k_{0}}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n},\sS^{n})\subseteq\Map^{1}_{\Disc^{+}_{*}}(\underset{k_{0}}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n},\sS^{n})

the subspace comprising maps of the form
[\frac{1}{r}\varepsilon,f_{1},\cdots,f_{k_{0}-1};\delta_{1}]
with
r\geq 1
. Then observe that there is a commutative square of the form

\textstyle{\Map^{\bullet}_{\Disc^{+}_{*}}(\underset{k_{0}}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n},\sS^{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\Map_{\Disc^{+}_{*}}(\underset{k_{0}}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n},\sS^{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{1}}
\textstyle{\Map^{\odot}_{\Disc^{+}_{*}}(\underset{k_{0}}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n},\sS^{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\Map^{1}_{\Disc^{+}_{*}}(\underset{k_{0}}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n},\sS^{n})}

in which the left vertical map is defined in the same manner as
\rho^{1}
, and can be readily verified to be a homeomorphism. Moreover, the bottom inclusion is weakly equivalent to the map between configuration spaces

\Conf(\sD^{n}\setminus\{0\},k_{0}-1)\longrightarrow\Conf(\sD^{n},k_{0}),

which is in fact a weak equivalence (cf., e.g.
[
10
]
). We deduce that the top inclusion is indeed a weak equivalence.
∎

Remark 6.2.18
.

The map
\rho
and the functor
\iota
work harmoniously together in the following sense. Let
g\in\Map_{\Disc^{+}_{*}}(\underset{l}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n},\sS^{n})
be any map. Then we have a commutative square of the form

\textstyle{\Map_{\Disc_{*}}(\underset{k}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n},\,\underset{l}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\iota}
\textstyle{\Map_{\textbf{Ib}^{\rE_{n}}}(l,k)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Map_{\Disc^{+}_{*}}(\underset{k}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n},\sS^{n})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\rho}
\textstyle{\rE_{n}(k)}

in which the left and right vertical maps are respectively induced by
g:\underset{l}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n}\longrightarrow\sS^{n}
and the operation
\rho(g)\in\rE_{n}(l)
. This will support the proof below.

Proof of Proposition
6.2.14
.

We will use the same notations to denote the topological categories associated to
\mathfrak{C}[\Delta^{m}]
and
\mathfrak{D}^{\triangleleft}_{\Delta^{m}}
for
m\geq 0
. An
m
-simplex of
\mathcal{C}^{\op}
is a functor
\widetilde{\varphi}:\mathfrak{C}[\Delta^{m+1}]^{\op}\longrightarrow\Disc^{+}_{*}
such that
\widetilde{\varphi}(0)=\sS^{n}
and
\widetilde{\varphi}(j)\in\Disc_{*}
for
j=1,\cdots,m+1
. We put
\widetilde{\varphi}(j)=\underset{k_{j}}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n}
. The restriction of
\widetilde{\varphi}
to
\mathfrak{C}[\Delta^{\{1,\cdots,m+1\}}]^{\op}
determines a functor
\mathfrak{C}[\Delta^{m}]\longrightarrow(\Disc_{*})^{\op}
taking
j
to
\underset{k_{j+1}}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n}
for
j=0,\cdots,m
. Composed with
\iota:(\Disc_{*})^{\op}\longrightarrow\textbf{Ib}^{\rE_{n}}
(see Lemma
6.2.17
), it yields a functor

\varphi:\mathfrak{C}[\Delta^{m}]\longrightarrow\textbf{Ib}^{\rE_{n}}.

We now construct a natural transformation
t:\mathfrak{D}^{\triangleleft}_{\Delta^{m}}\longrightarrow\rE_{n}\circ\,\varphi
between functors
\mathfrak{C}[\Delta^{m}]\longrightarrow\Top
. For each
j\in\{0,\cdots,m\}
, we need to define a natural map
t(j):\mathfrak{D}^{\triangleleft}_{\Delta^{m}}(j)\longrightarrow\rE_{n}(k_{j+1})
. This is given by the composed map

\mathfrak{D}^{\triangleleft}_{\Delta^{m}}(j)\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}\Map_{\mathfrak{C}[\Delta^{m+1}]^{\op}}(j+1,0)\longrightarrow\Map_{\Disc^{+}_{*}}(\underset{k_{j+1}}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n},\sS^{n})\overset{\rho}{\longrightarrow}\rE_{n}(k_{j+1})

where the second map is the structure map of
\widetilde{\varphi}
. It can be verified that the assignment
\widetilde{\varphi}\mapsto(\varphi,t)
determines a functor
\psi:\mathcal{C}^{\op}\longrightarrow\Tw(\rE_{n})
. In particular,
\psi
is given on objects by sending a map
f:\underset{k}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n}\longrightarrow\sS^{n}
to an operation
\rho(f)\in\rE_{n}(k)
as in Lemma
6.2.16
.

We shall now prove that
\psi
is an equivalence. Since
\psi
is surjective on objects, it remains to show that the induced maps between mapping spaces are all weak equivalences. Let
f:\underset{k}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n}\longrightarrow\sS^{n}
and
g:\underset{l}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n}\longrightarrow\sS^{n}
be any two objects of
\mathcal{C}
. The map
\Map_{\mathcal{C}}(f,g)\longrightarrow\Map_{\Tw(\rE_{n})}(\rho(g),\rho(f))
is weakly equivalent to the map between homotopy fibers

\{f\}\times^{\h}_{\Map_{\Disc^{+}_{*}}(\underset{k}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n},\sS^{n})}\Map_{\Disc_{*}}(\underset{k}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n},\,\underset{l}{\bigsqcup}\sD^{n}\sqcup\,\mathfrak{D}^{n})\longrightarrow\{\rho(f)\}\times^{\h}_{\rE_{n}(k)}\Map_{\textbf{Ib}^{\rE_{n}}}(l,k),

which is a weak equivalence due to Lemmas
6.2.16
and
6.2.17
. (See also Remark
6.2.18
, Proposition
6.2.7
and [
[
35
]
, Proposition 5.5.5.12]).
∎

6.3
Cotangent complex of simplicial operads

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a
C
-colored simplicial operad which is fibrant and
\Sigma
-cofibrant. We will show that the cotangent complex
\rL_{\mathcal{P}}\in\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op(\Set_{\Delta})
can be represented as a spectrum valued functor on
\Tw(\mathcal{P})
.

As introduced previously,
\Tw(\mathcal{P})
is the image of
\mathcal{P}
through the unstraightening functor

\Un_{\textbf{Ib}^{\mathcal{P}}}:\IbMod(\mathcal{P})\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}(\Set_{\Delta}^{\cov})_{/\sN(\textbf{Ib}^{\mathcal{P}})}.

We will abbreviate
\Un_{\textbf{Ib}^{\mathcal{P}}}
by
\mathcal{U}n
. Recall that there is a canonical left fibration
\Tw(\mathcal{P})\longrightarrow\sN(\textbf{Ib}^{\mathcal{P}})
. Observe now that
\mathcal{U}n
induces a right Quillen equivalence

\mathcal{U}n_{\mathcal{P}//\mathcal{P}}:\IbMod(\mathcal{P})_{\mathcal{P}//\mathcal{P}}\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}(\Set_{\Delta}^{\cov})_{\Tw(\mathcal{P})//\Tw(\mathcal{P})}

where
(\Set_{\Delta}^{\cov})_{\Tw(\mathcal{P})//\Tw(\mathcal{P})}
denotes the pointed model category associated to
(\Set_{\Delta}^{\cov})_{/\Tw(\mathcal{P})}
. So we have a right Quillen equivalence of stabilizations

\mathcal{U}n^{\Sp}_{\mathcal{P}//\mathcal{P}}:\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\IbMod(\mathcal{P})\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\Sp((\Set_{\Delta}^{\cov})_{\Tw(\mathcal{P})//\Tw(\mathcal{P})}).

Moreover, the straightening functor
\St_{\Tw(\mathcal{P})}:(\Set_{\Delta}^{\cov})_{/\Tw(\mathcal{P})}\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\Fun(\mathfrak{C}[\Tw(\mathcal{P})],\Set_{\Delta})
induces a left Quillen equivalence

\St^{\Sp}_{\Tw(\mathcal{P})}:\Sp((\Set_{\Delta}^{\cov})_{\Tw(\mathcal{P})//\Tw(\mathcal{P})})\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\Fun(\mathfrak{C}[\Tw(\mathcal{P})],\Spectra)

where
\Spectra
refers to the stable model category of spectra. We thus obtain a sequence of right or left Quillen equivalences

\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op(\Set_{\Delta})\overset{\mathcal{U}^{ib}_{\mathcal{P}}}{\underset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\IbMod(\mathcal{P})\underset{\simeq}{\overset{\mathcal{U}n^{\Sp}_{\mathcal{P}//\mathcal{P}}}{\xrightarrow{\hskip 28.45274pt}}}\Sp((\Set_{\Delta}^{\cov})_{\Tw(\mathcal{P})//\Tw(\mathcal{P})})\underset{\simeq}{\overset{\St^{\Sp}_{\Tw(\mathcal{P})}}{\xrightarrow{\hskip 28.45274pt}}}\Fun(\mathfrak{C}[\Tw(\mathcal{P})],\Spectra).

Let us describe the derived image of
\rL_{\mathcal{P}}\in\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op(\Set_{\Delta})
through this composed functor. We have seen that
\mathbb{R}\mathcal{U}^{ib}_{\mathcal{P}}(\rL_{\mathcal{P}})[1]\simeq\widetilde{\rL}_{\mathcal{P}}
(cf. Theorem
5.2.15
). More explicitly,
\widetilde{\rL}_{\mathcal{P}}\in\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\IbMod(\mathcal{P})
is the prespectrum with
(\widetilde{\rL}_{\mathcal{P}})_{k,k}=\mathcal{P}\circ\sS_{C}^{k}
where
\sS^{k}_{C}
is the
C
-collection given by
\sS_{C}^{k}(c;c)=\sS^{k}
for every color
c
and taking the value
\emptyset
on the other levels. Furthermore,
\mathbb{R}\mathcal{U}n^{\Sp}_{\mathcal{P}//\mathcal{P}}
sends
\widetilde{\rL}_{\mathcal{P}}
to the prespectrum

\mathcal{U}n(\mathcal{P}\circ\sS_{C}^{\bullet})\in\Sp((\Set_{\Delta}^{\cov})_{\Tw(\mathcal{P})//\Tw(\mathcal{P})})

with
\mathcal{U}n(\mathcal{P}\circ\sS_{C}^{\bullet})_{k,k}=\mathcal{U}n(\mathcal{P}\circ\sS_{C}^{k})
. We now denote by

\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}}:=\mathbb{L}\St^{\Sp}_{\Tw(\mathcal{P})}(\mathcal{U}n(\mathcal{P}\circ\sS_{C}^{\bullet}))\in\Fun(\mathfrak{C}[\Tw(\mathcal{P})],\Spectra).

So
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}}
is the derived image of
\rL_{\mathcal{P}}[1]
under the composed functor

\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op(\Set_{\Delta})\longrightarrow\Fun(\mathfrak{C}[\Tw(\mathcal{P})],\Spectra).

To get a description of the functor
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}}
, observe first that there is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\Fun(\mathfrak{C}[\Tw(\mathcal{P})],\Spectra)_{\infty}\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\Fun(\Tw(\mathcal{P}),\Sp)

where
\Sp
is the
\infty
-category of spectra
. Thus we will regard
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}}
as an
\infty
-functor
\Tw(\mathcal{P})\longrightarrow\Sp
. Let
\mu\in\mathcal{P}(c_{1},\cdots,c_{m};c)
be an operation of
\mathcal{P}
, regarded as an object of
\Tw(\mathcal{P})
. By construction,
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}}(\mu)\in\Sp
is the prespectrum with
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}}(\mu)_{k,k}
being given by the value at
\mu
of the functor

\St_{\Tw(\mathcal{P})}(\mathcal{U}n(\mathcal{P}\circ\sS_{C}^{k})):\mathfrak{C}[\Tw(\mathcal{P})]\longrightarrow\Set_{\Delta}.

By using [
[
35
]
, Proposition 2.2.3.15] (with the opposite convention), we obtain that

\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}}(\mu)^{\op}_{k,k}\simeq\mathcal{U}n(\mathcal{P}\circ\sS_{C}^{k})\times_{\mathcal{U}n(\mathcal{P})}\{\mu\}.

It follows by construction that
(\mathcal{P}\circ\sS_{C}^{k})(c_{1},\cdots,c_{m};c)=\mathcal{P}(c_{1},\cdots,c_{m};c)\times(\sS^{k})^{\times m}
.
Using Remark
6.1.7
, we can then show that
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}}(\mu)_{k,k}\simeq(\sS^{k})^{\times m}
and hence,
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}}(\mu)\simeq\mathbb{S}^{\oplus m}
the
m
-fold coproduct of the
sphere spectrum
.

Remark 6.3.1
.

We consider the operad
\rE_{\infty}
and describe the functor
\mathcal{F}_{\E_{\infty}}
. Since
\Tw(\E_{\infty})\simeq\sN(\Fin_{*}^{\op})
, we may regard
\mathcal{F}_{\E_{\infty}}
as a functor
\Fin_{*}^{\op}\longrightarrow\Spectra
given on objects by sending
\langle m\rangle
to
\mathbb{S}^{\oplus m}
. Let
f:\langle n\rangle\longrightarrow\langle m\rangle
be map in
\Fin_{*}
. Unwinding the definitions, the structure map

\mathcal{F}_{\E_{\infty}}(f):\mathbb{S}^{\oplus\{1,\cdots,m\}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{S}^{\oplus\{1,\cdots,n\}}

is given by, for each
i\in\{1,\cdots,m\}
, copying the
i
’th summand to the summands of position
j\in f^{-1}(i)
when this fiber is nonempty or collapsing that summand to the zero spectrum otherwise.

Here is another significant remark.

Remark 6.3.2
.

Without loss of generality, we can assume that
\mathcal{P}
comes equipped with a map
\varPhi:\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow\E_{\infty}
. We then claim that the (derived) functor
\varPhi^{*}:\mathcal{T}_{\E_{\infty}}\Op(\Set_{\Delta})\longrightarrow\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op(\Set_{\Delta})
sends
\rL_{\E_{\infty}}
to
\rL_{\mathcal{P}}
. Indeed, by using Theorem
5.2.15
, we just need to verify that the induced right adjoint
\varPhi^{*}:\mathcal{T}_{\E_{\infty}}\IbMod(\E_{\infty})\longrightarrow\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\IbMod(\mathcal{P})
sends the prespectrum
\widetilde{\rL}_{\E_{\infty}}
to
\widetilde{\rL}_{\mathcal{P}}
(see also [
[
24
]
, Corollary 2.4.8]). For this, we need to show that the functor

\varPhi^{*}:\IbMod(\E_{\infty})_{\E_{\infty}//\E_{\infty}}\longrightarrow\IbMod(\mathcal{P})_{\mathcal{P}//\mathcal{P}}

sends
\E_{\infty}\circ\sS_{*}^{k}
to
\mathcal{P}\circ\sS_{C}^{k}
for every
k\geq 0
. (Here we write
\sS_{*}^{k}
, reflecting that the operad
\E_{\infty}
has a single color). This follows from the isomorphisms of the form

\mathcal{P}(c_{1},\cdots,c_{m};c)\times(\sS^{k})^{\times m}\;\cong\;\mathcal{P}(c_{1},\cdots,c_{m};c)\times_{\E_{\infty}(m)}(\E_{\infty}(m)\times(\sS^{k})^{\times m}).

Accordingly, we deduce that the functor
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}}
can be represented as the composed functor

\Tw(\mathcal{P})\longrightarrow\sN(\Fin_{*}^{\op})\overset{\mathcal{F}_{\E_{\infty}}}{\longrightarrow}\Sp.

(6.3.1)

We summarize the above steps in the following statement.

Theorem 6.3.3
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a fibrant and
\Sigma
-cofibrant simplicial operad. There is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\mathcal{T}_{\mathcal{P}}\Op(\Set_{\Delta})_{\infty}\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\Fun(\Tw(\mathcal{P}),\Sp).

Moreover, under this equivalence, the cotangent complex
\rL_{\mathcal{P}}
is identified with
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}}[-1]
the desuspension of the functor
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}}:\Tw(\mathcal{P})\longrightarrow\Sp
(
6.3.1
), which is given on objects by sending each operation
\mu\in\mathcal{P}
of arity
m
to
\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}}(\mu)=\mathbb{S}^{\oplus m}
.

Corollary 6.3.4
.

For a given functor
\mathcal{F}:\Tw(\mathcal{P})\longrightarrow\Sp
, the
n
’th Quillen cohomology group of
\mathcal{P}
with coefficients in
\mathcal{F}
is computed by the formula

\sH^{n}_{Q}(\mathcal{P};\mathcal{F})=\pi_{0}\Map_{\Fun(\Tw(\mathcal{P}),\Sp)}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{P}},\mathcal{F}[n+1]).

We are particularly concerned about the cotangent complex of the little
n
-discs operad
\rE_{n}
. Let
\mu_{0}\in\E_{n}(0)
denote the unique nullary operation of
\E_{n}
and let
\eta:\{*\}\longrightarrow\Tw(\E_{n})
be the functor classified by
\mu_{0}
. Moreover, we write

\eta_{!}:\Fun(\{*\},\Sp)\longrightarrow\Fun(\Tw(\E_{n}),\Sp)

to denote the left adjoint functor induced by
\eta
. Let
\textbf{S}:=(\Set_{\Delta})_{\infty}
denote the
\infty
-category of spaces. We also write
\eta_{!}:\Fun(\{*\},\textbf{S})\longrightarrow\Fun(\Tw(\E_{n}),\textbf{S})
for the functor induced by
\eta
.

We wish to describe the functor
\eta_{!}(\mathbb{S}):\Tw(\E_{n})\longrightarrow\Sp
where we regard
\mathbb{S}
as a functor
\{*\}\longrightarrow\Sp
classified by the sphere spectrum
\mathbb{S}\in\Sp
. Observe that
\eta_{!}(\mathbb{S})
is equivalent to the image of
\{*\}\overset{\Delta^{0}}{\longrightarrow}\textbf{S}
through the composed functor

\Fun(\{*\},\textbf{S})\overset{\eta_{!}}{\longrightarrow}\Fun(\Tw(\E_{n}),\textbf{S})\longrightarrow\Fun(\Tw(\E_{n}),\Sp)

in which the second functor is given by the post-composition with
\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}:\textbf{S}\longrightarrow\Sp
. We first describe the functor
\eta_{!}(\Delta^{0}):\Tw(\E_{n})\longrightarrow\textbf{S}
. Let
\mu
be an operation of
\E_{n}
regarded as an object of
\Tw(\E_{n})
. Observe that
\eta_{!}(\Delta^{0})
sends
\mu
to
\Map_{\Tw(\E_{n})}(\mu_{0},\mu)\in\textbf{S}
. Suppose further that
\mu\in\E_{n}(k)
. Using Proposition
6.2.7
, we may show that
\Map_{\Tw(\E_{n})}(\mu_{0},\mu)
is equivalent to the fiber over
\mu
of the map

(-)\circ_{1}\mu_{0}:\E_{n}(k+1)\longrightarrow\E_{n}(k).

A classical result asserts that this fiber is equivalent to the
k
-fold wedge sum of the
(n-1)
-sphere (cf., e.g.
[
10
]
). So we get that

\Map_{\Tw(\E_{n})}(\mu_{0},\mu)\simeq\underset{k}{\bigvee}\sS^{n-1}.

Moreover, we have
\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}(\underset{k}{\bigvee}\sS^{n-1})\simeq\left(\underset{k}{\bigoplus}\,\mathbb{S}[n-1]\right)\oplus\mathbb{S}
. We thus obtain that
\eta_{!}(\mathbb{S}):\Tw(\E_{n})\longrightarrow\Sp
sends each operation of arity
k
to
\left(\underset{k}{\bigoplus}\,\mathbb{S}[n-1]\right)\oplus\mathbb{S}
.

Next, we write
\id\in\E_{n}(1)
for the identity operation, and write
\gamma:\{*\}\longrightarrow\Tw(\E_{n})
for the functor classified by
\id
. Moreover, we let

\gamma_{*}:\Fun(\{*\},\Sp)\longrightarrow\Fun(\Tw(\E_{n}),\Sp)

denote the right adjoint to the restriction functor along the functor
\gamma
. We also regard the functor
\gamma_{*}(\mathbb{S}):\Tw(\E_{n})\longrightarrow\Sp
. Unwinding the definitions, this is given on objects by sending each operation
\mu\in\E_{n}(k)
to

\gamma_{*}(\mathbb{S})(\mu)\,\simeq\,[\Map_{\Tw(\E_{n})}(\mu,\id),\mathbb{S}]\,\simeq\,[\E_{n}(2)\sqcup\;\underline{k}\,,\mathbb{S}]\,\simeq\,[\sS^{n-1}\sqcup\;\underline{k}\,,\mathbb{S}]\,\simeq\,\left(\mathbb{S}\oplus\mathbb{S}[-n+1]\right)\oplus\mathbb{S}^{\oplus k}

where
[-,-]
signifies the powering of simplicial sets over spectra and
\underline{k}
represents a discrete space of cardinality
k
. (For the computation of
\Map_{\Tw(\E_{n})}(\mu,\id)
, we make use of Proposition
6.2.7
again).

Remark 6.3.5
.

We may regard
\mathcal{F}_{\E_{n}}
as a sub-functor of
\gamma_{*}(\mathbb{S})
via the embedding
\mathcal{F}_{\E_{n}}\longrightarrow\gamma_{*}(\mathbb{S})
classified by the identity
\mathbb{S}=\mathcal{F}_{\E_{n}}(\id)\longrightarrow\mathbb{S}
. More precisely, there is a canonical cofiber sequence in
\Fun(\Tw(\E_{n}),\Sp)
of the form

\mathcal{F}_{\E_{n}}\longrightarrow\gamma_{*}(\mathbb{S})\longrightarrow\overline{\mathbb{S}}\oplus\overline{\mathbb{S}}[-n+1]

(6.3.2)

where
\overline{\mathbb{S}}:\Tw(\E_{n})\longrightarrow\Sp
refers to the constant functor on
\mathbb{S}\in\Sp
, and the second map is given at each operation
\mu\in\E_{n}
by the projection onto the component
[\sS^{n-1},\mathbb{S}]\simeq\mathbb{S}\oplus\mathbb{S}[-n+1]
of
\gamma_{*}(\mathbb{S})(\mu)
. Additionally, we have another cofiber sequence in
\Fun(\Tw(\E_{n}),\Sp)
of the form

\eta_{!}(\mathbb{S}[-n+1])\longrightarrow\gamma_{*}(\mathbb{S})\longrightarrow\overline{\mathbb{S}}

(6.3.3)

where the first map is the embedding classified by the inclusion

\mathbb{S}[-n+1]\longrightarrow\mathbb{S}\oplus\mathbb{S}[-n+1]\simeq\gamma_{*}(\mathbb{S})(\mu_{0})

(here we note that
\eta_{!}(\mathbb{S}[-n+1])
sends each operation of arity
k
to
\mathbb{S}[-n+1]\oplus\mathbb{S}^{\oplus k}
), and while the second map is given at each object by the projection onto the summand
``\mathbb{S}
” contained in
[\sS^{n-1},\mathbb{S}]
.

Theorem 6.3.6
.

There is a canonical cofiber sequence in
\Fun(\Tw(\E_{n}),\Sp)
of the form

\eta_{!}(\mathbb{S})\longrightarrow\overline{\mathbb{S}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{\E_{n}}[n]

(6.3.4)

where the first map is classified by the identity on
\mathbb{S}
.

Proof.

The cofiber sequence (
6.3.3
) gives rise to a coCartesian square in
\Fun(\Tw(\E_{n}),\Sp)
of the form

\textstyle{\eta_{!}(\mathbb{S}[-n+1])\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\gamma_{*}(\mathbb{S})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathbb{S}}[-n+1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\overline{\mathbb{S}}\oplus\overline{\mathbb{S}}[-n+1]}

in which the left vertical map is induced by the identity on
\mathbb{S}[-n+1]
. We can then deduce by combining this with the cofiber sequence (
6.3.2
).
∎

Corollary 6.3.7
.

Let
\mathcal{F}:\Tw(\E_{n})\longrightarrow\Sp
be a given functor. There is a long exact sequence of abelian groups of the form

\cdots\longrightarrow\sH^{-k-n-1}_{Q}(\E_{n};\mathcal{F})\longrightarrow\pi_{k}\lim\mathcal{F}\longrightarrow\pi_{k}\mathcal{F}(\mu_{0})\longrightarrow\sH^{-k-n}_{Q}(\E_{n};\mathcal{F})\longrightarrow\pi_{k-1}\lim\mathcal{F}\longrightarrow\cdots.

In particular, when
\mathcal{F}(\mu_{0})=0
then for every
k\in\mathbb{Z}
, we have a canonical isomorphism

\sH^{-k-n}_{Q}(\E_{n};\mathcal{F})\overset{\simeq}{\longrightarrow}\pi_{k-1}\lim\mathcal{F}.

These are explored more extensively in
[
28
]
, where we further investigate the cotangent and
Hochschild complexes
of algebras over an operad. Notably, the Quillen principle for
\E_{n}
-spaces is established there.

For more illustration, we shall now relate Quillen cohomology of the operad
\E_{\infty}
to the
stable cohomotopy of right
\Gamma
-modules
(cf.
[
43
]
). Let
k
be a commutative ring. By definition, a
right
\Gamma
-module
is a functor
\Fin_{*}^{\op}\longrightarrow\Mod_{\textbf{k}}
. One particularly regards the right
\Gamma
-module
t:\Fin_{*}^{\op}\longrightarrow\Mod_{\textbf{k}}
given by taking
\langle m\rangle
to
t\langle m\rangle=[\langle m\rangle,\textbf{k}]
the
k
-module of based maps from
\langle m\rangle
to
k
(where
k
has base point
0_{\textbf{k}}
).

Remark 6.3.8
.

We can show that
t\langle m\rangle\cong\textbf{k}^{\oplus\,m}
. Moreover, if
f:\langle m\rangle\longrightarrow\langle n\rangle
is a map in
\Fin_{*}
then the structure map
t(f):\textbf{k}^{\oplus\,n}\longrightarrow\textbf{k}^{\oplus\,m}
is given by, for each
i\in\{1,\cdots,n\}
, copying the
i
’th summand to the summands of position
j\in f^{-1}(i)
when this fiber is nonempty or collapsing that summand to the zero module otherwise.

We now fix
T:\Fin_{*}^{\op}\longrightarrow\Mod_{\textbf{k}}
to be a right
\Gamma
-module. According to
[
43
]
, the
k
’th stable cohomotopy group
of
T
is computed by the formula

\pi^{k}T\cong\Ext_{\Gamma}^{k}(t,T)\cong\pi_{0}\Map^{\h}_{\Fun(\Fin_{*}^{\op},\mathcal{C}(\textbf{k}))}(t,T[k])

where
\mathcal{C}(\textbf{k})
denotes the category of dg
k
-modules. Denote by
\overline{T}
the composed functor

\Fin_{*}^{\op}\overset{T}{\longrightarrow}\Mod_{\textbf{k}}\longrightarrow\Sp(\sMod_{\textbf{k}})

in which the second functor is defined by taking
M\in\Mod_{\textbf{k}}
to the suspension spectrum on
M
. Furthermore, consider the adjunction

\Sp(\mathbb{F}):\Spectra\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\Sp(\sMod_{\textbf{k}}):\Sp(\mathbb{U})

induced by the free-forgetful adjunction
\mathbb{F}:(\Set_{\Delta})_{*}\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\sMod_{\textbf{k}}:\mathbb{U}
between pointed simplicial sets and simplicial
k
-modules where a simplicial
k
-module is considered as a pointed simplicial set with base point
0
. We denote by
\widetilde{T}
the composed functor

\Fin_{*}^{\op}\overset{\overline{T}}{\longrightarrow}\Sp(\sMod_{\textbf{k}})\overset{\Sp(\mathbb{U})}{\xrightarrow{\hskip 22.76228pt}}\Spectra.

Proposition 6.3.9
.

The
(k-1)
’th Quillen cohomology group of the operad
\E_{\infty}
with coefficients in
\widetilde{T}
is isomorphic to the
k
’th stable cohomotopy group of
T
:

\sH^{k-1}_{Q}(\E_{\infty};\widetilde{T})\cong\pi^{k}T.

Proof.

By Corollary
6.3.4
, we just need to prove the existence of a weak equivalence

\Map^{\h}_{\Fun(\Fin_{*}^{\op},\,\Spectra)}(\mathcal{F}_{\E_{\infty}},\widetilde{T}[k])\simeq\Map^{\h}_{\Fun(\Fin_{*}^{\op},\mathcal{C}(\textbf{k}))}(t,T[k]).

Note first that the right hand side can be replaced equivalently by
\Map^{\h}_{\Fun(\Fin_{*}^{\op},\mathcal{C}_{\geqslant 0}(\textbf{k}))}(t,T[k])
where
\mathcal{C}_{\geqslant 0}(\textbf{k})
denotes the category of connective dg
k
-modules. On the other hand, by the Quillen adjunction
\Sp(\mathbb{F})\dashv\Sp(\mathbb{U})
, we have a weak equivalence

\Map^{\h}_{\Fun(\Fin_{*}^{\op},\,\Spectra)}(\mathcal{F}_{\E_{\infty}},\widetilde{T}[k])\simeq\Map^{\h}_{\Fun(\Fin_{*}^{\op},\,\Sp(\sMod_{\textbf{k}}))}(\Sp(\mathbb{F})\circ\mathcal{F}_{\E_{\infty}},\overline{T}[k]).

Here we note that
\Sp(\mathbb{F})\circ\mathcal{F}_{\E_{\infty}}
has already the right type, by Observation
2.5.8
. It therefore suffices to prove the existence of a weak equivalence of the form

\Map^{\h}_{\Fun(\Fin_{*}^{\op},\mathcal{C}_{\geqslant 0}(\textbf{k}))}(t,T[k])\simeq\Map^{\h}_{\Fun(\Fin_{*}^{\op},\,\Sp(\sMod_{\textbf{k}}))}(\Sp(\mathbb{F})\circ\mathcal{F}_{\E_{\infty}},\overline{T}[k]).

(6.3.5)

Consider the composed adjunction

\mathcal{C}_{\geqslant 0}(\textbf{k})\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\Sp(\mathcal{C}_{\geqslant 0}(\textbf{k}))\mathrel{\vbox{\halign{\hfil$#$\hfil\cr\scriptstyle\cr\kern 0.43057pt\cr\relbar\joinrel\rightharpoonup\cr\kern-1.29167pt\cr\begin{picture}\roundcap\polyline(0,0)(1,0)
\polyline(0.5,0)(0.5,1)
\end{picture}\cr\leftharpoondown\joinrel\relbar\cr\scriptstyle\cr}}}\Sp(\sMod_{\textbf{k}})

in which the second adjunction is induced by the Dold-Kan correspondence. Observe now that the functor
\Sp(\mathbb{F})\circ\mathcal{F}_{\E_{\infty}}
is weakly equivalent to the composed functor

\Fin_{*}^{\op}\overset{t}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{C}_{\geqslant 0}(\textbf{k})\longrightarrow\Sp(\sMod_{\textbf{k}}).

On the other hand, it is not hard to show that the derived image of
\overline{T}[k]
in
\Fun(\Fin_{*}^{\op},\mathcal{C}_{\geqslant 0}(\textbf{k}))
is weakly equivalent to
T[k]
. Thus, by adjunction, we deduce the existence of the weak equivalence (
6.3.5
).
∎
